     `docs/2026-10-03-session-messages-design.md` §2.2) for this cooling case alone, **as the steward reads the owner's decision**: D137's
     LIMITSWITCH1 note reaches *words to an ended record whose own account is cooling*, and the steward reads *an ended record* as including a
     closed quest's session. The evidence is the steward's (O2-7): the session the owner reported on 2026-10-25 was a finished build session
     whose quest it had itself closed as done (a build session closes its quest with `quest_respond`), so its quest was no longer open when
     its words were held for its own account's weekly reset, and LIMITSWITCH1, which reaches an open or taken quest's record alone, did not
     reach it; its FIX-LOG entry, which claimed the case, is corrected by a dated line. LIMITSWITCH2's note reads the decision so too (*which
     covers any ended record whose own account is cooling*), and D137 carries the amendment of point 4 as a dated note of round 3. The owner may
     overrule the reading (§8).
   The person's *Go on in a new session* still outranks it. **Ask Daoris** waits for its own account, as D137 §2.2 and D205's *one exception*
   have it (`HandOver.cs:52-57`; `CurveMove.cs:70-71`): at a limit it moves among its agent's accounts, and to another agent only with none of
   its agent's left (`LimitMove.cs:72`, `:85-88`; D205 §3.5(e)), unchanged, and going on elsewhere outside a limit is §8's first question.
   **A pick**: the record keeps none, so LIMITSWITCH2 walks every conversation's agent's accounts, a picked one's too (its note); row a-ii
   records the person's account pick on the start's binding, and from then a recorded account pick waits for its own account (D205 §3.5(d)).
3. **The own account cannot carry for another reason** (limited, refused, signed out, gone, the asked model not offered, and for a driven
   record alone outside the set or kept for conversations, `ResumeAccount.cs:31-36`; `ResumeAccount.Judge`): a limited record goes where
   D206 point 3 sends it; a driven record walks at once, as D137 point 7 has it today; a conversation's refused, signed-out or gone account
   stays as today, `CannotGoOn` to the person since *nothing carries a chat's words on by itself* (`ChatGoOn.cs:566-569`, `:581`), and going on
   by itself there is §8's second question; a model not offered starts its role afresh (`ChatGoOn.cs:518-523`), unchanged.
4. **The accounts a line may take**, by its constraint: the asked choice's other accounts of its agent, then the role's later choices where a
   limit ended it or every account of its agent cools (D205 point 5, D206 point 6; the driven half is LIMITRESUME1e's); **an account the
   person picked and the record keeps** only that account (D205 §3.5(d), D206 point 5), the person's named account on a hand-over press
   included, recorded on the start's binding in the desktop store (§13.3; O-9); **an agent pick** (`choice: pick` with no account kept), **a handed agent** and a role
   *as before* only that agent's accounts (D206 point 6). A named account on the other doors keeps today's reading: a conversation's
   hand-over, the agent's ask and the curve's included, opens its named account as a pick (`ChatHandOver.cs:156-157`; `CurveMove.cs:114-115`;
   *a pick is the one account, never moved*, `HarnessRoster.cs:887`), and a handed quest's named account comes first, then its agent's walk
   (`HarnessRoster.cs:923-934`); only the person's press is recorded as an account pick. A record from before the account pick is kept is an
   agent pick once row a-ii lands: Daoris invents no historical pin (X).
5. **None ready: it waits** until the earliest reset of exactly the accounts step 4 allows **and of the record's own account wherever step 1
   may resume there** (O2-4), one computation (`EarliestReset`), where an account signed out, refused, not offered or outside the set adds no
   reset (X): LIMITSWITCH1 as built keeps the own account's reset as the wait where the walk finds none sooner (`HarnessRoster.cs:1026-1034`;
   `:1035-1048` at `e8f1aed4f`), and resumes there by itself, as D206 has it. Said in D206 §3.7's words on its record and its rail, woken by the look (D206 point 4).
   **The one computation is Part B's** (row a-iii, with §7 row 3; O4-3): it reads step 4's set, which a-ii's recorded pick, a-iii's
   `ElsewhereAsync` and LIMITRESUME1e define. Today three sets are read: the walk's states (`Unready`, `HarnessRoster.cs:1766`); the switch's walk
   against the own reset (`:1032-1048`); and, for a limited conversation's wake, every cooling account of the agent, the walk's or not
   (`LimitWaits.cs:157`, `:220`, through `CoolingsOf`, `HarnessRoster.cs:251-256`). Until a-iii, row a-i reads each caller's set as today, behind
   one function that names which set it reads.
   **Where none of them has a reset**, the own account and every other signed out, refused or not offered, it is `Hold`, naming the repair (a
   sign-in, an account added, the model offered), as `HarnessRoster.Unready` keeps a non-cooling refusal today (`HarnessRoster.cs:1736-1740`;
   `:1762-1779` at `e8f1aed4f`), never a manufactured reset or a promised resumption (X-3.3).
6. **A new part takes one settled selection** (§2.4). **An automatic new part** (a limit's move, a carry-on, the cooling go-on) never takes the
   own account where its conversation could resume there (D206's rejection of a briefed carry-on on the same account). **The person's own press
   for a fresh part** (*Go on with Daoris's brief*, *Go on in a new session*) is the one exception, since the person chose a new part over the
   resume: it takes the walk with the own account first where ready (X2-3.4), where today the briefed move picks the cooling account
   (`BriefedMove.cs:133`, the door that fails exactly when it is needed, survey §2.4 row 13). A part whose own conversation is proven lost has no
   resume to keep, so its walk takes the own account where ready, as the ended move's lost path does today (`LimitMove.cs:238-239`).

It replaces (survey §2.4): row 1's flag at its three places and LIMITSWITCH1's `switches` parameter; row 2's seven answers, which become three
(own for a resume, the settled selection for a new part, a named account first); row 5's pick, by step 4; row 9's maker reading, asked by the
roster for every own account a limit cooled, the wake's included; row 11's two earliest resets; row 13's door.

### 3.3 The move record

Every `Move` keeps one durable **move record**, written before the old part ends where one runs and at `old-ended` for a move from an ended
record, which has no part to end (`LimitMove.cs:191-196`; O-20), and the record owns the move's delivery and recovery until the new part's
first turn answers. It is SWITCHCTX1's carry record (its §2.3) made the record of every move, and what LimitBox already is for one.

- **Where** (round 3). A row of the desktop store's `moves` table, its words in transfer in `move_words` (§13.3), owned
  by the Course subdomain (§14); **one standing move per record left**, by a partial unique index, `moves_standing`, on the record left over
  the rows whose phase is not `delivered`, `fell-back` or `closed`, so a line moves again once its move has ended, and an ended row stays as
  the move's evidence (round 6, *O5-F1*: round 5 keyed the row by the record left for its whole life, so after a move closed superseded, or a
  hold the person resolved, the next move of that record found the row and read `Never` for good); deleted with the session's record by a
  delete or a clear, as the session's files are today (`SessionHomeFiles`), and a move whose record left the ledger no longer holds closed
  `gone` at the take-up. If SWITCHCTX1a lands first, its `<s>.carry.json` is a family row f-i imports; there are never two records of one move.
- **What.** Its executor (`conversation` or `driven`) and its **claim**: the process run that holds it and its generation (§13.3, §13.4); its phase, each with its
  moment: `decided` → `quiescing` (waiting for its natural boundary, the person's cut against an agent's ask kept apart) → `old-ended` →
  `opening` (its `opens` key written) → `opened` (the new record's id and the words its open moved) → [`copied` → `verified`, the carry's] →
  `delivering` → `delivered`, or `fell-back` (its code), or `held` (its reason) → `closed`, or `closed` (its reason: `superseded`, the old part
  having gone on before the open; `gone`, its record left or its destination deleted; `resolved`, a hold ended by the one rule below) (round 6,
  *O5-F1*, *C5-1.5*). **A `held` move closes `resolved` by one rule** (round 7, *O6-F7*: round 6 said in §3.1 that only the person's press ends a
  hold and in 19.3 that it also ends by itself, and named no moment): at the person's press through one of its doors, or, read by its executor
  at each come-up, at each look and **wherever the course reads the destination's standing move as a precondition** (§3.1's `Never`, under the
  claim), once nothing it names still waits on its destination's `said`; until then it stands, and its destination takes no move of its own
  (§3.1). *(O7-F11)* A shell beside a headless lease holder runs no look, building its watch only once it holds the lease
  (`Modules/Loop/DriverLoop.cs:839-842`), so round 7's two moments left its conversations' holds standing until its next come-up or a press,
  and the destination's limit move, hand-over or curve held with them; the course's own read closes them first. **A begun effect alone keeps no
  hold**: it waits on no `said`, the hold's note has said it, and it is never repeated by itself whether the hold stands or not. *(O8-F6)* No
  path reads the course before rows h and i adopt it (row e is read by nothing), so the rule's own proof is f-i's with the course called
  directly in its test, and the destination's limit move then made is proven where a path first reads it: a conversation's in row h's
  limit-move branch, a driven line's in row i. Its **effect mark**, the external effect begun (a spawn, a prompt,
  a copy), its moment and, once known, its outcome; `why`, `by`, `how`; the settled selection's account by profile name; the brief's recipe (the
  why and the event bound it is composed up to, `ContextBriefs.cs:59-95`); the delivery: each word's id, producer key, transfer id and
  disposition, a limit's refused turns' bound, the channel messages held. Never a word's text, a path or a key.
- **Who writes and recovers it, claimed and fenced** (round 3, X2-3.1; O2-12; round 4, O3-1, X3-1.1, X3-3.1). The executor of its kind: a
  conversation's move by the shell whose chat runner runs that conversation; a driven line's by the loop whose look runs that line, the
  instance's or a `--share` loop's beside it, each claiming the moves of the lines it runs, since the two race for the same quests and the take
  decides (`Host/Program.cs:869-875`, `:890-895`); a closed quest's session going on as a conversation by the chat runner the look hands it to.
  **The claim is the process run that executes the move**: a run id drawn at that process's start, kept with its pid and its start time (the
  lease's own way of telling a live holder, `Driver/Platform/HomeLease.cs:165-173`, `:427-448`) in the store's `store_runs`, and the move's
  `claim_run` and `claim_generation` (§13.3). Every write is one store transaction under the write lock that checks the claim first, so a read,
  a check and a write never interleave across processes. **A move is taken over only from a run that is dead by the lease's own test**: its pid
  gone, or alive under another start time, a process whose start time cannot be read counting as alive, as the lease's *cannot tell* holds
  (`HomeLease.cs:66-68`, `:446`). Never because a role passed, a lease was given up or shared, or another shell or loop came up on the home: a
  live executor, however long it pauses and whether or not it holds the lease, keeps every move it claimed, so **the claim holds through the
  effect itself**, nothing being able to take it while the process that marked the effect lives (X3-3.1). **Every external effect is fenced by a
  mark** written, under a claim check, before the effect: no longer what keeps a second executor out, which the liveness rule does, but the
  evidence a taker reads, an effect a dead run marked and never recorded being the taker's `held`, made at most once and never repeated by
  itself (§13.4). A hung executor keeps its moves until its process ends: a move past `old-ended` and quiet for ten minutes under a live run is
  said once on its line, a note with its code beside `part.quiet` worded in both catalogues, and in the machine log as `move.quiet`, and the
  person's quit ends it (round 5, §19.1; round 6, *O5-F15*). Of two executors deciding a move of one line, two shells on one home among them,
  the second finds the standing row and reads the line as `Never`; a shell that finds another live shell's run on its home says so once,
  refusing nothing; the home is one machine's, as the lease assumes (§19.1). A process's in-memory exclusion (`GoingOn`, `Chat/ChatContext.cs:64`)
  is no claim. **What is not a move is kept apart by the service's steps** (round 6, *O5-F2*, *C5-1.1*): the keyed open's step first looks its key
  up in `session_opens` and answers a repeat from that row, before any other check, so a retry of a committed open, whose record left is no
  longer the head, is answered and never refused (round 7, *O6-F5*); only then does it refuse unless the record left stands, is its line's head
  (`Lines.HeadOf`, row d-i) and is not active, answering the head, or `gone` for a record that does not stand; and every move of a record out of
  an ended or parked state inside `SessionLedger.AdvanceAsync`'s step (`Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:1660`), the go-on's first step taken
  before anything is spawned (`GoOnAsync`, `:1726`; `Chat/ChatGoOn.cs:663-667`; `Loop/DriverContinuations.cs:273`), the move back to the person
  of an ended record that waited on a helper (`BackToPersonAsync`, `:1727-1728`, `:1901`) and a park's move to working, refuses a record a keyed
  open has left while the record it opened stands, naming that record (round 7, *O6-F6*: round 6 named the go-on alone). So a resume and a keyed open of one record are one or the
  other, whichever step commits first, whether the resumer is a newer shell or an older one talking to this host; a keyed open refused so closes
  its move `superseded`, or `gone` where its record left does not stand, and a go-on refused so holds its words as today's refused go-on does
  (`ChatGoOn.cs:668-671`).
  At its come-up, and at each look after, an executor takes up every move of its kind whose claim's run is dead, and those alone, before
  anything else that could act on the same records: a dead run's conversation moves by any shell of the home, the first to claim under the write
  lock; a dead run's driven moves by the lease holder's look, a `--share` loop taking up only its own. A conversation's take-up runs where
  `LimitMove.TakeUpBoxesAsync` runs today (`ChatGoOn.cs:195`), before the come-up's go-ons (`:213-257`), the hand-over boxes' take-up (`:260`) and
  the sweep (`:136-168`), none of which reads a box today, and before the lease; a driven line's at the slot where uncertain words are settled
  (`DriverWatch.cs:739`); and before either, the imports of any box an older build may have written, which run only once this start's
  confirmation is settled (§13.5): until a box is imported, the course reads its record's old part as `Never`, so no go-on, sweep or take-up acts
  on it meanwhile (O3-4). Taking one up writes the taker's run and a newer generation. The shell's guard for words said meanwhile (`TakeWaits`, `SessionWords.cs:544-545`), which reads only the hand-over
  box, reads the move record. Each phase is safe to run again, and an unknown effect outside Daoris (a spawn or a prompt whose answer was lost) is
  `held` and never repeated by itself (X).
- **The destination, never opened twice.** The ledger's opens take an optional `opens` key (round 4, X3-3.3, O3-12, X3-6.1). The service keeps
  each keyed open in a table of its own, `session_opens`: the key, unique; the record left; the record opened; and every word the open moved,
  with its producer key, its transfer id and its order; written in the open's own exclusive step and kept while its record left stands (below). A
  repeat open with the key answers the record and the words from that table, in the open's own step, even after the words have left `said`
  (`Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:1602-1607`), so a lost answer is answered whole and nothing opens or moves twice (X; §3.4 rule 3). The key travels
  in the open's request and the transfer in its answer; nothing is added to the record and nothing to the wire, so `session-record-fields.md`'s
  chain is not walked. It is row d-i's, landing before any row that moves words. **A row goes with its record left**, by a trigger on the
  record's delete; where its opened record is deleted first, the same trigger keeps the row, its words cleared and its opened record marked gone,
  so a repeat open with its key is answered `gone`, a terminal answer on which the move closes, never another open; the table stays bounded by the
  records the home keeps, and an older build's delete moves it the same way (round 5, §19.5; round 6, *C5-1.5*: round 5 let the rows go with the
  opened record, so a desktop that lost the answer and came back after an older build had deleted the destination opened again). An older host skips an `opens` member it
  does not know (`Http/ApiContracts.cs:611-617` at `abf03ea26`), so the desktop reads the host's protocol marks, kept per host run, before it
  opens (§19.12; *C5-1.12*), and reads an answer with no echo of its key as an older host's. **A keyed step whose answer was lost is asked
  again only after the marks are read again by `GET /api/protocol`**, since the host that lost it may have died with it and an older host
  replaced it; and **a retried open answered without the key's echo is held for the person**, the records `from` the one left named as
  candidates, never made as today's, since the first open may have made a destination and moved its words before its answer was lost (round 7,
  *O6-F4*). Against a host
  without `opens`, which answers a new record each time and moves no words, the move is made as today's are, and an open whose answer was lost is
  `held` for the person with the candidates named, never searched for by `from` and moment, since successors are not unique by `from`
  (`Sessions.cs:998`; X-3.5), so the guarantee *never twice* is a newer host's alone.
- **No old box is written beside it** (round 3; X2-1.1, O2-14). Round 2 wrote `<s>.limit-move.json` and the conversation's hand-over box beside
  the move record through the rollback window and claimed the two builds dedupe against each other by the limit box's key. They do not: a
  producer's key answers only while its word waits on `said` (`SessionLedger.cs:956`), the limit box's first delivery is a live turn rather than a
  keyed word (`ChatHandOver.SayHandedOver`, `ChatHandOver.cs:238-250`, called at `LimitMove.cs:277`; only the take-up's re-delivery is keyed, its
  guard the new part's `User` event, `:366-378`), and the hand-over's take clears its box however it came out, leaving a part it opened unbriefed
  (`HandOver.cs:271`, `:294`). So a move the store carries writes no box, and an older build recovers it from the ledger alone, as it recovers today
  a move that keeps no box; §13.5's table says what it does at each phase and what the newer build reads of its evidence when it comes back,
  before it acts, the line's head reconciled in every phase before anything is delivered (X3-3.4). A box an older build writes while it runs is
imported once at the newer build's come-up, once that start's confirmation is settled, and set aside. Three records are not moves and stay what they are (O): the hand-over's **ask** (`HandOverAskBox.cs:28`), the agent's request
  through the connector, the service's, the move beginning at its approval; `data/handed/<quest>.json`, the agent a quest is handed to until it
  leaves the machine (`Driver.cs:1914-1917`), a constraint the course reads; and `<s>.new-session.json`, the person's press. The last two stay
  files in Part B, as today: z-viii, which would move them into the store, is proposed for a later design (§19.15; round 6, *O5-F12*). A same-conversation
  resume writes none: no word changes record.

Today one path of five keeps a move durable, the ended limit move's box (survey §2.5); the record lands first on the live limit move, the
curve and the driven carry-on, which keep none.

### 3.4 Words across a move

The dispatcher's guarantee (`.claude/knowledge/session-words.md`) holds by construction once six rules hold, each in one place:

1. **A word keeps its whole identity everywhere it is held**: its producer's key, level, files and display choice, through the shell's held words,
   their file and the record path, and the headless fallback (§0.2; row b). A display choice never becomes `SaidWord.Choice`.
2. **Words follow the line's head, judged inside the say door's exclusive step.** `SessionLedger.SayAsync` already reads, judges and writes as one
   step (`SessionLedger.cs:931-973`); it gains the head inside it, by **one service function**, `Lines.HeadOf`, which follows the `from` chain
   across kinds, a driven part's successor conversation included (a closed quest's lost session already goes on so today, `Driver.cs:2374-2381`),
   and for a driven part with no successor its quest's last run here; the partner desk's walk (`PartnerDesk.ContinuedByAsync`,
   `Core/Sessions/PartnerDesk.cs:114-120`) and the helper desk's (`HelperDesk.cs:140-145`) read the same function (O-6; X-3.4). The
   service has no such refusal today (`SessionSayRefusal`, `SessionLedger.cs:89-175`): a new value, `Continued`, carries the head, read by
   `ServiceClient` and the headless door (`SessionSay.cs:207-209`), each say caller's handling named in row d's census from the survey §2.5's
   list, an older driver reading it as the refusal it already reads for an unknown value. No part can open between the judgement and the keep.
   The driver's `WordsNever.ContinuedIn`, which follows chats alone (`SessionSay.cs:54-58`), stays the older host's fallback (row d). The walk
   is indexed, since the sessions table has none on `from_session` or `quest` and the step holds the write lock: row d-i adds `sessions_from`
   and `sessions_quest` with `Lines.HeadOf`, which its keyed open's check reads (round 6, *O5-F2*); a word whose record has no successor costs one
   probe on `sessions_from`, and a driven part with none a second on `sessions_quest`, its quest's last run here (`Sessions.cs:1041-1051`), each
   branch measured (*C5-1.13*); row d-ii reads the walk in the say door and lands only with the say door's p95 on the install within a tenth of before
   (round 5, §19.13).
3. **The open moves the words** (round 3). The new record opens in one service step that also moves the old part's waiting words: inside
   the say door's exclusive step (`SessionLedger.cs:933`), the ledger opens the new record under the move's `opens` key (§3.3) and keeps on it
   the words the course named by id (`Carries`, rule 6, judged in .NET) and every word of the person's kept on the old part since the course
   read it, which the service tells apart by its author fields (`SaidWord`'s `From`, `By` and `Helper`, `Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:392-424`),
   each under **its producer key** with a **transfer id** (the move's key and the word's own id, so a word with no key has one too, X2-3.3), and
   takes them off the old. So the head and the words move at once, and no word falls between a read and an open. A word said to the old part
   after the open is refused there with the head named (rule 2), and its caller passes it on. The keyed open is refused where the record left
   does not stand, is not its line's head or is active (§3.3; round 6, *O5-F2*). A retried open with the same key answers the
   record it opened and the words it moved from the service's `session_opens`, whether or not those words still wait, and `gone` once that record
   is deleted (*C5-1.5*), and moves nothing again,
   so a lost answer neither opens twice nor moves twice (X-3.6; X3-3.3); the move record keeps the moved ids it is answered (§3.3). Moved words keep their order ahead of any word kept on the new part after its open, since
   the new part's first turn is the move's delivery (O-19). LIMITWORD1's second read, and the reads before the open that the ended and live limit
   moves, the driven carry-on, and the briefed move and its not-offered path make today (`BriefedMove.cs:99`, `:158`, `:179`, `:250`; O-7), are
   retired by it. Row d's census takes every site that hands words to a part it opens, `from` or not: start-from's `Start` names the record it
   takes the words from, with no `from` link (`ChatRunner.StartFrom.cs:101-131`), and a refused open moves its words (`ChatRunner.cs:1961-1982`).
   **What those sites read once the open moves the words** (round 7, *O6-F13*): each composes its delivery from the destination's moved words,
   never from the old record's ids, the ended limit move's among them, whose body reads the old record's waiting ids and their files
   (`Chat/LimitMove.cs:338-355`, `:344`, `:350-351`), whose `DeliveredAsync` takes by the old record's ids (`:373-376`), and whose
   `TakeUpBoxesAsync` says the composed message to the new part again under `limit-move/<session>` at each come-up (`:394-429`, `:419-420`), each
   named in d-ii's census until f-vi retires the box. **The delivery records each moved word's `User` event under its id** on the destination,
   as `ResumeAsk.Opening` records a go-on's words (`Driver/Conversations/Domain/ResumeAsk.cs:112-134`), where today a move's message is one
   joined `Asked` event with no id (`Chat/ChatRunner.cs:2588-2604`, `:2641-2647`), so the conversation pairs each word shown waiting with where
   it was asked and a hold can say per word whether the destination shows it (round 7, *O6-F2*); that event is the display's, recorded before
   the write, and never delivery's evidence (rule 5). **The move's take-up says the `lost` note itself** *(O7-F4)*: `UncertainWords` writes it
   only for a word whose own `User` event it does not find (`ShownTaken`, `Conversations/GoOn/UncertainWords.cs:84-91`, `:96-98`), so a moved
   word attempted and cut off before the write would be taken back with no *Say it again*; the take-up, which settles a moved word attempted
   without `sent` before any come-up's sweep reaches it (§3.3), writes the note for every such word whatever the destination's events show.
   *(O8-F8)* An older build's come-up has no take-up: it finds the word's own event and settles the word with no note, where today a move's
   one joined event, which has no id, leaves it the note; so during a downgrade such a word is settled silently, and the person learns of it
   only when the newer build returns, which holds the move and offers each of the person's *left* words for saying again (§13.5's
   `delivering` row; row f-ii's downgrade case). The
   event stays before the write, so the conversation shows the person's words before the agent's answer, which an event recorded as the write
   returns could not promise, the door's pump rendering the agent's frames as they come (`Driver/Agents/Acp/Acp.cs:2426-2430`). **A moved
   word's files go with it** (round 7, *O6-F8*): files are kept per record, by its
   id (`Chat/ChatFiles.cs:90-110`), and the open moves none, so the site copies each moved word's files from the folder of the record the open
   moved it from into the destination's, by their kept names, before it delivers; the copy is idempotent, a kept name already there passed
   over, so every later reader (a go-on's `ChatFiles.Kept`, a take-back's offer, a later move) finds them where the word waits.
   Row d needs no settlement row for this (X2-5.1): the open's key, its `session_opens` row and the transfer ids on the new record are its
   idempotency, inside one service step. The keyed open and its table are row d-i, proven in process there and across a crash in row f-i, and
   the head and the sites that move words row d-ii (O3-12; X3-6.1).
4. **Every word is kept on `said` before anything holds it, a winding-up or moving record included.** The say door keeps a word for an active
   record whose dispatcher closed as waiting for its next part, asked only by the shell and the driver as `running` is (refused today,
   `SessionLedger.cs:945-952`); the shell's holds become keeps; `held-words.json` is read once at a come-up for an older shell's words and,
   against a host whose marks keep `opens` and the head, never written again, the shell keeping today's hold against one without them (§19.11;
   round 6, *O5-F12*); a chat dispatcher's `Hold()` during a move (`ChatTurns.cs:468-476`, under which a word is neither kept nor taken, `:192`) keeps
   and queues, as a limit's wait and a press already do (`:499-510`, `:570-599`). Such a word is the move's while the move stands (§3.1's
   `Never`). An older sweep would resume an old part in the tree its successor holds (`ChatGoOn.cs:191-262`; `SessionWords.cs:455-458`; O-2),
   but against a host whose marks keep `opens` and the head the successor and the move of the old part's words are one exclusive step, and the
   say door refuses the old part a word after it, so the two never coexist; before the open, an older shell's resume does what it would had the
   move never been decided, and the newer build closes the move superseded (§13.5). So **the shape is `said` itself** (round 5, §19.11), proven by
   an older shell's own come-up and sweep at each phase; against a host without those marks the shell keeps today's hold. It lands after rows h
   and i (row o). **What `said` does not reach** (round 6, *C5-1.11*, *O5-F2*): an older build's own move reads its words before its own open, which
   carries no key, and delivers its copy (`Chat/BriefedMove.cs:99`, `:142-143`, its take at `:158`), and an older go-on sends a word whose mark
   failed (`Driver/Conversations/SaidDoor.cs:47-62`, `:84-88`; `Driver/Service/ServiceClient.cs:569`); so against an older build's own move a word
   can go twice, once by each build, as between two of today's shells on one home (§19.1), each delivery said on its own part. *Once* is asserted
   only where the service's steps decide the order: a keyed open against a say, a take, a go-on's step or another keyed open.
5. **A word is attempted once, its disposition kept, and an attempt acknowledged before the wire.** The mark that fails open today (§0.2) becomes
   mandatory, and the attempt's answer names each word it marked, so a word it does not name is not attempted and does not go: today the service
   answers an `unmarked` list (`SessionLedger.cs:1234-1246`) that the client ignores, answering success (`Driver/Service/ServiceClient.cs:569`)
   (X-3.7). **Each attempt keeps one disposition, per attempt** (X2-3.2): **taken** (the turn took it: never sent again); **refused at a limit**,
   only where the door's refusal is conclusive that the agent never accepted the prompt the word was attempted in (the turn refused as a limit:
   the definite refusal carries it once to the part that goes on, as a limit's refused turns' words are carried today, `Chat/ChatTurns.cs:1225`;
   `LimitWaits.cs:223`); **uncertain** otherwise (attempted, no conclusive answer: never sent again by itself, said with *Say it again*). A try the
   provider failed as transient is no conclusive refusal, since the agent may have read its prompt, which is why `LimitHeld.Of` clears every word
   asked before a transient event (`Driver/Conversations/Domain/LimitHeld.cs:35-41`): a word attempted in such a try stays uncertain, and a limit
   that then ends the episode carries only the words no try attempted, never reclassifying an earlier attempt. Round 2's *a transient try
   followed by a limit's refusal is the limit's* is withdrawn. **A word's settlement is the service's** (round 4, X3-3.2, O3-5, X3-1.2). Round 3
   kept a producer's receipt in the desktop store and had the desktop's client read it before it said; that races the service's take, since a
   retry can read no receipt, pause, and reach the say door after another dispatcher wrote the receipt and the word was taken, where the door
   finds no waiting key (`SessionLedger.cs:956`) and keeps a second word (`:965`); and two keyed producers never pass the say door at all, a
   helper's answer under `helper:<id>` and a partner's piece under `partner:<piece>`, each kept by its own desk's `KeepSaidAsync` through its own
   route (`Core/Sessions/HelperDesk.cs:180-189`; `Core/Sessions/PartnerDesk.cs:136-147`; `Http/Program.cs:1739-1748`). So **a word leaves `said`
   only by a statement that settles it**: a trigger on `sessions`, `AFTER UPDATE OF said`, guarded by a real change on this machine's record,
   writes into the service's `said_settled`, for each word the list lost, one row keyed by **the record it left, the word's id and its producer
   key** (or, for a word with none, `id:<word id>`), with its moment, `INSERT OR REPLACE` on those three, so a word that leaves two records (moved
   by an open, then taken) leaves a row on each, and no row of another record or of another word is ever replaced (round 7, *C6-1.2*, *C6-3.1*:
   round 6 keyed the row by the word's id alone and called it unique, but a word's id is eight hexadecimal characters drawn at random,
   `Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:1383-1384`, and an answer kept before `said` reads as the literal `answer` on every record,
   `Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:1545`, `:1651`, so two unrelated records' words could replace each other's settlement; round 6's own correction of
   round 5, *C5-1.2*, *O5-F7*, keyed the row by the record rather than the line, `Core/Sessions/SessionEntries.cs:46`). An answer still in the old
   column of a record from before `said` settles nothing when taken, its take leaving `said` null on both sides (`Sessions.cs:1597-1612`), and no
   producer keyed it; once a word is kept beside it, it is `said`'s first word under the id `answer` (`:1566-1572`) and settles as any keyless
   word does, under its own record. So every writer settles a word as it takes it, a connector built before the table included,
   and no take changes (round 5, §19.2). The settlement keeps no disposition, which is the attempt's (`move_words`, the record's notes): round 4's
   disposition column is withdrawn. **It says only that the word left `said`**, never that a turn took it: a take-back, an uncertain word's
   settlement, a park's clear and a supersession leave by the same statements (`Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:1312`;
   `GoOn/UncertainWords.cs:72`) (round 6, *C5-1.4*, *O5-F6*). A word an open moves leaves the old record for the new one, its transfer `session_opens`'s.
   **Every door that answers a producer's key, the supervisor's excepted (below), reads the same function inside its own exclusive step**,
   `SaidKeys.Settled(records, key)`, over the waiting words of those records and `said_settled` by key and record (its index, `said_settled_key`,
   on the key and the record), **`records` being the line's records**: the record the say names and its predecessors back along `from`, one probe
   by id each, from row p, and from d-ii also the records the head walk visits forward to the head, a record already walked ending either walk
   (round 7, *C6-1.3*, *O6-F1*: round 6 walked only forward from the record named, so a retry that names a successor missed a settlement on its
   predecessor, as the shell's pump does when it forwards a word whose say's answer it lost to the conversation its record went on in,
   `Modules/Loop/SessionWords.cs:773-799`, `:843-863`): the say door (`SessionLedger.cs:956`), the helper's desk
   (`HelperDesk.cs:180`) and the partner's (`PartnerDesk.cs:137`), each step a `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction across processes
   (`Sessions.ExclusiveAsync`, `Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:785-786`). **Each door asks it first**, before any refusal but the record's absence: the
   say door before `Never`, `running` and `Continued`, where today it answers `Never` and `running` before the key (`SessionLedger.cs:941-956`),
   and each desk before its own refusals, so a re-say at the head after `Continued` and a retry to a record now running are answered as kept,
   never refused and never kept twice (round 7, *O6-F1*). **A key the line has settled is answered `Settled`, its own terminal kind, never a
   word** *(C7-1.1, O7-F12; narrowed by O8-F1)*. The door answers `Again`, with the word, only for a word waiting on the record the say names,
   as today (`SessionLedger.cs:956-961`); a key that left `said` from a record of the walk within the horizon, or, from d-ii, whose word waits
   on the line's head, is answered `Settled`: nothing kept and no word, its message naming the record that holds it (the record the word
   left, or the head where it waits to go), on the wire a success with `settled` and that record and no `said`. **A key whose word waits on a
   record the line has left is answered by the door as it stands on the record named** *(O8-F1)*: its key read on that record's waiting words
   alone, as today, so it is kept there as a new word (from d-ii, refused `Continued` at a record that is not the head, and kept at the head).
   Such a word never goes on where it waits: the go-on judges a continued record's words `Continued` or `Superseded`
   (`Conversations/GoOn/SessionSay.cs:40-45`) and the come-up skips them (`Chat/ChatGoOn.cs:228`), as 19.11 says. Round 8 answered such a key
   `Settled`, so the shell's pump, forwarding a word whose say's answer it lost (`Modules/Loop/SessionWords.cs:789-792`) to the conversation
   its record went on in (`:773-779`, `:843-863`) after a briefed or live limit move had read the old record's words before the keep and taken
   only those (`Chat/BriefedMove.cs:99`, `:157-158`), dropped the word, delivered nowhere, where today the successor keeps it and delivers it
   once; the same held for an older build's unkeyed successor after d-ii and for the helper desk's walk to the newest asker
   (`Core/Sessions/HelperDesk.cs:140-176`). Round 7 answered a settled key `Again` with the settled word's id, but the wire carries no `Again` (`Http/Program.cs:1546`;
   `Driver/Service/ServiceClient.cs:512-515` reads the word alone), both dispatchers keep an id they have not seen as a new word of the record
   named (`Chat/ChatTurns.cs:207-215`; `Conversations/DrivenInbox.cs:441-462`), and its attempt and take find a word by its id alone
   (`Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:1623-1637`, `:1597-1612`): a retry answered with the id of a word that left another record could mark and take
   a different word with the same eight characters on the record named, the retry's own text sent again; and the shell's `Show` wrote the
   answered word as a `User` event on the record the say named (`Modules/Loop/SessionWords.cs:1025-1045`). **Only a client that asks gets
   it**: the request's `settled: true`, which the newer client sends on every keyed say and hand; a request without it, an older client's, is
   answered by today's door, its refusals first and its key read on the named record's waiting words alone, so after a take it keeps a second
   word, as today, and never an id of another record (19.12); an older host reads no such field and answers as today. **Every reader of a keyed
   say's or hand's answer handles it before it reads a word**, each named in p's census: the client's `SayAnswer` carries it, kept and with no
   word; `ChatTurns.KeepAsync` and `DrivenInbox.KeepAsync` answer it as a retry of a word they hold already, holding, queueing, steering,
   attempting and taking nothing; the shell's pump takes its held word off and its `Show` writes nothing (`SessionWords.cs:796-797`); the
   helper pass's dispatch and the partner's event (`Loop/DriverHelpers.cs:816-817`; `Loop/DriverPartners.cs:486-492`) dispatch and record
   nothing, the hand marked done; every other say caller of the census (row b's `SayKeysCensusTests` list) reads it as kept. **The supervisor's
   door stays today's** *(O7-F12)*: `SuperviseAsync` compares a retry's decision, question, choice and text (`SessionLedger.cs:1031-1044`),
   which a settlement row cannot carry, so it reads no settlement and answers its key only while its word waits, as today, while no
   appointment is offered (19.9). So a retry after the take is answered as kept by any keyed producer but the supervisor, with no read across
   stores and no window between a check and a keep; and the one line rule SQL and C# share is the record's own id, the walk giving the
   line, so a record with no stored `entry` and its successors, whatever theirs, answer one word and no `entry` index is needed (*C5-1.2*, *O5-F7*,
   *O5-F8*). **The horizon, decided** (round 5, §19.2): *never sent again by itself* holds
   with no horizon for the attempt, a word attempted never re-sent whatever its age, and for a producer's retry of a say whose answer it lost
   for seven days after the word last left `said`, past which a retry is a new word, said on its line; the trigger trims **by time alone**, in its
   own statement over an index on the moment, and keeps no count cap, so the seven days hold exactly, and the table's size is a week's takes,
   which row p measures on the install and lands within (round 6, *C5-1.2*: round 5's cap of 50,000 rows could trim a settlement inside the
   week). **Its identity** (X2-3.3) is the key over one line's records, so
   equal keys on unrelated lines are two words, equal word ids on unrelated records two rows, and a retry through several successors, or to any
   of them, finds its word (round 7, *C6-1.2*, *C6-1.3*). The desktop store keeps a
   move's view alone: each moved word's disposition in `move_words`, its `sent` written as its door's write returns and before its take (below),
   and the phase `delivered` written **after** the service has taken the word, so no word waits on `said` while its move says `delivered`
   (round 5, §19.4), and a crash between is finished at the come-up
   **from the move's own `sent`, never from the settlement or a session event** (round 7, *C6-1.4*, *C6-3.2*, *O6-F2*: round 6 read the
   destination's `User` event for the word, but the runner records that event before the write, the go-on's opening at
   `Chat/ChatRunner.cs:3502-3506` before its prompt at `:3509`, a turn's at `:3517` before `:3519`, and the native door's at `:3758` and `:3765`
   before `processes.Send` at `:3771`, which can fail, `:3771-3783`, so a crash after the event and before the write, then an older come-up's
   uncertain take-back, `GoOn/UncertainWords.cs:72`, read as taken a word no agent was sent; `UncertainWords.ShownTaken`, `:96-98`, makes that
   reading to decide whether its `lost` note is written, `:84-91`, which is why the move's take-up writes its own, rule 3 *(O7-F4)*; and a
   move's message records no per-word event at all today, `Chat/ChatRunner.cs:2588-2604`). **The executor writes each moved word's `sent` in
   `move_words` as its door's write returns**, in one store transaction **before** its dispatcher takes the word *(O7-F5)*: on the ACP door at
   the prompt's `OnWire` (`Driver/Agents/Acp/Acp.cs:2109-2113`), whose `wrote` is the conversation's turn's `sent` (`Chat/ChatTurns.cs:1226-1233`,
   then the message's `Delivered` and the take), since a move's composed message carries no word id (`Chat/LimitMove.cs:353-354`) and the door
   records its `sent` receipt only for a message that does (`Acp.cs:2120-2122`); on the native driven door as the new part's start returns
   its process (`Loop/Driver.cs:3661-3664`), row f-iv's, where no `sent` is recorded today; and, for a driven new part on the protocol door
   (`Loop/Driver.cs:3730`, `:3810`), as its first prompt's write returns, outside `ChatTurns`, row f-iv's too *(O8-F7)*: the run's
   `prompted`, told once that prompt is on the wire (`Driver/Agents/Acp/Acp.cs:797-800`, `:846-852`), from `SendPromptAsync`'s `OnWire`
   (`:2066`, `:2109-2113`), and, where a transient failure opens an episode, each try's write return, `TryAsync`'s `OnWire` (`:1482`,
   `:1535`). Round 7 named the receipt and
   `Loop/DriverContinuations.cs:1252-1256`, which is the native go-on's resumed run and writes no move record. So at the come-up a word gone from `said` with `sent` was written to its agent and is *taken*; one still waiting with `sent` is taken
   off as taken by the move's take-up and never written again, and where an uncertain settlement took it first it is gone with `sent`, taken
   all the same; one gone without `sent` is *left*, never delivered by the move, whichever door took it off (the person's take-back, an older
   come-up's uncertain settlement, an older build's own delivery); one still waiting, attempted and without `sent`, is settled uncertain and
   said, as any attempted word is; and a `sent` whose transaction fails leaves the word read *left*, the conservative side. The move closes
   `delivered` when every word it moved has `sent`, and is `held` otherwise, naming its *left* and uncertain words and its begun effect. An older host writes no settlement and its doors answer a key only while its word waits, as today; an older
   writer over the migrated database settles its takes through the trigger and reads no settlement (§19.12). The come-up sends only words proven never attempted (X; row p). Across a harness write that carries no receipt, that is the guarantee there is: no automatic second
   attempt, the evidence kept, the person's press.
6. **Which words a new part carries is one predicate**, `Carries`, where five sites write it today (survey §2.4 row 14): the person's waiting
   words and a limit's refused turns' words, each once; Daoris's never carried, composed again under their keys; a helper's answer and another
   agent's findings never to a new part (D188 point 4).

### 3.5 The errors

D210's four kinds are the course's failure input, read by the one reader for every door and every kind of run.

| Kind | The course's answer | What changes |
|---|---|---|
| `limit` | §3.2: resume own at its reset, go on now elsewhere, or wait; never a strike (D206 point 1) | a conversation's and a brainstorm's limit read by the reader's code as well as its sentence (today the sentence alone, `ChatRunner.cs:2697`; `DriverBrainstorms.cs:778`); a conversation's limited record written as the ledger's flag at its end, which travels already, instead of a scan of its events (`LimitHeld.cs:17-56`) |
| `access` | `Hold` for the person, naming the account (D210 point 5) | — |
| `transient` | the retry inside the run, driven and conversation (AGENTERRORS1a, 1b); past its bound `Hold`, released by the person's word or *Try again* | AGENTERRORS1's remaining rows reach the other doors and kinds as designed |
| `fatal` | the end with its reason; the planner's strikes as today | — |

A limit or access refusal at a resume's open runs the same readers as every other end (AGENTERRORS1's C4-6), since the open's failure
reaches the course like any other. One try count per kind is stated in the course's table: five transient tries, one native resume that exited
before naming its conversation, three strikes.

## 4. What is said: the event

### 4.1 One envelope, two minters, three orders

A `DaorisEvent` is `{ position | (run, seq), at, code, subject, scope, facts }`: an immutable description of a committed change, its facts
generated ids, closed codes, counts and times, never a name, a word, a path, an account or a key.

| | Record events | Machine events |
|---|---|---|
| What | a service record moved: a part opened (with its `from`, `why`, `by`), moved to a state, limited; a word kept or taken; a quest operation; an ask or a channel message kept | a fact no record holds: an account's wait, a Confirm waiting, a tool call held, a part gone quiet, a landing tried, a move's phase, the look's news, a child seen |
| Minted | **by trigger, in the statement that writes the record**, into `event_journal` in the service's database, so every writer mints it (the host, the driver through it, each connector, and a connector built before the table), as the revisions are minted (`Sessions.cs:652-681`). A trigger journals the **raw transition** only (the record, the column, its old and new value, and for `said` each word's id added or removed), guarded by what changed (`WHEN OLD.state IS NOT NEW.state`, and per column), since `AFTER UPDATE OF` fires on a statement's list and `SetStateAsync` names `state`, `limited` and `said` on every move (`Sessions.cs:899-902`); an attempt mark, which rewrites `said` at its length (`:1626-1632`), journals nothing; a mirror's upsert (`:931-936`) journals nothing, since another machine's records are not this machine's facts; a trigger holds no logic that can raise, so an older writer's statement cannot fail through it (O-4; X-4.1). Each row carries **an allow-listed snapshot** of the facts its coding needs, taken from OLD and NEW at capture, so no later lookup is needed (round 3, X2-4.1; below). The code, the end's class, the work and a park's `for` are judged in .NET at projection from that snapshot alone, never in SQL (D182 point 6) | **by the hub of the process that runs the work**, at the judgement that owns the fact (O): the lease's loop for driven work, the shell for its conversations, the executor for a move's phases. The lease's loop alone tells plugins and outside readers (EVENTHOOK1 §4.1) |
| Id and order | `position`, an integer in commit order under the store's write lock, never reused after a trim, its epoch the store's identity | `run` and `seq` (EVENTHOOK1 §4.3) |
| Wake | the journal's insert moves a family of its own in the change stream, `journal`, so a reader is woken by every append, a word taken included, which today moves neither the session's revision nor the words family (`Sessions.cs:1605`, `:677`; X-4.1), and by nothing the guards leave out; the stream names `journal` only to a reader that asks for it, since an older client's stream reader wakes its `ledger` topic for any family named (`Driver/Service/LedgerChanges.cs:66-70`, `:107-115`) and only its family-filtering readers drop one (`Modules/Loop/QuestsStateSlice.cs:132-138`) (round 6, *O5-F10*) | the hub's teller |
| Scope | the record's workspace, filtered as data (NEEDSSCOPE1) | this home |
| Kept | durable, machine-local, never on the sync wire, in the service's own store (the brief §9); at most 50,000 rows of at most 1 KiB each, the insert's own statement deleting the oldest past the count, so an older build that never trims still bounds it; the **floor**, the lowest position kept, answered with every read (X-4.2; round 3's bound per row, X2-4.2) | memory, one run, bounded per reader with `missed` |

**The capture schema** (round 3; X2-4.1, X2-4.2). One table, `event_journal`, one row per transition, every column but `position`, `at`,
`record` and `change` nullable, so no writer's statement can leave out a value a constraint requires:

| Column | Holds | Bound |
|---|---|---|
| `position`, `at` | the commit order and the moment | an integer never reused; the store's clock |
| `record`, `family`, `change` | the record's id; its family (`sessions`, `quest_log`, `asks`, `channel`); one closed code of what changed (`opened`, `state`, `limited`, `word-added`, `word-removed`, `words-overflow`, `quest-op`, `ask`, `message`) | ids at most 64 characters (`substr`), codes 32 |
| `state_old`, `state_new`, `limited_old`, `limited_new` | the state and the limit flag on either side (round 4's `transition` column, the record's transition number, withdrawn in round 5 with `session_transitions`: the row's `position` is the occurrence's one key, §4.5, §19.6) | codes |
| `note_codes` | the codes of the note's parts on NEW, at most eight, never their values or the note's text | 8 × 64 |
| `marks` | the work's facts on NEW: `kind`, and whether `ask`, `opinion`, `serves`, `piece`, `help_mode` and `daoris:help` hold, and the mirror's origin | codes and flags |
| `entry`, `from_session`, `from_why`, `from_by`, `workspace`, `quest` | the line, the part it came from and why, its scope and its quest | ids and codes |
| `word`, `word_author`, `word_level`, `word_keyed` | for `word-*`: the word's id; its author class from `From`, `By` and `Helper` (`person`, `daoris`, `agent`, `supervisor`, `helper`, `opinion`); its level; whether it carries a producer key | ids and codes, never the word's text or key |
| `op`, `ask`, `message`, `line_code` | a quest's operation, an ask's or a message's id, the channel's line code | ids and codes |

A row is at most 1 KiB, so the table's payload is at most 50 MiB at its 50,000 rows, plus its one index; row k's measured landing limit (under
5 MB at the install's rate) is the typical size, never the bound. Pages a trim frees are reused, so the file stays at its high-water mark without a
`VACUUM`; the bound leaves out the write-ahead log, which SQLite checkpoints, measured by row k. A statement that adds more than 64 words journals
the first 64 and one `words-overflow` row with the count, so an overflow is said, never silent; no path adds more than one today. **Never raising,
qualified** (X2-4.2): the triggers hold no `RAISE` and no constraint their inserts can violate, so no writer's statement fails through their logic;
a storage failure (a full disk, an I/O error) fails the whole statement, the record's write with it, since a trigger's insert is part of its
statement: capture is atomic with the write, never skipped. **The projection's inputs are immutable** (X2-4.1): .NET codes a transition from its
row's snapshot alone, since a record's state and notes are overwritten (`Sessions.cs:886`) and a word leaves `said` (`:1605`); row k proves an
end, a reopen and an end, and a park, its answer and a park again, all made before any reader runs, each coded from its own rows.

**Each process reads the journal from its come-up** (O-3): the shell for its conversations' record events whether or not it holds the lease,
as `ServiceConfirmations.RunAsync` runs before it (`DriverLoop.cs:787`), and the lease's loop for driven work and the outside readers. Today
a conversation's end reaches the shell in process from the routes' `onEnded` (`ConversationRoutes.cs:55`; `DriverLoop.cs:1233`) whoever holds
the lease, and a shell waiting on the lease builds no watch and hears no change stream (`DriverLoop.cs:841`, `:895`), so a projection moved onto
the journal without this would lose or delay a conversation's events while a headless loop drives.

**Three orders, never one** (X): the journal's `position` orders what a store committed; `run`/`seq` orders what a hub told; a slice's
`epoch`/`seq` orders a projection's snapshots and deltas (D182 point 3). A timestamp orders nothing across processes. The change stream's families
stay the wake (`ChangeStream.cs:76-80`): the journal is what a woken reader reads, so it learns *what* moved, in order, rather than *that* a family
moved. Records stay the truth: the journal is never replayed into state, so this is no event-sourced database (D182's rejection; D182 point 7's
*no new bus*: the journal is a table and the hub a teller on the bus there is). **What a trim loses** is said: a reader whose cursor is below the
floor reads its records whole, and its history between the cursor and the floor is incomplete, since a record's state and notes are overwritten
(`Sessions.cs:899`) and a park answered between cannot be rebuilt from them; the reader says *incomplete since* its cursor, never a complete
history (X-4.2).

### 4.2 The vocabulary

One registry, `EventCodes`, beside `NoteCodes`, extending EVENTHOOK1's `WorkEventGrammar` (O): each code with its closed facts, its privacy class
(this machine, or outside) and its rendering family (X), each judged in .NET from the journal's raw transitions and the hubs' facts. A code without
words in both catalogues fails its census, so *ConfirmingRestored*'s English toast (survey §3.3) cannot recur. Codes are subject-specific where the
facts differ (X): a part ends, a line of work goes on, work lands.

| Kind of change | Code | Facts |
|---|---|---|
| a part opened | `part.opened` | work, entry, `from`, `why`, `by` |
| a park, and its answer | `part.parked`, `part.answered` | `for` (the person-waits kinds, `person-waits.md`); `by` |
| an end | `part.ended` | state; `reason`, the note's `ended.*` code; `class`, one closed code from the record's state, its `limited` flag and its note's code (`completed`, `failed`, `limited`, `transient-bound`, `access`, `stopped-person`, `stopped-role`, `stopped-daoris`, `refused-choice`, `awaits-helper`, `stood-down`, `unknown`), judged in .NET; where the look concluded a driven end, its `SessionEnded` flags (`Attention.cs:250-252`) refine the class in the look's process, never in the trigger |
| a limit | `part.limited`, `account.waits` | the reset as a moment; the account never off this machine |
| a move | `move.decided`, `move.settled`, `move.held` | the move's old and new parts, `how`, `why`, reason |
| a word | `words.kept`, `words.taken`, `delivery.uncertain` | the word's id, `by`, level |
| *answered*, four facts apart | `turn.answered`, `channel.replied`, `question.answered`, `part.answered` | each its own correlation (survey §3.3) |
| a landing | `work.landed`; `work.integrated` once FABRIC-END proves the line holds it | its code |
| a wait on the person | `person.waiting`, `person.answered` | its kind, a confirmation's id; only for a wait no record holds, a park being `part.parked` alone |
| a quest's operation | `quest.<op>` | the quest log's operation names, mapped at projection from the channel's own line codes, which keep their names: they are read from the quest per read, never stored (`Core/Asks/Channel.cs:106-116`, `:1121-1133`), and reach the page, the connector's `channel_read` and older builds (O-20) |
| a held call; quiet | `call.held`; `part.quiet` | the part |
| a child | `child.seen`, `child.ended` | part, child |
| the hub | `run.started`, `events.missed` | the counts of what waits; missed |

The four meanings of *ended* (survey §3.4 row 1) become one event and four filters in one table, each tested: `SESSION_ENDED` keeps its contract,
a conversation's end (`DriverLoop.cs:1228-1234`); `session/ended` the ends the look drained, as today; the log's `session.ended` every end; *Ended*
the classes the watch says today (`Attention.cs:246-252`). The usage slice wakes on `part.ended` rather than on `SESSION_ENDED`, so a driven end
reaches it at once. `cause`, derived from the class by one table (`person`, `role`, `daoris`, `agent`, `limit`, `transient`, `choice`, `unknown`),
is what a notification reads. `unknown` is a record from before codes, or a writer that wrote none: what nothing said stays unknown (D173's guard).
EVENTHOOK1's published kinds, `session.parked` and `session.ended`, keep their narrower meaning through its adapter, never silently redefined (X).

**Where a sentence is composed.** On the page from the catalogue, in both languages, as notes are (D116; `translation-parity.md`); on the terminal
and in the log in Daoris's English from the same table; to a plugin and an outside agent never a sentence, the code and ids alone (EVENTHOOK1 §4.3).
No reader parses a sentence back into a code.

### 4.3 The channels

| Channel (survey §3.1) | After |
|---|---|
| Pushed slices | **state**, D182's, woken by events, carrying no news |
| `STATE_CHANGED`, `DRIVER_TICK` | compatibility wakes for unmoved readers and plugins (D182 point 2), retired slice by slice; `DRIVER_TICK`'s data (considered, untrusted, waits) moves into the driver and attention slices, so no key is both pushed and invalidated |
| Held answers | demand-driven answers (D182 point 2), each declaring its inputs and forgotten when one moves, a stale completion refused by its version (X; COWORKVIEW1 row a's source fence) |
| `UNDER_WAY`, the confirmations' lists, command answers | correlated operation state, each its own numbered telling; command answers private to their requester |
| `SESSION_ENDED`, `SESSION_ATTENTION`, `NOTICE`, `MESSAGE` | projections of events through one filter table; the streams (`SESSION_OUTPUT`, `SESSION_STREAMS`, `TERMINAL_*`) stay streams |
| The attention watch | an attention policy over events; it no longer establishes that a park happened by comparing snapshots (X) |
| Notifications | the news projection: the notices its one durable record, owned by `Notices`, today `data/notices.json` under the file's keeping (`Driver/Platform/Notices.cs:12-17`, its `KeepLock`) and from its row the desktop store's `notices` (§13.6), to which every process that tells news (the lease's loop for driven work, the shell for its conversations) submits facts in one transaction, each teller with **its own journal cursor**, and each piece of news keyed by its occurrence (§4.5; O-12, X2-4.3) |
| The machine log | the machine's diagnostics, its lifecycle lines carrying the events' codes; its twin writers stay twins |
| The event file, the lineage, the ask's channel | stores: the conversation's record (D189 point 1), the child's binding (C1a), D167's and D188's messages, which no event replaces |
| Hook points | `session/ended` and `state/changed` projections; `work/event` the outside projection; `quest/consider`, `work/land`, `work/step`, `work/perform`, `work/watch` and `work/state` calls a plugin answers, which stay |
| The chain | internal wakes over the same vocabulary; ranks 3 and 4, declared and bound nowhere, are not bound because they exist (X): removed by row s's note unless a row by then reads them |
| The quest log | the quests' part of the journal: its insert mints `quest.<op>` |
| Presses told, `ReopenTelling`, `HeavyTold`, `AskMessages` | presses told stay the terminal's channel into the loop; the others become events where they tell news and stay where they tell an agent |
| EVENTHOOK1's relay | an outside projection of `work/event`, as designed |

### 4.4 The readers

| Reader | May read | Never |
|---|---|---|
| This machine's page | every slice, allow-listed, a machine path only in a desktop-only projection; the notices; command answers | a credential, a role key, a raw event's payload |
| The person's notifications | the news projection, from one table; the notify switch gates news alike, and a Confirm waiting is a wait on the person, not news, and stays ungated (D196) | a press the person made, told back to them (`Attention.cs:124-127`) |
| A plugin | `work/event` frames; `session/ended`'s frame as today (D101's fields), with `reason` and `class` added; `state/changed`; `quest/consider` | `said`, a key |
| An outside agent's session | `work/event` frames through the relay: ids, codes, counts, times | a name, a word, a path, an account; an event is no authority to act |
| The terminal | the same .NET judges over the pipe (D182 point 6), English from the codes | another home's records |
| A teammate's mirror | the records that travel, their travelling fields (`session-record-fields.md`) | the journal, move records, the work, the role's fields, `said`, the words' settlements and the keyed opens, the lineage, notices, the log, the role catalogue |

Never leaves the machine: the journal, every machine event but a relayed frame, move records, the words' settlements and the keyed opens, notices, the event file, the lineage, the
log, `said`, a participant's binding, every account's name, every path, the role catalogue.

### 4.5 Durability and loss

| What | Kept | A restart | How a reader knows it missed something |
|---|---|---|---|
| Records, the journal | durable | nothing lost that the journal still holds | a cursor below the journal's floor, or another store identity: the reader reads its records whole and says its history is incomplete since its cursor (X) |
| News | its said-set durable in the desktop store (`news_said`, §13.3), kept apart from the visible notices so a prune or a dismissal never forgets what was told, bounded by its horizon, one journal cursor per teller beside it (`journal_cursors`) | resumes from its cursors | the floor |
| Machine events | memory, one run | a new `run`, `run.started` | a new `run`, a `seq` gap, `missed` (EVENTHOOK1 §4.4) |
| Slices | memory, rebuilt from the truth | a new epoch | the epoch and the manifest (D182 point 3) |
| Move records | durable, in the desktop store | taken up by their executor, claimed and fenced (§3.3) | — |
| Held answers, `UNDER_WAY`, the confirmations' lists, the hook queue, held calls | memory | rebuilt or empty; a held call ends with its process, which holds it in memory alone (`Modules/Loop/HeldCallAnnouncements.cs:22`) | the read asked again; pipe-waiting keeps its own record |

**One rule for a first read** (survey §3.4 row 6): the baseline gives way to the said-set. A piece of news is told **at most once**: its key is
claimed in the said-set, durably, before the balloon or the toast is shown, so a crash between the claim and the showing loses that one
announcement and never repeats it, while the wait it told of stays where the person reads waits (*What needs you*, judged from records); one
claimed before is never told again, so reopening the app replays no balloon (X; X-4.3). A park made just before a restart, never claimed, is told
once after it (today dropped by the first look's baseline, `Attention.cs:224`; `SessionLog.cs:38-41`). A held call is told once while its owning
process lives; a restart of that process ends the hold and tells nothing for it (X-1.2). Only a home with no said-set yet, the first run of the
build that adds it, takes what stands as said, and so does a teller telling for the first time on a home, its cursor starting at the journal's
newest position. A claim the store refuses shows nothing, which keeps it at most once.

**A key is an occurrence, and forgetting one never tells it again** (round 3, X2-4.3). A piece of news is keyed by its occurrence, never by its
subject alone, and **one occurrence has one key whichever path finds it** (round 4, X3-3.6). Round 3 keyed a record event by its journal
position and a reconciliation below the floor by the record's `updated`, so after a trim a second teller could claim the second key for an end
the first had told, and `updated` moves on a note-only write besides (`Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:886-890`). Round 4 keyed it by the record's
transition number, kept by trigger in `session_transitions`, so that both paths computed one key; round 5 withdraws that table and the
reconciliation path's news with it (§19.6): **an occurrence's key is the durable id it was minted with, and one path tells it.** A record event is
keyed by the store's identity and its journal `position` (round 6, *O5-F9*), so an end, a reopen and an end are two occurrences and a position
of another store is never taken for this one's; a teller whose cursor is below the floor reads its records
whole for state, tells nothing of the gap and says its history is incomplete, the waits staying in *What needs you*. A conversation's turn
answers with no change of its record (`Chat/ChatRunner.cs:2893`, `:3824-3833`), so its answer is keyed by its session, the shell's process run
(its `store_runs` id, §13.3) and the moment its queue recorded the turn's end (`ChatQueue.LastTurnEnded`, set at `Chat/ChatTurns.cs:1276` and
read by the answer's judgement at `Chat/ChatRunner.cs:1673`), and told by the shell that runs it, a restart ending the run as today and telling
nothing for it (X4-3.3). Round 5 keyed it by the `seq` of its `turn` event, which is not durable across writers: `SessionEvents` caches the
sequence per instance and locks that instance alone (`Driver/Sessions/Events/SessionEventLog.cs:30`, `:45`), so two shells alternating on one
conversation can number two events alike, and `Keep` lets a failed append pass while the conversation goes on (`:84-94`); the run and the
moment depend on neither. **Its loss case** (round 7, *C6-1.8*: round 6 said only a clock stepped back could repeat a moment): the moment is
`DateTimeOffset.UtcNow` read as the turn ends (`Chat/ChatTurns.cs:1276`), which promises no unique value, so two turns of one session in one
run ending within one reading of the clock, or a clock stepped back, give one key, and the later answer's telling is dropped, never a telling
repeated; row m proves it with a held clock giving two turns one reading (round 6, *C5-1.6*, *O5-F9*). A Confirm keeps its press id, a held call its request
within its process's run, an account's wait the account and its cool-off's `until`, and the look's news its code and subject. The said-set and the news a
teller may tell share **one horizon**: a teller tells only occurrences whose moment is later than both the notices' week ago (`Notices.cs:32-35`)
and the moment of the oldest key the said-set still holds, and the said-set forgets a key only once its moment is outside the week or its own
count (10,000, oldest first, trimmed by its own statement) pushes it out, which raises the horizon with it. So no forgotten key's occurrence is
ever eligible again; the 200 the notices keep bound what is shown, never what was said. Row m-iv proves more than 200 eligible pieces of news at
once, a key expiring with the horizon, a new teller, a conversation answering three times with no change of its record, the same with two
shells running its turns one after the other and with an event append failing between them (*C5-1.6*), two turns ending at one reading of a
held clock, the second's telling dropped and none repeated (*C6-1.8*), and a teller below the
floor telling none of the gap and saying so, none told twice. The journal's
retention is not EVENTHOOK1's delivery: its relay keeps no replay and says its losses (`docs/2026-10-24-event-hook-design.md` §4.4), and moving
its session kinds onto the journal lets a restart of the lease's loop re-read what the journal still holds, never replay what a plugin missed
(X-4.4).

### 5.2 Records and owners

Every record of the survey's §4 keeps its owner unless this table or §13.6 says otherwise; *older* says what a build from before reads. Where a
record moves from a JSON file into the desktop store, §13.6 names its row and §13.5 what an older build does with it.

| Record | Owner after | Travels | An older build |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sessions` with `said`, the marks, `from`, `entry`, `limited`, the actor's fields | the service, unchanged; the work read, never stored; `actor` naming the seeded role of its work (§2.7) | as today; the work, `actor` and `from` never | reads it as today |
| `session_opens`, the keyed open and the words it moved (new, row d-i; round 3's `opens` field on the record, withdrawn in round 4, X3-3.3) | the service, written in the open's own exclusive step, the open's request carrying the key and its answer the transfer; a table, since a repeated open must be answered after its words leave `said`; the brief §9's one exception, its uniqueness the open's own statement; a row deleted with its record left by trigger, and kept as `gone` where its opened record is deleted first (round 5, §19.5; round 6, *C5-1.5*) | never | never reads it, and opens as today: a lost open against it is held (§3.3); its delete moves the rows through the trigger |
| `said_settled`, a word's settlement after it leaves `said` (new, row p; round 3's receipts in the desktop store, withdrawn in round 4, X3-3.2) | the service, written by a trigger in the statement that takes the word (round 5, §19.2), one row per record left, word id and key, trimmed at seven days by time alone, read by key over the line's records, back along `from` and forward to the head, inside each keeping door's step before any refusal, the supervisor's excepted, a key the line has settled, or from d-ii one waiting at its head, answered `Settled`, never a word, to a client that asks, and a key waiting on a record the line has left answered by the door as it stands on the record named (§3.4 rule 5; round 6, *C5-1.2*, *O5-F7*; round 7, *C6-1.2*, *C6-1.3*, *O6-F1*; round 8, *C7-1.1*, *O7-F12*; *O8-F1*); it says the word left `said`, never that it was delivered, which the move's own `sent` tells (*C5-1.4*, *C6-1.4*) | never | never reads it; its doors answer a key only while its word waits, as today; its own takes settle through the trigger |
| `session_transitions`, a record's transition number (round 4, X3-3.6) | **withdrawn in round 5** with the reconciliation path's news, the journal's position being the occurrence's one key (§4.5, §19.6) | — | — |
| The participants' bindings (new home of `<s>.harness.json`, row z-iii): the kept conversation, the account picked and whether the person picked it (row a-ii), the role taken and its revision (row x) | the desktop store, the Participants subdomain (§13.3, §14), each writer updating its own columns by name | never | the file set aside at the import, so a build from before the store finds none and falls back to a carry-on, never a stale conversation (§13.5; O3-6) |
| `event_journal` (new, row k) | the service, by trigger, in the service's store | never | never reads it; its own writes mint rows through the triggers, which bound it |

## 6. The names

None of these is a new word on the page except a role's, which the person sees and sets. They name code, records and prose.

| Concept | en | zh | On the page | Code | Retires |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fabric | *Fabric*, the three concepts together, in prose (D173) | none | never | none | — |
| participant | *participant* in prose | none until it reaches the page | never | `Participant`, `Participant.Of`, `RuntimeBinding` | — |

**The role is 角色, in both its shapes** (round 4, the steward's decision on both readers' reads; O3-15, X3-5.1). A role is one concept in two
shapes: the design makes D205's actor and D208's appointed role one record, `Role`, read by one catalogue and one judge, and the AI screen's
actor rows are the catalogue's working roles (§2.7). So it takes one Chinese word, 角色, which seven shipped strings say for it, both screens'
titles among them: `zh/settings.ai.json:28`, `zh/work.json:2`, `zh/person.json:123`, `zh/agents.json:121`, `zh/note.json:10`,
`zh/settings.daoris.json:108` and `:139`. **Ten more say 职责 for the appointed role** (O4-2), the supervisor's and the steward's appointing flow:
`zh/projects.json:393`, `:398`, `:399`, `:402`, `:404`, `:413`, `:414`, `:415`, `:416` and `zh/settings.daoris.json:179`; round 4 said every
shipped string already said 角色, which was wrong, and with either word some shipped strings change. Row u makes those ten 角色. Where the
appointed shape must be said, **任命的角色** says it, as `zh/settings.daoris.json:139` already does (你任命的角色); a working role needs no
qualifier, being what the page has called 角色 since D205. **职务 is withdrawn**, with its
reason: round 3 gave the role 职务 and left 角色 the actor's so that *the configured position and the staffing screen* read apart, which
separates two screen titles, not two concepts, and would show one record under two Chinese words on two screens; it would also retire 角色
from all seven strings and rewrite the glossary's *actor* entry (`glossary.json:475`), whose avoid list already refuses 岗位, a word close to
职务. Codex's read agrees that the role is one concept and would say 职务 for it; the steward keeps the word the page has. *Role* enters the
glossary with its reasons (D116; row u): *role*, 角色, *a configured position Daoris fills: a working role takes work, with its ordered choices
of agent, model and effort (D205's actor); an appointed role holds acts the owner appoints it to (D208, D209), 任命的角色 where that must be
said*, its avoid list 职务, 岗位, 演员, 人设, 执行者 and 职责, its match covering the labels *Actor*, *Actors* and *Roles*
(`work.actor.title`, `settings.ai.actors.title`, `settings.daoris.label.roles`), where *actor*'s match covers `^Actor$` alone today. *Actor* cannot keep 角色
beside it, since `names:check` records two terms sharing a Chinese name as a malformed glossary and exits 2
(`src/Daoris.Web/scripts/names-check.mjs:118-121`, `:299-302`): its entry becomes `use: "role"`, as *request*'s points at *quest*, saying that
*actor* is a code and prose word for a working role's staffing and the page says the role (O4-2). **Ten page strings change**, row u's, and no
English one. Code names that change:
`ResumeChoice.Waits` read through `LineAccount` (row a-i); `HoldsSlot` over the work (row c); `SessionRole` (row r); `LimitBox` read as a move
(row f), its file moved in row v; `AttentionEvent` a projection of `DaorisEvent` (row m); and the design's own types named apart from their
subdomains' namespaces, `LineCourse.Decide`, `Participant.Of` and `ParticipantWork.Of` (O3-9). Only in prose: Fabric, participant,
capability, line of work, move, event.
