## 8. What is the owner's

Nothing built from round 4, in either part, needs D191 point 3's answer. It changes no direction: the owner's FOUNDATION1 direction asks for exactly this
structure, and the owner's directions on roles (§2.7), on SQLite (§13) and on the driver's subdomains (§14) are taken as given, two of their
lines deferred to later designs by round 5's cuts, each listed below for the owner (round 6, *O5-F13*), with the line
between JSON and SQLite and the map's names and grouping the steward's by the owner's leave. It spends nothing beyond development's own runs
and what the owner already decided: LIMITSWITCH1 and LIMITSWITCH2, built and absorbed by rows a-i and a-iii, move a driven record's, a
repository conversation's and a closed quest's session's words to another ready account where the own account cools, which LIMITSWITCH1's decision covers in its own words (*words to an ended record whose
own account is cooling no longer wait … while another account of the line's choices is ready*); and an account the person named stays pinned
(§3.2), so no account is spent that the owner did not choose. **One reading the owner may overrule** (O2-7): that *an ended record* includes a
closed quest's session. The steward reads it so on the evidence of the case the owner reported (§3.2 step 2) and records it as amending D137
point 4 for the cooling case alone, a dated note on D137; LIMITSWITCH2 built it on the same reading and merged, and row a-iii absorbs it; the
owner's word otherwise puts the case back to the wait before LIMITSWITCH2, a row of its own. It needs no sign-in, publishes, pushes and rewrites nothing, and loses none of the owner's data: the journal is a new history the
records do not hold, bounded by its own trim, whose loss is said (§4.1); a JSON file the store imports is kept as its backup, or set aside under
its import's receipt, never deleted (§13.5); an old box or `held-words.json` is retired only once no build reads it and every word in it is on
`said`, which row v proves. Enabling a plugin that tells outside programs, appointing a role and changing a role's authority stay the owner's
existing doors; no row turns one on.

**Two open questions for the owner**, cut from row a by round 2 (O-1), each a decision of its own; until each is answered the code keeps today's
answer and no row builds it:

1. **Ask Daoris whose own account cools**, outside a limit: today its words wait for that account (D137 §2.2); it goes on elsewhere only at a
   limit, among its agent's accounts and to another agent only with none of its agent's left (D205 §3.5(e), *the one exception*,
   `HandOver.cs:52-57`; `LimitMove.cs:72`, `:85-88`). Should its words go on now in a new part of its room on another ready account, as a
   repository conversation's now do? Yes spends another of the owner's accounts on Ask Daoris's conversation and opens a new part in the room's
   history; no keeps today's wait, said in D206 §3.7's words. Read D205 §3.5(e) and this design's §3.2 step 2.
2. **A conversation whose own account is signed out, refused or gone** (*kept* and *outside the set* are a driven record's holds alone,
   `ResumeAccount.cs:31-36`): today it is `CannotGoOn`, the person offered *Start a conversation with these words*, since nothing carries a
   chat's words on by itself (`ChatGoOn.cs:566-569`, `:581`; D137 point 4). Should it go on by itself in a briefed part on another ready
   account, as a driven record does (D137 point 7)? Yes spends another account without the person's press and moves a conversation the person
   may mean to repair; no keeps today's door. Read D137 points 4 and 7 and this design's §3.2 step 3.

Near the line, and named so the steward can check them: rows q, r, t, v, w-0, y-i and y-ii grant or refuse authority (who may start, call and
act) or delete records, and rows z-ii, z-iv and z-xi rehome authority records (the configuration's `access`, `drivable`, `trees` and `standing`;
the appointments and the standing confirmation's grant; the permissions, the configured access and the plugin approvals, the GitDoor's grants
among them) and z-ix the records a removal follows, so each is built only after both makers have read it (D191's note of 2026-10-24; O3-14);
row z-ii's import holds a change of authority for the person's press; and every one of them is Part B's, waiting for its own rounds. Part A
grants, refuses and deletes nothing. Round 5 proposed w-0, t, y-ii, z-ii's general settings, z-iv, z-ix and z-xi for later designs (§19.15),
and both makers' reads of round 5 said yes to both cuts, which leaves q, r, v, w-s and y-i the ⚖ rows of Part B, w-s being z-ii's `actors` part
kept by the steward's change.

**Two deferrals the cuts make, the steward's, for the owner to overrule** (round 6, *O5-F13*; *C5-2.6*). Each defers a line of the owner's
directions to a later design rather than changing it, and the owner's word puts either back into Part B as rows of its own:
1. **The brief's §8, *the two appointed roles seeded into configuration*.** In Part B the catalogue holds working roles alone; the supervisor and
   the steward stay in code, as today, until the design read when the first appointment is offered (§19.9), since this build offers none
   (`Modules/Platform/StandingConfirmation.cs:314`, `:330`, `:336`). Nothing a person can do changes.
2. **The brief's §9, *the appointments and every setting `driver.json` holds today in the store, `driver.json` the export and import alone*.**
   In Part B the store holds the Fabric's records Part B's protocols read, the bindings, the moves, the notices and every role's staffing (row
   w-s); the appointments, the general settings and the other families stay files until the later design of the store's families, D50 standing
   for every key but the staffing. The brief's §9 carries a dated note of this.

## 10. The rows, in build order

Each row is its own branch, lands with every gate of the brief §6 green and its area's suites run whole, leaves a person who sets nothing seeing
Daoris work as today except where its row names a decided change, and adds its own proof. A row that introduces a concept reads it beside today's
mechanisms and moves no reader; a row that moves readers moves one path or channel, the old surface its adapter, the census failing on a path that
neither moved nor is named. **Rows marked ⚖ grant or refuse authority, or delete, and are built only after both makers' reads** (D191's note of
2026-10-24). The rows are filed in `TASKS.md` part by part: Part A's when Part A is accepted, Part B's when Part B is; until then FOUNDATION1
points here. Both makers' reads of round 4 accept Part A after named changes, which are made (§17, §18), so Part A waits only for the
steward's acceptance; its first row, a-i, accepted alone on the Opus reader's read and built on a branch held off main until Codex's read
(D191's note of 2026-10-24), takes §18's corrected description of where the walk is asked (the Standing). *Row a-i* in this design is
FOUNDATION1a-i, and so on. **The order** (round 6, *O5-F12*; round 4's, which placed z-ii, z-iv and w-0 before w and t and y-ii before v, is
withdrawn with round 5's cuts): Part A's rows a-i, b, c, mod-0 and u, then the
driver's moves mod-i to mod-xxi (§14.6), bottom up, each when no open branch holds the files it moves, their cuts after each; Part B's rows z-i
(after mod-ii, whose Store folder `DesktopStore` is written in), z-iii, a-ii, a-iii, p, d-i, d-ii, e, f-i to f-vi, g, h, i, o, j, w-s, w, x, y-i,
k, z-v, l-i to l-v, m-i to m-v, n, q, r, s and v, once Part B is accepted, and any Part B row whose files a move of Part A holds waits for that
move, rows e, f-i and h after mod-xiv and its port's cut (§19.14). The rows proposed for later designs (w-0, t, y-ii, z-ii's general settings,
z-iv and z-vi to z-xiv) are ordered by those designs; the namespace rows last
(§14.6). Row p comes before row d as well as f (X2-5.1), and row d-i, the keyed open, before d-ii and every row that moves words (O3-12,
X3-6.1), so that each lands on acknowledged attempts and an idempotent open; p adds `GET /api/protocol` with its `settled` mark, d-i adds
`opens` 1, d-ii raises `opens` to 2 and adds `head`, and k adds `journal` (§19.12; round 6, *C5-3.2*, *C5-1.12*), so each host says exactly
which of them it keeps. The Opus reader's precondition for row a-i, that
LIMITSWITCH1 merge onto a main that had moved under its branch (O's §4), is met: it merged at `a85004bd0`; and LIMITSWITCH2 landed its parts of
rows a-i and a-iii ahead of them, merged at `db883b1cc` (§5.1).

### Part A: the rows that only reorganise

Part A is a-i, b, c, mod-0, u and the driver's moves mod-i to mod-xxi with their cuts (§14.6). Each stands on today's code and records and on
the other Part A rows alone, never on a Part B contract (the Standing says why, row by row), and each lands behind today's tests with its own
proof. Three change what a person sees or gets, each already decided (the Standing; O4-2, O4-8): row b's repair, row c's partner and row u's ten
Chinese strings.

**FOUNDATION1a-i — one account rule, over LIMITSWITCH1 and LIMITSWITCH2 as built** (driver; on LIMITSWITCH1 and LIMITSWITCH2, merged; cut back
in round 3 to the cooling rule for driven records and conversations, its earliest reset and its hold, O2-4, O2-5; in round 4 to a reorganisation
alone, Part A's; with round 4's named changes, each caller's earliest-reset set read as today and the walk asked only where today's code asks it,
O4-3, O4-4; accepted alone on the Opus reader's read of round 4 and built on a branch held off main until Codex's read, D191's note of
2026-10-24; with Codex's named changes, the walk's places named in full, X4-1.1, X4-4.1).
- *What.* `LineAccount.Decide(own hold, trigger, constraint, line kind, walk) → Resume | Elsewhere(selection, why) | Wait(earliest, accounts, set) |
  Hold(reason, repair)`, pure, beside `ResumeAccount` in `Accounts/Rotation/`, which the map places in Agents with the roster that reads it (§14.2;
  O3-8): §3.2's rule as LIMITSWITCH1 and LIMITSWITCH2 built it. **The walk is asked only where today's code asks it** (O4-4; X4-1.1, X4-4.1):
  `walk` is a lazy input, never handed up front, since a walk counts the start it chooses (`SelectAsync`, `HarnessRoster.cs:894-911`), so a
  walk fed where today's code makes none would walk and count starts it does not; where a caller cannot hand a lazy walk, `Decide` runs twice,
  the second time with the walk's answer where the first asked for it. Today the rule's walk is asked in `HarnessRoster.ResumeAsync` (`:975`)
  alone, at three places, each kept as it stands. (i) **The no-toolchain branch** (`:981-984`): an adapter with no accounts walks as a start
  does, `SelectAsync` with no account chosen, counted whatever `walks`, `switches` and `walkCounts` say, and resumes no own account. (ii) **The
  switch's walk** (`:1022-1032`): the own account cooling, the person having chosen no new session and no limit having ended the record, under
  `switches`, whatever `walks` says, counted by `walkCounts`; its answer taken where it is allowed (`:1033`), its cooling compared with the own reset where it is not
  (`:1036-1043`). (iii) **Its own `ElsewhereAsync`** (`:1063-1075`), which walks with no account chosen, counted, only where `walks` is true, and
  otherwise walks and counts nothing and answers a refusal saying what held the own account (`:1067-1069`); it is asked from three branches: the
  own account not ready outside the cooling hold of (ii) (`:1052`: cooling where the person chose a new session or a limit ended the record, or
  refused, signed out, gone, outside the set or kept for conversations); the own account ready but not offering the asked model, the walk
  passing it (`:1053`, `:1065`); and the own account's named selection answering signed out (`:1058-1059`). Beside the walk, two steps stay the
  roster's and count as today: a limited record's own account, ready by its cool-off, asked its maker's
  reading before the rule reads the own hold (`:1015-1020`), a probe that counts no start; and the own account asked by name where it is ready,
  as a pick is (`ChooseAsync`, `:1057`), counted. Today's callers ask `walks` true at the driven go-on (`Loop/Driver.cs:1990-1993`) and false at
  the driven limit resume (`:2000-2001`), the chat's go-on (`ChatGoOn.cs:490-492`) and Ask Daoris's (`:731`). The moves' own walks are their
  executors', not the rule's, and stay where they are: the live limit move's (`Chat/LimitMove.cs:85-86`) and its `ElsewhereAsync` over the
  role's later choices (`:92`, `:486-511`), which round 4's named change cited as the rule's where the read meant the roster's own, and the
  ended move's one walk (`:247-248`). **The constraint** is none, a handed quest's named account (first, then
  its agent's walk, `HarnessRoster.cs:923-934`), or a hand-over press's account opened as a pick (*a pick is the one account, never moved*,
  `:887`); no record keeps an account pick until row a-ii. Read by `ResumeChoice` (`Waits` and LIMITSWITCH1's `Switched`) and the roster's resume
  (`HarnessRoster.ResumeAsync`, `:975`), whose inline twin (`:1022`) and `switches` and `walkCounts` parameters become one call; by the chat's
  go-on (`ChatGoOn.SwitchableAsync`, `ChatGoOn.cs:489-492`, read at `:614-645`) and the wake (`LimitWaits.cs:156-157`, `:194-220`). Executors
  unchanged: a repository conversation's cooling *elsewhere* goes on through the ended move's body (`switching`), among its agent's accounts, its
  new part continuing the old by `retry`, waiting for the earliest reset of any where none of them is ready (O2-13), the brief and the waiting
  words handed once; a closed quest's session through `GoOnLostAsync` with the reason `account`, a headless host waiting; all as LIMITSWITCH2
  built it. The closed quest's hold (`Driver.cs:2381-2384`) reads the rule through `LineAccount` as LIMITSWITCH2's switch does. Ask Daoris's
  go-on (`ChatGoOn.cs:723-746`, its resume at `:730-733`) reads the rule and keeps its wait; a picked conversation walks its agent's accounts as
  LIMITSWITCH2 built it, since the record keeps no pick (round 3 had it keep its own account, which is a-ii's recorded pick, Part B's); a
  conversation's other `CannotGoOn` cases are unchanged (§8). **The earliest reset is read as today, each caller's own set** (O4-3), behind one
  function, `LineAccount.Earliest(set, …)`, whose `set` names which it reads: the walk's states (`Unready`, `HarnessRoster.cs:1766`); the
  switch's walk against the own reset (`:1032-1048`), the own account's included where it may resume (O2-4); or, for a limited conversation's

  The one reset over exactly the accounts a line may take, ineligible ones adding none, is row a-iii's, Part B's, with §7 row 3, since it reads
  step 4's set, which a-ii, a-iii and LIMITRESUME1e define. Where none has a reset, the hold that names the repair, which is

  answer, every walk and every counted start is today's, read through one call. It reads no recorded pick, no settled selection, no move record
  and no store.
- *Census.* Every caller of `ResumeAsync`, `CoolingOf`, `CoolingsOf` and `ResumeChoice.Waits` reads `LineAccount` or is named *not yet moved*,
  and every walk it asks is one today's caller asks, at one of the three places above, each named in the census with its branches (X4-1.1).
- *Proof.* `LineAccountTests`: own (ready, near, cooling, signed out, refused, missing, outside, kept, not offered) × trigger × constraint (none,

  counted; on and judging only) × the person's new session and a limit's end (X4-1.1), so each of the three places and each branch into

**FOUNDATION1b — a word keeps its identity wherever it is held** (modules, driver; any time, a day).
- *What.* The shell's held word carries its producer's key, level, files and display choice (`SessionWords.cs:179`, `:464`), kept in
  `held-words.json` additively, so an older shell reads text, files and door as before (`:915-935`), whose reader takes each field by name
  (`:981-996`); the record path passes the producer's key (`SayAsync(id, word.Text, word.Files, ct)` at `:701`, `:782`), and so does the headless
  fallback (`SessionSay.cs:197`). **So does the forward** (X4-1.2): a held word passed to the conversation its record went on in
  (`PassAsync`, `SessionWords.cs:838-855`) is said there through the shell's own `SayAsync` (`:843`) with neither its key, its level nor its
  display choice today, so it lands keyless and at the say's default level (`MessageLevel.Urgent`, `:382`); it passes all three, as the first
  say did, the display choice reaching a running conversation's turns and never the record path's `choice`. The display choice never becomes
  `SaidWord.Choice`: the record path passes no `choice`, since the say door refuses a choice from any author but the supervisor and then keeps
  nothing (`SessionLedger.cs:901-907`).
- *The repair* (O4-8). A producer's retry after a shell restart keeps one word where today it keeps two, and so does a retry while a forwarded
  word waits on its successor: the guarantee D137 and D188 decided, so the row asks no new decision. *Delivers once* across a restart holds while
  the word still waits on `said` (`SessionLedger.cs:956`); after a take it is row p's settlement.
- *Census.* Every `SayAsync` outside tests passes a key or is named as a legacy case, `PassAsync`'s among them (X4-1.2).
- *Proof.* A producer's retry keeps one word; a restart keeps key, level, files and display choice; an ordinary display choice never reads as the
  supervisor's and the word is still kept, the record path passing no `choice` (`SessionLedger.cs:901-907`); a Process case restarts the shell
  while a record winds up, before the take, and delivers once, the case after the take being row p's; **a hold restarted and then forwarded to
  a live successor keeps its key, level and display choice there, and a producer's retry said to that successor while the word waits there keeps
  one word** (X4-1.2);
  `SessionWordsTerminalTests`, `SessionWordsWaitingTests`, `SessionLedgerTests` and `SessionStoreTests` whole.

**FOUNDATION1mod-0 — the structure gate** (driver tests, and the modules' and the service's tests that read driver sources by path; §14.5).
- *What.* `StructureTests` and `fixtures/structure.json` hold the twenty-one subdomains in their order, each one's folder and its allowed
  `Domain/`, **every file of the driver placed** in one subdomain (O4-5), and the subdomain, direction, purity, Loop, mirror and global-using
  rules, today's edges against the order named as *not yet cut*, **each an exact edge**, a file and the type it names, listed under its edge
  family and the subdomain row after which it is cut, never by a row id the design does not have yet (O4-6), so a family admits only the edges it
  lists and a new crossing of it, a new file or a new type, fails (X4-1.4); today's test folders listed as not yet moved, so the mirror rule
  (`StructureCheck.cs:183-213`) passes over them, each mod row moving its subdomain's tests (O4-5); new code written in place from here on. The
  direction rule reads **every type reference** (X3-4.1) by **Roslyn's semantic model** (X4-1.4, the stricter of the two ways O4-5 offered): the
  tests build one compilation of every driver source over the base library, which is all the driver references, and take each name that binds to
  a type a driver file declares as an edge to that file's subdomain, whatever its spelling: plain, namespace-qualified (`Daoris.Driver.<Name>`,
  `global::Daoris.Driver.<Name>`), an alias's target, a nested type's alias among them, or a `using static` target. So
  `Daoris.Driver.HolderKey.Taken` (`Sessions/SessionsCommand.cs:185`) and `using static Daoris.Driver.X;` (`Accounts/AccountCooling.cs:5`,
  `Agents/Tools.cs:6`), which today's identifier scan skips with every name that follows a `.` (`StructureCheck.cs:385`), are edges. **A
  member's occurrence binds to the member, never to a type**, so `DaorisHome`'s `HomeEntry` members named like higher types
  (`Platform/DaorisHome.cs:326`, `:352`, `:354`, `:362`, `:423-427`) count none, told by the symbol and never by the spelling, and a file that
  declares such a member and also names the type of that name keeps the type's edge. **Every declaration is read as the compiler reads it**:
  today's pattern takes `record struct` and `record class` as a type named `struct` or `class`, which its table drops, so such a type is
  declared nowhere (`StructureCheck.cs:238`, `:388`; `Plugins/BoundedLines.cs:9` declares one). A comment or a string names nothing, a `cref`
  included, as today's scan removes them. The tests take Roslyn's C# package, `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp`, pinned in the desktop's central
  package versions for the driver's tests alone; the driver, the GitDoor and every project that ships take none, so the driver's *zero package
  dependencies* (`src/Daoris.Desktop/Directory.Packages.props:6-9`) stands; the compile's time in the fast half is measured and said in the
  row. **Edges into Loop** (O4-1): no exception names an edge into a file in `Loop/`'s folder, so today's absolute
  rule (`StructureCheck.cs:216-217`) holds from this row on and no move takes a class out of Loop that names `LoopContext`; an edge into a file the
  map places in Loop that still sits outside its folder (the chat runner's core and the wiring view, until mod-xxi) is a census exception named
  with its cut, and every such cut lands before mod-xxi moves the file in, so the rule is absolute over all of Loop after it. The fourteen sites
  that read driver sources by folder path (§14.6) are made name-based here, before the first move, as D166 point 4 orders (O4-7). Every namespace
  stays `Daoris.Driver` (D166 point 4). It amends D166 point 1's fifteen modules, Brainstorms the fifteenth by D176 point 1
  (`Driver.Tests/StructureTests.cs:189-191`), by the twenty-one subdomains, a dated note on each (O3-10).
- *Proof.* The gate passes over the tree with today's exceptions listed and fails on a planted upward type named plainly, fully qualified (as
  `Driver/Sessions/SessionsCommand.cs:185` names one today), through `global::`, through a namespace alias, through a nested type's alias and
  through a static import; passes a planted member named like a higher type, and fails on a planted file that declares such a member and also
  names the higher type as a type (X4-1.4); reads a planted `record struct` and a planted `record class` as declared, an edge to each failing
  where it points up (X4-1.4); fails on a planted edge of a listed family that its exceptions do not list (X4-1.4); fails on a file
  `structure.json` does not place; fails on a planted edge into a file in `Loop/`; a planted `Domain/` file naming `ServiceClient`; a planted rule
  in Loop; a planted global using; `StructureTests`' fixtures for each.

where they compile a moved file, the modules' tests and the service's Mcp tests run, and `StructureTests`' census shorter by the subdomain's
files and edges (O4-7); each proof a requirement its row's build demonstrates, none run by this design (X4-1's opening). **mod-xxi's proof** is the end
state (X4-1.26): the census with no upward exception left and no edge into Loop from outside it, the ten executors built from `LoopContext`
still in Loop.

### Part B: the rest, in order

Proposed, each row waiting for Part B's acceptance, read in rounds of its own. Round 5 (§19) settles the agenda round 4 left them and marks
the rows it proposes for later designs (§19.15): w-0, t, y-ii and z-iv, the appointed half of the catalogue; z-ii's general settings and z-vi
to z-xiv, the store's families no Part B protocol reads. z-ii's `actors` part stays, cut out as row w-s, so every role's staffing has one home
in `role_staffing` (§19.15). Without the proposed rows Part B's order is z-i, z-iii, a-ii, a-iii, p, d-i, d-ii, e, f-i to f-vi, g, h, i, o, j,
w-s, w, x, y-i, k, z-v, l-i to l-v, m-i to m-v, n, q, r, s, v, and w waits for w-s alone, no longer for z-ii, z-iv or w-0; rows e, f-i and h
land after mod-xiv and its port's cut (§19.14). Round 6 (§20) carries both reads of round 5 into the rows below: each row that lands a
trigger, an index or a read in the say door's step on `sessions` lands only within its limits (p, d-i, d-ii, k); each row writing in Course or
Participants keeps `StructureTests` whole in its proof (*C5-1.14*); a row that reads a concept beside today's, *read by nothing*, changes no
decision path until its adoption row (*C5-3.3*); and a row's proof that needs words moved by an open is d-ii's, the rows before it proving their
own steps over fixtures planted by the service's own statements (*C5-3.2*). Round 7 (§21) carries both reads of round 6 into them: p's
settlement keyed by the record left, the word's id and its key and read over the line's records with the key first (*C6-1.2*, *C6-1.3*,
*O6-F1*); d-i's open looking its key up first, refusing every move out of an ended state its open left, and a lost answer asked again only
after the marks are read again (*O6-F4*, *O6-F5*, *O6-F6*); d-ii's sites reading the destination's moved words, recording each word's event and
copying its files (*O6-F2*, *O6-F8*, *O6-F13*); the move's delivery told by its own `sent` from f-i, and a proof that needs a move that delivers
f-ii's (*C6-1.4*, *O6-F12*); and w-s moving the staffing's readers with its writers, fenced and baselined (*C6-1.5* to *C6-1.7*, *O6-F3*,
*O6-F9* to *O6-F11*). Round 8 (§22) carries both reads of round 7 into them: p's settled key answered `Settled`, a terminal answer with no word,
to a client that asks, its readers in the census and the supervisor's door left as today (*C7-1.1*, *O7-F12*); f-i's hold closed where the
course reads the destination (*O7-F11*); f-ii to f-iv's `sent` at the doors' real write returns and the take-up's own `lost` note (*O7-F4*,
*O7-F5*); and w-s smaller, the file the store's projection whole, one record lock and one check (*C7-1.2*, *O7-F1* to *O7-F3*, *O7-F6* to
*O7-F9*), its added-role case moved to w (*O7-F10*). The order is unchanged. Round 8's named changes (§23) carry both reads of round 8 into
them: p's `Settled` only for a key the line settled or, from d-ii, one waiting at its head, the pump's forward after a lost answer p's case and
an older build's unkeyed successor d-ii's (*O8-F1*); f-i's limit move made after a hold closes proven in h and i (*O8-F6*); f-iv's `sent` on
the protocol door too (*O8-F7*); f-ii's downgrade without the `lost` note (*O8-F8*); and w-s's seam of three strings, its file step's
baseline, the fence on every newer save, *Take it* as the found file showed, the lock's wait and the hot path's limit (*C8-1.1* to
*C8-1.3*, *O8-F2* to *O8-F5*). **One order is added** (*O8-F2*): w-s lands after Part A's mod-iii, mod-v and mod-xi moves and their cuts
(the settings' parser's and verbs' names of other subdomains' rule types, `Install names Roles`, and Roles' move), since its seam is
written against the record those cuts leave, and its `StructureTests` whole means no census entry added.

written against (*O8-F2*).

## 11. What stays unmeasured, and what this design's checks do not cover

- **The triggers' cost** on `sessions` at the install's write rate, and the journal's size at its bound: row k measures write latency and CPU against
  its landing limits. That every end writes a reason code in `note_parts` is read from the state's statement and the codes' list, not proven for
  every writer; one that writes none is told `reason` absent and class `unknown`.
- **The head's cost at the say door**: a chain of `from` reads per word, and the sessions table has no index on `from_session` or `quest`
  (`Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:504`, `:632`, `:762`; round 5 read it); row d-i adds both with the walk its keyed open reads, and row d-ii, which
  reads the walk at the say door, lands only with the say door's p95 within a tenth (§19.13), each branch measured: one probe for a word whose
  record has no successor, a second for a driven part's quest's last run (round 6, *C5-1.13*).
- **A hung executor and two shells on one home** (round 5, §19.1): how often a move is said `move.quiet`, measured by row f-i on the install.
- **Whether the course reproduces every live combination**: it is proven on the cases the paths' suites hold and on the census, not on every state a
  line reaches on the install.
- **The live limit move's word gap** (survey §2.4 row 3, *unverified* that it loses one): row d's first test.
- **News after a long downtime**: whether a cursor resuming after days tells too much at once; row m bounds it by the notices' week and measures it.
- **The settlement's and the move record's cost**, and how often an uncertain delivery happens: rows p and f measure them; the settlement's horizon
  is a decision (round 5, §19.2: seven days for a producer's retry, none for the attempt), trimmed by time alone with no count cap (round 6,
  *C5-1.2*), so its size is a week's takes, which row p measures on the install; a week far past that rate makes the table larger, never the
  horizon shorter.
- **Delivery's evidence across a crash** (round 7, *C6-1.4*, replacing round 6's reading of the `User` event, which the runner records before
  the write): a moved word is delivered where the move's own `sent` stands, written in the desktop store as its door's write returned and
  before the take; `sent` says the write to the agent returned, never that the agent read it, as the door's `sent` receipt says today
  (`Driver/Sessions/Domain/SessionEvents.cs:60-61`). A crash after the write returned and before `sent` reads the word uncertain or *left*, and
  a `sent` whose transaction fails reads it *left*: the move is held, the conservative reading, never a second delivery.
- **The settlement's line read** (round 7, *C6-1.3*, *O6-F1*): the walk back along `from` adds one probe by id per predecessor inside the say
  door's step, from row p, and the forward walk is d-ii's; neither is measured until p and d-ii land, each within its limit (§19.13).
- **`driver.json`'s staffing against an older writer** (round 8, *C7-1.2*, *C7-3.1*, *O7-F7*, replacing round 7's one window, which bounded
  only a paused newer writer): `KeepLock` is taken as stale a minute after its holder took it, however short the pause after its last read
  (`Driver/Platform/KeepLock.cs:38`, `:78`, `:120`), and an older save renames with no second look at it (`Driver/Platform/DriverConfig.cs:1018`,
  `:1027`). w-s's newer writers serialize on the staffing's record lock, which lives with its holder, never on `KeepLock`. Two windows are
  left, decided in 19.15 and named in w-s's proof: an older save renamed after a newer write leaves the file wider than the store until the
  next check (the next look of a newer loop holding the lease, else the next newer start or write), an older build's start in between acting
  on it and the newer build never; and an older save completed between a newer write's last look at its aged lock and its rename is lost, as
  any `driver.json` write is under CONFIGLOCK1, a turn-off in it then undone in both builds until the person makes it again. Nothing proves
  either closed. *(C8-1.2)* The second window is every newer save's of the home's `driver.json`, an unrelated setting's included, since each
  takes the same fence, its `KeepLock` read again just before its rename and the rename made only while the lock still holds its text; without
  the fence, an unrelated save paused after its re-read (`Driver/Platform/DriverConfig.cs:1018`) and renamed (`:1027`) after an older Intake-off
  save would lose that save outside any stated window, its staffing paths showing `written` again so the check found nothing.
- **The staffing's hot path** (*O8-F5*): after w-s every load of the home's `driver.json`, at each look (`Loop/DriverWatch.cs:719`), each
  conversation's `MachineConfig` (`Chat/ChatContext.cs:126`), the routes and the verbs, reads the store too, and each look gains the check; row
  w-s measures the look's time and a load's latency before and after on the install and lands only with each p95 within a tenth of before.
- **A moved word's `lost` note across a downgrade** (*O8-F8*): once d-ii records each moved word's `User` event under its id before the
  write, an older build's come-up settles a moved word attempted without `sent` with no `lost` note (`Conversations/GoOn/UncertainWords.cs:84-91`,
  `:97-98`), where today a move's joined event, with no id, leaves it one; the person learns of it when the newer build returns and holds the
  move, offering each of their *left* words for saying again. How often a downgrade meets a move at `delivering` is unmeasured.
- **An older client's keyed retry after a take** (round 8, *C7-1.1*): it sends no `settled`, so a newer host answers it as today's door does
  and it keeps a second word, as today, never an id of another record; how often it happens is unmeasured.
- **An older build beside an added role that does not read** (round 7, *C6-1.7*): an older build reads no added role, so where the newer
  build holds every start in such a record's scope, an older build starts a work that may not be off there on the seeded staffing; the works
  that may be off are off in it while the record does not read.
- **A turn's answer at one clock reading** (round 7, *C6-1.8*): two turns of one session in one run ending within one reading of
  `DateTimeOffset.UtcNow`, or a clock stepped back, share a key, and the later answer is not told; how often is unmeasured.
- **Whether lineage reaches Daoris over either harness's ACP door** (FABRIC-ACP1, C2b's), on which row r's child column waits.
- **Whether two processes append one session's event file**, and whether a shell not holding the lease reconciles its slices only at the manifest
  (survey §3.2, §3.4 row 7, *unverified*): the hub's rule assumes neither, and since round 6 no news key reads an event's `seq` (§4.5; *C5-1.6*).
- **How often each move happens on the install**, which sizes row f's recovery cases: read from the machine log's `session.moved` lines first.
- **A brainstorm voice resolved at the connector through its box** in time for its first call (*unverified*).
- **An older build's continuation of a record started on an added role**: answered by round 3 (§2.7: `actor` keeps the seeded id, O2-3), proven by
  row x's first case.
- **The store's cost** (round 3): a write's latency with `synchronous = FULL` and write-ahead logging, the busy waits between the shell, a headless
  loop and the verbs, the wake file's latency against today's file watches, and the time of each family's import on the install's home: row z-i
  and each family's row measure them. The move's claim rests on the lease's way of telling a live process, which is read (`HomeLease.cs:427-448`),
  and on no single-instance guard (round 4, O3-1). **The GitDoor's start with SQLite** on a remote act (round 4, O3-3; round 5, §19.10): row z-i
  measures it against today's door (about 0.25 s, `docs/decisions/D198.md:290-293`), its limit a p95 start no more than a tenth slower; until a
  door passes it no family the door reads moves into the store, and with §19.15's proposal none does in Part B, so Part B's door opens no store.
- **The map's census**: the edge counts of §14.1 are a measurement by type names with comments and strings removed, a lower bound of the
  dependencies (a type named only in a string or by reflection is missed, and so is a `record struct` or `record class`, which the scan's pattern
  declares nowhere, X4-1.4), and one of them over-counted (`DaorisHome`'s, §14.1; O3-8); row mod-0's census reads every type reference by Roslyn's
  semantic model, each exception an exact edge (O4-5; X4-1.4), and still misses a type named only in a string or by reflection.
- **The defects' classes** (survey §6): a judgement, which this order inherits.
- **The checks.** A document only: `doc-links`, `doc-system`, `doc-shapes`, `doc-duplicates`, `doc-budgets` and `orient-index --check` check its
  place, its links and the index, never its claims. The code was read, not run, on main at `92ac2bd7e` and `c8eccf30e` (one `src/`), at `2a08bde4a`
  for round 3 (whose `src/` is round 2's `29a066182`), at `e8f1aed4f` for round 4 (whose `src/` is round 3's `68a396c65` but for one test), and
  LIMITSWITCH1 on its branch at `3fdc6bbeb` and `adacd5fdc`, merged at `a85004bd0`; LIMITSWITCH2, merged at `db883b1cc` as round 3 was committed,
  was read from its note on D137 in round 3 and in its code in round 4, at the lines round 4 cites; EVENTHOOK1, COWORKVIEW1's routing half and SWITCHCTX1's carry are proposed, not built, and what this
  says of them is read from their documents at that commit. Round 5 read the code on main at `eced2688e` and round 6 at `6736d2db9`, each
  `src/` being `86f32f40c`'s unchanged; by round 6 EVENTHOOK1's rows 1a to 1c are accepted, and COWORKVIEW1's row a is in its
  round 5 (§5.1). Round 7 read the code on main at `abf03ea26`, which carries Part A's rows 1a-i, 1b, 1c, mod-0 and u; the lines those rows
  moved are re-cited in §21.3, and an earlier round's line elsewhere is its own round's commit's. Round 8 read it on main at `74725d604`, which
  carries row mod-i as well, and for every file both reads of round 7 cite its `src/` is `da419d196`'s unchanged (§22.4); a line round 8 adds
  is that commit's. Round 8's named changes (§23) read it on main at `26ef34d80`, which carries row mod-ii as well: its `src/` differs from
  `41a596671`'s, where both reads of round 8 were made, only by mod-ii: Store's seven files moved into `Store/` (`KeepLock.cs`,
  `RecordLock.cs` and `JsonKeeping.cs` among them, unchanged; `AtomicFile.cs` and `Json.cs` changed by its cut), its cut's two new Platform
  reads and the four Platform files that read through them, and the census and its fixture, so a line of a moved file is cited at its new path,
  the census's at `26ef34d80`, and every other line as round 8 and its reads cite it (§23.3).

## 13. Where state is kept: the desktop store

The owner's direction of 2026-10-25, after round 2 (the brief §9): as configuration and state grow complex they move from JSON files to
SQLite. The line between the two is the steward's, by the owner's leave (the brief §9): **SQLite holds what Daoris owns and more than one
reader or writer reads and sets; JSON stays only where something outside Daoris owns the format, where the file is a repository's reviewed
record, where it is read before the store opens, or where it is an export or a stream.** This section is the fourth area of the design, and
it is where round 3 makes the move's fencing and rollback choices Codex asked for (X2-1.1, X2-3.1), and where round 4 settles the claim by
process run, the stamp inside every writing transaction, the timings against D139's confirmation and the authority-bearing backups (§16), and
where round 5 settles what Part B's agenda left of them (§19) and proposes the families no Part B protocol reads for a later design (§19.15). The
whole of §13 is Part B's. The inventory below was read at `2a08bde4a`, every file's writers and readers by `file:line`.

### 13.1 Today

- **The desktop opens no SQLite.** No project under `src/Daoris.Desktop` references `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite`; the service does (10.0.12,
  `src/Daoris.Service/Directory.Packages.props:30`), and its one database, `data/knowledge.db` (`Core/Platform/DaorisHome.cs:338`), is already a
  file several processes open: the HTTP host and every session's connector (`Http/Program.cs:90-91`; `Mcp/Program.cs:78-79`), its index stamped
  by `user_version` and its quests by their own `quest_store_version` (`Core/Quests/Quests.cs:716-720`, `:941-965`; `compatible-builds.md`).
- **About sixty JSON files and families** sit in the home's `data/`, `profiles/` and `run/`, written by the driver library in the shell and in
  `daoris-driver`, by the modules in the shell, by the service and its connectors, by the GitDoor (the git launcher and remote helper, D198,
  which compiles fourteen of the driver library's files, `Daoris.Desktop.GitDoor.csproj:20-37`), by the launcher, and by the Node CLI. They are guarded three ways: a
  `KeepLock` sidecar (`<file>.lock`, `Driver/Platform/KeepLock.cs:17-38`, which every `JsonKeeping.Save` takes, `JsonKeeping.cs:72`), a
  `RecordLock` held handle (`run/locks/records/<name>.lock`, `RecordLock.cs:38-67`), or an in-process lock alone, so that `data/windows.json`,
  `data/reads.json`, `data/usage.json` and `data/sessions/held-words.json` can race between the shell and a `daoris-driver` (`AccountWindows.cs:98`;
  `AccountReads.cs:104`; `Accounts/Usage.cs:90`, `:169`; `Modules/Loop/SessionWords.cs:916` beside `SessionHomeFiles.cs:333-368`).
- **`data/driver.json` has many writers and no version stamp**: `DriverConfig.Save` under its `KeepLock` (`DriverConfig.cs:1009-1028`), reached
  from fourteen sites of `DriverChoicesCommand`, the shell's `DriverContext.Change` (`Modules/Bridge/DriverContext.cs:149-151`), the steward's
  acts (`StewardActRows.cs:418`, `:432`) and a dozen more, and a raw writer for the git pointers (`GitHostKeys.cs:75-98`); read on every tick
  (`DriverWatch.cs:1118`), by the headless host (`Host/Program.cs:864-865`) and by the GitDoor (`GitIdentityUse.cs:80-86`). An older build reads
  it by keeping keys it does not know (`DriverConfig.cs:918-928`, `:1057-1063`, held by `DriverConfigKeepsTests`).
- **Cross-process wakes are file watches**: `HomeWatch` watches `driver.json`, `cooling.json`, `harnesses.json`, `held-words.json`,
  `lines-moved.json`, the opinion gates, the notices and the proposals (`Driver/Platform/HomeWatch.cs:192-232`).
- **The home's lease is a file naming its holder** (`run/home.lease`: kind, pid, the process's start time, the home's id;
  `Driver/Platform/HomeLease.cs:307-320`), taken by a create-only move so one of two racing takers lands (`:337-343`), stale only once its pid is
  dead or belongs to a process started at another time (`:66-68`, `:165-173`), released only by the holder whose text it is (`:63`). The shell
  waits on it (`Modules/Loop/DriverLoop.cs:841`, `:1270-1310`), the headless loop takes or shares it (`Host/Program.cs:868-885`), and a verb
  beside a live instance holds nothing (`HomeLease.ForVerb`, `:219-225`).
- **The Node CLI** spawns `daoris-driver` for its management verbs (`src/Daoris.Cli/src/driverprogram.ts:27-30`, `:90-101`) and writes no
  `driver.json` since PUSH1f2b (`jsonkeep.ts:8`, `:68`); it reads `run/running.json` and `data/home.json` and writes `data/browser/settings.json`,
  `data/browser/favorites.json` and `profiles/remotes.json` itself (`service.ts:115`; `home.ts:393`; `browser.ts:80-216`; `remotemap.ts:143-185`).

### 13.2 The store and its owner

- **One file**, `data/desktop.db`, a row of D172's table of the home's entries, which has three twins (`Core/Platform/DaorisHome.cs:317-411`,
  `Driver/Platform/DaorisHome.cs`, the CLI's `home.ts:98`; `twins.md`), changed together in row z-i.
- **Its owner is the driver library**: one class, `DesktopStore`, in the Store subdomain (§14), is the only code that opens the file, and every
  family's table is read and written by the subdomain that owns it (§13.3) through it, the shell's library's own families (the appointments and
  the standing confirmation, §2.7) through the same class. It is machine-local and never synced: no table travels, no shared host opens it (no
  shared host runs the driver), and no route answers its rows but through the judges that own them.
- **Its package, the driver's and the GitDoor's first** (round 4, O3-3). The driver keeps to the base library today, its package pins saying it
  has *zero package dependencies* so that it never reaches the service's store directly (`src/Daoris.Desktop/Directory.Packages.props:6-9`, the
  doors of D46 §7), and the GitDoor is framework-dependent and references nothing so a door starts small on every remote act
  (`Daoris.Desktop.GitDoor.csproj:3-7`). `DesktopStore` needs `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite` and its native bundle, so row z-i amends both recorded
  rules: the driver takes the package, pinned at the service's version (`src/Daoris.Service/Directory.Packages.props:30-31`), for its own store
  alone, never `Daoris.Service.Core`, so the doors' reason stands; and the GitDoor takes it with the store class compiled in as its other files
  are, its start on a remote act measured against today's in row z-i, which lands only within its limit (§11). **Round 5** (§19.10): until a door
  passes that limit no family the door reads moves into the store and the door takes no package; with §19.15's proposal no such family moves in
  Part B, so z-i adds the package to the driver alone.
- **The service keeps its own store** for sessions, quests, asks, the channel and the event journal, and never opens the desktop's; what the
  service needs of the desktop's state reaches it as today, over its HTTP API from the driver. **What this design adds there stays there**, in
  tables of the service's own and never on its record (round 4): the keyed open's record, `session_opens` (§3.3), since a destination's
  uniqueness and its answer must be the open's own statement in the service's exclusive step; and a word's settlement, `said_settled` (§3.4 rule
  5), since a producer's retry must be answered inside the step that would keep it, written by a trigger so that every writer settles (§19.2).
  Round 4's third, a record's transition number in `session_transitions` (§4.5), is withdrawn in round 5 (§19.6). The service also answers its
  protocol marks with its run's id, `GET /api/protocol`, added by row p, every answer of a step a mark changes naming that run, so the desktop
  reads the marks again whenever an answer names another run or none (§19.12; round 6, *C5-1.12*).
- **Who opens it**: the shell (the modules and the driver library in one process); `daoris-driver`, as the headless loop and as each terminal
  verb, the Node CLI's management verbs reaching it by spawning `daoris-driver` as they do today; and the GitDoor, read-only, for the families it
  reads today (the git pointers, the permissions, the tools, the git identities), through the store class compiled into it as `GitIdentityUse`
  is, with the package above, once those families move and its start is measured within its limit (§19.10), which no Part B row needs. The service, its connectors, the launcher and the Node CLI never open it. The CLI's doctrine commands read no store at all.
- **Kept out of it, by the line**: the formats other tools own (an agent's `settings.json`, `.mcp.json` and `.claude.json`, the rooms'
  `.claude/settings.json`, the window kit's `window-state.json`, plugin and tool manifests); the tracked repository records (`daoris.json`,
  `daoris.lock`, `daoris.gates.json`, the lanes file); the install's manifests (`update/build.json`, `swap.json`); the files read before the store
  opens or by processes that never open it (`run/home.lease` and its older places, `run/running.json`, `data/home.json`, `data/update.json`, which
  the launcher reads, `run/home-move.json`, every lock under `run/locks/`); the streams (`.jsonl`: a session's events and lineage, the usage
  history's readings and turns, the machine log, the pipe's journal); **the credentials** in `profiles/` (`profiles/keys.json`, the keys in
  `profiles/remotes.json`), which stay in their own home so no export of the configuration can carry one; the files the Node CLI writes itself
  (`data/browser/*`, `profiles/remotes.json`) until its browser verbs reach them through `daoris-driver`, row z-xiv; and **`driver.json`
  itself**, which becomes the configuration's export and import format alone (§13.5).

### 13.3 The schema's shape

Each family's tables belong to one subdomain (§14), never to two. Every column a writer always writes is required; a column is added nullable
only for rows an older store-aware build may keep writing; a column is never repurposed; new meaning goes into a new table
(`compatible-builds.md`). Rows never hold a word's text, an account's key or a credential.

| Family | Tables | Subdomain | Replaces |
|---|---|---|---|
| The store's own | `store_version` (the schema's version and the oldest build that may write it); `store_revisions` (one revision per family, moved in the writing transaction); `store_runs` (each process run that writes: its run id, kind, pid and start time, and the moment it began, the claim's identity, §3.3; round 4, O3-1); `imports` (each file imported: family, its path in the home, size and SHA-256, moment, build, rows, class) | Store | — |
| Settings | `settings` (one row per key of today's `driver.json`, its value the key's JSON shape, read by today's one parser, `DriverConfig.Parse`, key by key, and every key this build does not know kept as it is, as `JsonKeeping` keeps it today) | Settings | `data/driver.json` |
| The role catalogue | `roles`, `role_revisions` (every revision of every role, never rewritten), `role_staffing` (each working role's staffing, seeded or added, its one home, holding what `driver.json`'s staffing holds today: per role its stored entry, its choices or an entry that does not read kept as found with why, and the three first-agent keys; the seeded eight's imported once from `driver.json` by row w-s, cut out of z-ii, an added role's from its record; round 5, §19.15; round 8, *O7-F2*, *O7-F8*), `role_staffing_projection` (one row, `written`: the four staffing paths of `driver.json` as w-s last wrote them, or found them at the import; round 6, *C5-1.15*, *O5-F4*; round 8, whose staffing lock replaces round 7's pending, *O7-F6*), `role_staffing_found` (each change of those paths a check found and did not make: the paths as the file showed them, `written` as it stood, and whether the person took or dismissed it; round 8, *O7-F1*, replacing round 7's `role_staffing_offers` and its `observed`) | Roles | `driver.json`'s `actors`; round 2's `roles` key |
| Appointments (*proposed for a later design*, round 5, §19.9) | `appointments` (each with its grant: the acts *Appoint* snapshotted, the set-up tick, its scope, its generation, its window, and the role's revision at *Appoint*, every revision since intersected at each act rather than written into the grant, §19.9), the standing confirmation's own records | the shell's standing confirmation (`Modules/Platform/StandingConfirmation.cs`), outside the driver's map, through `DesktopStore` (O3-6) | `data/standing-confirmation.json` |
| Bindings | `bindings` (one per session record of this machine: the kept conversation's id and door; the account by profile name and whether the person picked it, row a-ii; the role taken and its revision, row x; the process generation), each writer updating its own columns by name, never replacing a row (O3-16) | Participants | `data/sessions/<s>.harness.json` |
| Accounts | `account_cooling`, `account_windows`, `account_reads`, `account_names`, `usage`, `usage_epochs` | Agents | `data/cooling.json`, `windows.json`, `reads.json`, `accounts.json`, `usage.json`, `usage-history/epochs.json` |
| Agents' wiring | `agent_offers`, `harness_wiring`, `tools` | Agents (`tools` Install's) | `data/agent-offers.json`, `harnesses.json`, `tools.json` |
| The course | `moves` (§3.3: one standing move per record left, by the partial unique index `moves_standing` over the rows not `delivered`, `fell-back` or `closed`, so two executors of one line find one row and a line moves again once its move ends, round 5, §19.1; round 6, *O5-F1*; the executor kind; the claim, `claim_run` naming a `store_runs` row and `claim_generation`; the phase and each moment; the effect mark; the selection; the recipe; why, by, how), `move_words` (each word in transfer: its id, producer key, transfer id, order and disposition: `sent`, its door's write returned, written before its take and the delivery's one evidence, refused at a limit, uncertain, or *left*, round 6, *C5-1.4*; round 7, *C6-1.4*), and, with z-viii *proposed for a later design*, `go_on_marks`, `reopen_marks`, `go_on_new` (the person's press), `handed_quests` | Course | `<s>.cannot.json`, `.reopened.json`, `.new-session.json`, `data/handed/<quest>.json`; the boxes `<s>.limit-move.json` and `.hand-over.json`, imported and no longer written |
| News | `notices`, `news_said` (the said-set, §4.5), `journal_cursors` (one per teller) | Events | `data/notices.json` |
| The workplace | `keeping_runs`, `landed_branches`, `session_branches`, `abandoned`, `auto_landings`, `lines_moved`, `registry_followed` | Workplace | `data/keeping.json`, `landings.json`, `session-branches.json`, `abandoned.json`, `sessions/auto-landings.json`, `lines-moved.json`, `registry-followed.json` |
| Work records | `workflow_plans`, `workflow_runs`, `workflow_units`, `workflow_resources`, `workflow_closures`, `chat_looks`; `setup_plans`; `opinion_gates`, `opinion_dues` | Workflows; Setup; Review | `data/workflows/*`; `data/setup/<workspace>.json`; `data/opinions/gates/*`, `opinions/due.json` |
| Rules | `permissions`, `configured_access`; `git_identities`; `plugin_state`, `plugin_approvals` | Participants (admission's rules); Workplace (git); Plugins | `data/permissions.json`, `configured-access.json`; `git-identities.json`; `plugins.json`, `plugin-approvals.json` |
| Ask Daoris's links; the archive | `help_links` (its `.help.json`, `.wired.json`, `.published.json`); `session_archive` | Help; Workplace (history) | `data/sessions/<s>.help.json`, `.wired.json`, `.published.json`; `sessions/archived.json` |

Each family is migrated by its own row (§13.6). **Migrations** are numbered steps in code, forward only, each in one exclusive transaction:
the first process to open a store older than its build migrates it while the others wait on the write lock; a crash mid-step leaves the store
as it was, since SQLite's DDL is transactional. `store_version` is never lowered. A build opening a store whose oldest-writer stamp is past its
own reads the tables it knows and refuses every write, saying the store was written by a newer Daoris and that it should update, as the quest
store refuses a take's bind (QUESTSTOREVER1); a build older than a table never opens it. **The stamp is checked inside every writing
transaction** (round 4, X3-3.5): each write reads the oldest-writer stamp under the write lock its `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` holds, before it changes
anything, and refuses if the stamp is past its build, so a process that opened the store before another process's migration and kept its
connection cannot write after it. **A migration that raises the oldest-writer stamp runs only once the start's confirmation is settled** (§13.5;
O3-4), so a build rolled back before it confirmed still writes; a migration that adds a table an older store-aware build never opens leaves the
stamp where it is and runs at the first start.

### 13.4 Transactions and processes

- **One connection per process** behind a gate, as the service's `ConnectionGate` keeps its own (SQLITETX1); write-ahead logging, so a reader
  never waits on the writer; `synchronous = FULL`, since a claim or a mark lost to a power cut could repeat an effect, at a write rate low
  enough for it, measured in row z-i; a busy wait of five seconds, then a refusal said in the process's own words.
- **Every write is one transaction under the write lock, taken at its start** (`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`), so a read, a check and a write never
  interleave across processes, which round 2's file claim could not promise (O2-12). No transaction spans an await on another process or an
  external effect: an effect is a mark's transaction, the effect, and an outcome's transaction.
- **A move and its words in transfer in one transaction; its open claimed once** (the brief §9; X2-3.1; round 4, the settlement the service's,
  X3-3.2). The move's protocol:
  1. *Claim.* The executor writes the move in one transaction: its phase, its claim (its own process run, `store_runs`, and a generation) and
     the `opens` key it will open under.
  2. *Every external effect is fenced by a mark.* Before a spawn, a prompt or a copy, a transaction checks the claim (the row's run and
     generation are the writer's) and writes the effect's mark; then the effect; then a transaction writes its outcome. A mark whose transaction
     fails stops the executor before the effect. Between the mark and the effect nothing can take the move, since a claim passes only from a dead
     run (below).
  3. *The open is the service's, idempotent.* The open under the `opens` key moves the old part's words in the service's one step and keeps its
     result in `session_opens` (§3.3, §3.4 rule 3); its key is looked up there first, a repeat answered from its row (round 7, *O6-F5*), and
     only then is it refused unless the record left stands, is its line's head and is not active, the move then closing `superseded` or `gone`
     (round 6, *O5-F2*); an open whose answer was lost is asked again only after the host's marks are read again, and a retry answered without
     the key's echo is held (round 7, *O6-F4*); the next transaction writes `opened`, the new record's id, every word the open moved
     (`move_words`) and the phase, together.
  4. *Delivery.* The prompt's mark (step 2) and the service's acknowledged attempt (§3.4 rule 5); the moved words' files copied into the
     destination's folder and each word's `User` event recorded under its id, the display's (§3.4 rule 3; round 7, *O6-F8*, *O6-F2*); the
     write; **as the write returns, one transaction writes each word's `sent`** (round 7, *C6-1.4*); then the service takes it off `said`,
     whose trigger settles it (§3.4 rule 5); and only then does one transaction write the phase `delivered`, so no word waits on `said` while
     its move says `delivered` (round 5, §19.4, where round 4 wrote `delivered` before the take). A crash between is finished at the come-up
     from `sent`, never from the settlement, which says only that a word left `said`, nor from a session event, recorded before the write: a
     word with `sent` is taken, taken off by the take-up where it still waits; one gone without it *left*; one waiting, attempted, without it
     uncertain, the take-up settling it with the `lost` note itself (round 8, *O7-F4*); and the move closes `delivered` only when every word it moved has `sent`, else `held` with its begun effect, its *left* and
     uncertain words named (round 6, *C5-1.4*, *O5-F6*; round 7, *C6-1.4*).
  5. *Before anything is delivered, in every phase, the line's head is read* (round 4, X3-3.4): `Lines.HeadOf` from the move's destination, and
     the destination's attempts. Where the head has moved past the destination (a record `from` it under no key of the move's, which only an older
     build makes, since a newer one moves neither the old part nor the destination while the move stands, §3.1), nothing is delivered to the destination: the move is
     `held` for the person (reason `superseded`), its note naming the destination, the head and the destination's waiting words, the person's
     own offered to be taken back there and said at the head with today's presses, others' kept as claims, each said where the head's events
     already show it, per word where they carry its id and per part for an older build's copy (§19.3; round 6, *O5-F14*; round 7, *O6-F2*);
     each attempted one is settled uncertain and said. Round 4's `Lines.Forward`,
     a new service step moving them to the head, is withdrawn in round 5 for that hold (§19.3).
- **The claim is a process run, and passes only from a dead one** (round 4, O3-1, X3-1.1, X3-3.1). Round 3 made the claim's holder *the
  holder of the executor's role* and let a role's current holder take a move over from a run that no longer held the role. No such holder
  exists to read: `--share` runs a second driven loop beside the instance holding nothing, the two racing for the same quests
  (`Host/Program.cs:869-875`, `:890-895`), so a lease given up or shared passes no role; and the window's guard is one instance per install
  (`App/Program.cs:368`) while the host's start claim is keyed by the service's address and refuses no second launch
  (`Modules/Loop/HostSupervisor.cs:478-486`), so nothing excludes a second shell on the home before its conversations run, which they do before
  and without the lease (`DriverLoop.cs:777-800`). And a run that lost its role between a committed mark and its effect would make the effect
  with no next mark to refuse it (X3-3.1). So: every process that writes the store keeps a row in `store_runs` (a run id drawn at its start, its
  kind, its pid and its start time, the lease's own way of telling a live process, `Driver/Platform/HomeLease.cs:165-173`, `:427-448`); a move's
  claim names its executor's run; and **a move is taken over only where its claim's run is dead by that test**, its pid gone or alive under
  another start time, a start time that cannot be read counting as alive (`HomeLease.cs:66-68`, `:446`). Each live executor claims and keeps the
  moves of the lines it runs, whichever process it is: two shells on one home each keep their own conversations' moves, a `--share` loop and
  the instance each keep the driven lines they took, and a paused executor whose process lives is never taken over, so between its mark and its
  effect, however long it pauses, the move is its own. A dead run's effect, marked and never recorded, is the taker's `held`, named and never
  repeated by itself (X). Row f-i proves the three cases with live processes, not only two writes: two shells on one home, a `--share` loop
  beside the instance, and an executor paused after its mark commits and before its effect while another process tries to take its move.
  **Round 5** (§19.1): one standing row per record left makes two executors of one line exclusive (round 6: a partial index, *O5-F1*); a
  pre-store build beside a newer one is §13.5's older build at every moment, an empty destination held; a hung executor is said `move.quiet`
  past ten minutes, on its line and in the log, and keeps its moves until its process ends; a shell says once that another runs on its home.
  **Round 6** (*O5-F2*, *C5-1.1*) restates what the service's steps decide: a keyed open against a resume, by the open's check and the go-on's
  step (§3.3), and against a say, a take or another keyed open, by the say door's and the open's exclusive steps; there each word goes once.
  An older build's own move reads its words before its own unkeyed open and delivers its copy (`Chat/BriefedMove.cs:99`, `:142-143`), so
  against it a word can go twice, once by each build, as between two of today's shells; that is the bound, said, never *once*.
- **Wakes.** A write moves its family's revision in `store_revisions` in the same transaction, and after its commit touches the store's wake
  file, `data/desktop.db.wake`, which holds nothing; `HomeWatch` watches that one file where it watches `driver.json`, `cooling.json`,
  `harnesses.json`, `held-words.json`, `lines-moved.json`, the gates and the notices today, reads the revisions, and wakes exactly the families
  that moved. The look's period is the fallback where a watch drops an event, as it is today.
- **The GitDoor**, once its families move and its start passes z-i's limit (§19.10), opens the file read-only and reads its families' tables at
  each git act, never waiting on a writer under write-ahead logging; it writes nothing there, as it writes none of those files today. Until then,
  and in Part B, it reads its files as today.

### 13.5 Older builds, the rollback window and the export

- **An import's timing keeps D139's rollback whole.** The launcher rolls a new build back only before it writes `confirmed`, which it does once
  composed (D139; `docs/2026-10-03-update-when-idle-design.md` §3 step 5, §5); a build that confirmed is never rolled back by itself. So a family
  an older build only *reads* (a configuration or a cache) is imported at the first start of the build that moves it and **left in place as the
  backup**; a family an older build would *act on* (a box it recovers, a press it performs, a mark that gates a go-on, a record a landing or a
  removal follows, a conversation id it resumes) is imported only after that build has confirmed, then **set aside** under its import's receipt
  (`<file>.imported`, never deleted), and its readers take it up only after its import. A rollback before the confirmation therefore meets every
  file as it was.
- **When a start's confirmation is settled** (round 4, O3-4). The app confirms as it composes, off its start's thread
  (`App/Program.cs:323`; `InstallUpdater.StartedAsync`, `Modules/Update/InstallUpdater.cs:125-150`, `StagedBuild.Confirm` at `:135`); a swap can
  end `confirmed: false` when the new build stays silent past the launcher's minute (the update design §3 step 5); and a headless start never
  confirms. So a start's confirmation is **settled** once its install's swap journal (`<install>/update/swap.json`, `Driver/Setup/StagedBuild.cs:183`,
  `:198`, `:222`) is at no phase the launcher can still roll back from: no journal, no install (a workspace build), or `confirmed`, `installed`
  (confirmed or not, the silent swap among them, never rolled back after, `Driver/Setup/InstallSwap.cs:322-326`), `rolled-back` or `refused`;
  `swapping` and `started` are not settled (round 5, §19.7), nor is a journal that is there and does not read, which `StagedBuild.ReadJournal`
  answers as null like a missing one (`Driver/Setup/StagedBuild.cs:357-361`), so the guard reads absence and unreadability apart (round 6,
  *C5-1.7*). The window confirms in `OnStarting` before its loop starts (`App/Program.cs:307-335`),
  so its come-up meets it settled; a headless start and a verb meet it settled or do not run, since the launcher holds the home's Swap lease
  through its wait (`Launcher/Program.cs:90-107`) and they wait thirty seconds and refuse (`Host/Program.cs:870-886`). It is read at each come-up
  and look until settled. Every import of a family an older build acts on, the come-up's import of an older build's box among them (§3.3), and every migration
  that raises the store's oldest-writer stamp (§13.3) run only once it is settled; until a box is imported, the course reads its record's old
  part as `Never`, so nothing acts on the box's move meanwhile. Row z-i proves a rollback after a first start: the older build meets every file it
  acts on as it was and a store it can still write.
- **No older build ever reads back a revoked grant** (round 4, O3-2). A family left in place as the backup is read by an older build run on the
  home as it stood at its import, so a revoke or a narrowing made in the store after the import would come back with that build, unasked: the
  standing confirmation's `presses` grant, live from its file at an instance's start (`Modules/Platform/StandingConfirmation.cs:267-271`);
  `driver.json`'s `access`, `drivable`, `trees` and `standing`; `permissions.json`, which the GitDoor reads at every remote act,
  `configured-access.json` and `plugin-approvals.json`. So each **authority-bearing family** is one of two, chosen in its row by how its older
  reader reads absence, and proven there by a revoke made in the store followed by an older build's start finding it revoked: **set aside** where
  its older reader reads a missing file as off (the standing confirmation's, which reads *absent, unreadable, newer or another machine's* as
  off, `:267-271`: row z-iv; and each of z-xi's files whose reader is shown to do the same); otherwise **narrowings written through**: the
  family's own writer applies every narrowing made in the store to the backup file too, in its own format and under its own lock, narrowing
  only and never widening, so the backup is never wider than the store (`driver.json`'s authority keys, row z-ii, its other keys left as they
  were imported). That is the one write through a backup takes, and it carries no widening, which stays an import's, by the person's press.
  **Round 5 makes the choice file by file** (§19.8, each older reader's absence read at its line): set aside `standing-confirmation.json` and
  `plugin-approvals.json`, read as off; left in place, narrowings written through, `driver.json` (`drivable`, `access` and `standing`; `trees`
  grants nothing and is an ordinary key), `permissions.json`, `configured-access.json`, `git-identities.json` and `plugins.json`, each of whose
  older readers reads absence as wider somewhere. **The order across a crash:** the backup first, under its file's lock, then the store in one
  transaction recording the backup's new SHA-256 as its import's baseline; a crash between leaves the backup narrower, which the come-up applies
  as a narrowing import with no press, so the backup is never wider than the store at any moment an older build reads it. **Across two shells**
  every reader reads the store, never a copy in memory, and only the instance serving the home's pipe holds appointments
  (`Modules/Platform/InstancePipe.cs:575-590`).
- **After the move, no file is written through but a narrowing**, `driver.json`'s staffing excepted, which row w-s keeps as the store's
  projection, every staffing write written through to it, a widening by the person's press (§19.15; round 8). A file left in place is the configuration or state as it stood at its import,
  narrowed where the store narrowed an authority; an older build run later on this home (the person publishing an older build in place, a
  second install on the home, or a stage rolled back within the minute before its confirmation, which is all D139's rollback window is, §19.7)
  reads it and writes it as it always did. When the newer
  build comes back, it compares each left file with its import's SHA-256: a configuration changed since is **offered as an import**, through the
  same judges and, for a widening of authority, the person's own press, never applied by itself, while a narrowing of authority found there is
  applied without one, as the crash order needs (round 5, §19.8); a state family changed since is merged by its
  family's rule (a cool-off by the later `until`, a notice by its id, a landing record by its branch). A set-aside family whose file an older
  build wrote anew (a box, a press) is imported once and set aside again. Nothing is lost on a rollback: the store keeps what the newer build
  wrote, the files what the older one did.
- **Store-aware builds.** A build that knows the store reads and writes the tables it knows and never opens a newer one; one opening a store
  stamped past its writing refuses to write and says so (§13.3).
- **The export and import of the configuration** (the brief §9). `daoris driver config export` and Settings → *Export* write the settings, the
  role catalogue's records and the appointments' grants as readable JSON in `driver.json`'s shape (its keys as today, `actors` for the seeded
  roles' staffing, `roles` for the rest), to review, back up or edit by hand; `daoris driver config import` and Settings → *Import* read one back
  through every judge a door uses (D50, D110: the window, the terminal and Ask Daoris's proposal reach the same judges), each key judged as its
  own door judges it, and a change of authority (a role's acts, an appointment's grant, `access`, `drivable`, `trees`, `standing`) held for the
  person's own press in the window. The first start of the build that moves `driver.json` imports today's file once, as it stands, without a
  press: it is the configuration the person already set, granting what it already granted. `DAORIS_DRIVER_CONFIG` names a file imported into a
  store of its own, as the tests seed a configuration today (`DriverConfig.cs:846`), imported again whenever its SHA-256 differs from its last
  import, since tests and rehearsals rewrite it between runs; a fixture home's own `driver.json` is imported at its first start (O3-11).
- **D50, amended for the configuration** (round 4, O3-11). D50 keeps hand-editing working because *the file, not the surface, is authoritative*
  (`D50.md:7-9`), and today a hand edit of `driver.json` takes effect at the next tick (`HomeWatch.cs:200`; `DriverWatch.cs:1118`). After row
  z-ii the store is the truth: a hand edit of the left `driver.json` takes effect only through an import. At every start, and when the home's
  watch sees the file change, the newer build compares the file with its last import's SHA-256 and, where it differs, offers it as an import in
  Settings and on the terminal (`daoris driver config import`), each key through its door's judge and a change of authority held for the person's
  press, never applied by itself. Hand-editing keeps working as D50 asks, through the export and the import rather than the live file, which
  is the dated note on D50 row z-ii adds. Row z-ii's census names every test and tool that writes a home's `driver.json` and reads it back:
  the family rehearsal (`tools/family-rehearsal.mjs:981`, `:5603-5607`) and the proof kit (`tools/proof-kit.mjs:285-290`), each moved to
  `DAORIS_DRIVER_CONFIG` or to `daoris driver config import` in the row, so no later write of theirs goes inert.
- **A move in flight across a downgrade** (X2-1.1; O2-14). A move the store carries writes no old box, so an older build recovers it from the
  ledger alone, as it recovers today a move that keeps no box (the live limit move, the curve, a driven carry-on), and the newer build, back,
  reads the evidence it left before acting:

| Phase at the downgrade | The ledger shows | An older build | The newer build, back |
|---|---|---|---|
| `decided`, `quiescing`, `old-ended` | the old part as it was, its words on `said` | settles the old part as after any crash: an ended record's words go on by its own rules, resumed or held for its account; nothing opened | a later run on the old part, or a record `from` it under no key of the move's, closes the move as superseded; else it goes on from its phase |
| `opening` | nothing new (the open not made), or the new record under the move's key with the words already moved (the answer lost) | without the open, as above; with it, the new record is the line's head and its words wait there: its come-up resumes it where its conversation exists, else takes it as lost and briefs it (today's path), skipping the old part, which went on (`ChatGoOn.cs:228`) | asks the open again under its key, is answered the record and the words, and reconciles as below; or is answered `gone`, the older build having deleted the destination meanwhile, or refused, the record left gone or no longer the head, and closes the move `gone` or `superseded`, said on the line, never opening again (round 6, *C5-1.5*, *O5-F2*) |
| `opened`, `copied`, `verified` | the new record with the moved words, no prompt yet; or, where an older build found no kept conversation, a later record `from` it that the older build opened before it moved the new record's words (`Chat/BriefedMove.cs:130-158` opens before it delivers and takes) | as the open's second case: each moved word goes once, from the new record; or it opened its successor and crashed | the head read first (§13.4 step 5): moved words an older build's turn took are gone without the move's `sent`, so *left*, and the move is `held`, closing by §3.3's one rule, its note saying where the destination's events show them asked, per word where an event carries the word's id and per part for an older build's joined copy, never closed `delivered` by an older build's delivery (round 7, *C6-1.4*, *O6-F2*: round 6 closed it `delivered` from the `User` events, which are recorded before the write); a head past the destination holds the move for the person (reason `superseded`), its waiting words named as §19.3 offers them and its attempted ones uncertain and said (X3-3.4; round 5, §19.3; *O5-F14*); else it delivers |
| `delivering` | the new record, its words marked attempted or not, or taken and settled | settles attempted words as uncertain, never sent again (`GoOn/UncertainWords.cs:25-94`), and goes on with the rest: the window every word has today between its attempt and its take (round 5, §19.4), **but with no `lost` note for a moved word** *(O8-F8)*: d-ii records each moved word's `User` event under its id before the write, so the older come-up finds it shown and writes none (`:84-91`, `:97-98`), where today a move's one joined event, with no id, leaves the note; round 8's *no wider* was wrong for the note | the head read first, as above; a word is taken where the move's `sent` stands, whether or not it still waits, and *left* where it is gone without it, an older come-up's uncertain take-back and the person's take-back among them, never delivered again by the move (round 6, *C5-1.4*, *O5-F6*: round 5 read a word gone and settled as taken; round 7, *C6-1.4*: round 6 read the destination's `User` event, recorded before the write); the begun effect is `held` for the person unless every moved word has `sent`, **the hold offering each of the person's *left* words for saying again**, so a word an older come-up settled silently is said to them at the return (*O8-F8*) |
| `delivered`, `held`, `fell-back` | an ordinary line, or one held for the person with its reason; at `delivered` no moved word waits on `said`, since the take precedes it (§19.4) | as any such line | closes or keeps the hold |

No phase leaves a box an older build could execute, and no phase delivers to a part that is not the line's head, so no move is made twice
across builds and no word reaches a superseded part (round 4, X3-3.4); the price, said here, is that an older build briefs a part the newer one
opened by its own lost-conversation path rather than the move's recipe. Against an older *host*, which takes no
`opens` and moves no words, a move is made as today's are, a lost open held for the person (round 2's X-3.5), the host told by its protocol marks
(§19.12). The same table holds while an older build runs **beside** a newer one, a second install's on the home, and not only after a
downgrade, and a destination an older build's resume of the old part left with no words is held for the person (round 5, §19.1). Where the
service's steps decide the order, a keyed open against an older shell's go-on step, say or take, each waiting word goes to one of them; an
older build's own move, which reads its words before its own open and delivers its copy, can send a word the newer move also delivers, the
bound §3.4 rule 4 states (round 6, *O5-F2*, *C5-1.1*: round 5 said the service's steps gave every word to one of them).

### 13.6 The migration rows, one family each

Each row moves every writer and every reader of its family through its subdomain's seam onto the store, imports the family once (§13.5), and
proves an older build's own reads and writes over what it leaves (`compatible-builds.md`). The gates of the brief §6 stay green in each.
**Round 5 proposes** (§19.15) that Part B keep the families its own protocols read, z-i, z-iii and z-v, with the course's moves (f-i), the
roles' staffing (w-s, cut out of z-ii by the steward's change) and the catalogue's working roles (w), and leave z-ii's general settings, z-iv and
z-vi to z-xiv to later designs, each row below standing as that design's input with §19.8's choices.
