inventory missed, so its delete and clear did not take them (`<s>.limit-move.json`, `.hand-over-ask.json`, `.hand-over-state.json`), filed as
SESSFILES1; **it is built** (round 7, *C6-3.3*: round 6 still called them absent): `SessionHomeFiles.Inventory` names all three
(`Driver/Sessions/History/SessionHomeFiles.cs:127-132` at `abf03ea26`), and the task archive records it (2026-10-25).

### 13.7 Rejected

| Rejected | Why |
|---|---|
| The desktop's tables in the service's database, or one store for both | The service's database is the service's (D46, D47 §4), opened by every connector; a second owner of one file would make its schema two components' and a shared host's store carry desktop state |
| The service reading the desktop store (for settlements or bindings) | The service stays blind to the desktop's state; a word's settlement is the service's own, written where the word is taken (§3.4 rule 5; round 4) |
| A word's receipt in the desktop store, read by the client before a say (round 3's) | It races the service's take across the two stores, and the helper's and partner's hands never pass the client's say (X3-3.2, O3-5) |
| A store per family | The one transaction across a move and its words in transfer, and the one wake, would be lost |
| `driver.json` written through beside the store, or read live | Two homes of one setting; and an older build's later write would be silently overruled or silently win. Its authority keys' narrowings alone are applied to the backup (§13.5; round 4, O3-2), which makes no second home: the store stays the truth, and the backup is only never wider. The staffing's projection (row w-s, round 8) is written through whole and still no second home: the newer build reads only the store, the file only by the check, and an older build's write the check overrules is kept whole and said, never silently (§19.15) |
| Every file kept as a JSON blob row | Leaves each file's parser and loses typed reads for the Fabric's records; only the settings keep `driver.json`'s key shapes, so its one parser and the export stay one |
| `said` in the desktop store | The words are the service's record (D137 point 6), read by the say door inside its exclusive step |
| The Node CLI opening SQLite (`node:sqlite`) | Its doctrine commands keep no runtime dependency and the packaged CLI supports Node from 22; its management verbs reach the store through `daoris-driver` |
| Credentials in the store | Their home is `profiles/`, and an export must never carry one |
| A version stamp on `driver.json` to warn an older build | A stamp binds only the builds that read it (`compatible-builds.md`); no build before it would |
| Importing every family at the first start | A family an older build acts on would meet a rollback before the confirmation already moved; such a family waits for the confirmation (§13.5) |

## 14. The driver's subdomains: its module map

The owner's direction of 2026-10-25, after round 2 (the brief §10): the full update also analyzes the driver into its subdomains, among them
the context engine, the memory and knowledge system, the Fabric and the actors. The owner confirmed **Roles** as one subdomain's name and left
the map's other names and grouping to the steward; **the map below is the steward's decision by the owner's leave**, each choice over the
steward's draft (Company, Fabric, Course, Context engine, Knowledge, Events, Agents and accounts, Work, Workplace, Store, Platform, Loop) given
its reason in a line. §14 is Part A's but for the *Owns* column of §14.2, which names Part B's tables, and row w-0, Part B's. Read at
`2a08bde4a`: `Driver/` is 784 files and 231,407 lines, every file in one namespace, `Daoris.Driver` (the project's
`RootNamespace`), so namespaces follow nothing.

### 14.1 What the code says today

- **Fifteen folders cut by technique**: Trees 49,071 lines (its `Nodes/` 11,039 of them the workflow runtime, its `SessionTrees/` 17,581),
  Platform 26,019 in one flat folder of 86 files, Sessions 24,727, Conversations 20,409, Agents 19,062, Accounts 15,188, Loop 14,558, Help
  12,935, Plugins 10,854, Workflows 8,893, Service 7,442, Review 6,701, Quests 6,552, Setup 6,034 (install and update among it), Brainstorms 2,961.
- **One cycle.** Measured by which folder's type names each folder's files use (comments and strings removed), 59 of the 105 pairs of folders
  depend on each other both ways, and all fourteen folders but Loop form one strongly connected cycle: Sessions and Conversations (9 files one
  way, 35 the other), Accounts and Agents (5 and 12), Trees and Platform (96 and 24), Trees and Sessions (19 and 26).
- **Platform is no leaf**: it names types of fourteen folders, `DriverConfig` of nine and `DriverChoicesCommand` of ten, `NoteCodes` eight of
  Conversations'; 47 of its files name nothing outside it (`AtomicFile`, `Moments`, `MachineLog`, `Chain`, …), `DaorisHome` among them: round 3
  counted six folders' types in it, which were its `HomeEntry` members (`HomeEntry.StandingConfirmation`, `.Notices`, …), and it compiles into the
  GitDoor with the base library alone (`Daoris.Desktop.GitDoor.csproj:20-37`), so it names none (round 4, O3-8); the gate's scan as it stands
  still reads eleven of those member names as type names of higher subdomains (`Platform/DaorisHome.cs:326`, `:352`, `:354`, `:362`,
  `:423-427`: `DriverConfig`, `Notices`, `HelpProposals`, `OpinionGates`, `AgentOffers`, `GitIdentities`, `ConfiguredAccess`, `PluginState`,
  `UsageHistory`, `RuleProposals`, `SessionBranches`), which mod-0's semantic census reads as members, never types (O4-5; X4-1.4).
- **Loop holds rules**: `LimitResume.NotLifted` (`Loop/LimitResume.cs:74-83`), `SupervisorQuarantine.Unhandable` (`:46`), `OpinionLook`'s
  `Concluded`, `MadeSince` and `HoldsPush` (`:19`, `:127`, `:134`), `DriverWatch.Due` (`:883`), `Driver.OnAnotherAccount` (`Loop/Driver.cs:4581`),
  `DriverHelpers.LeadWaits` and `Wait` (`:428`, `:547`), `DriverContinuations.HandedToResume` (`:966`), `ReopenTelling.BringsToSource` (`:357`).
  **Ten `Loop/` classes are the look's executors, built from `LoopContext`** (O4-1), most of them from `Driver` too: `DriverContinuations.cs:24`,
  `DriverHandOvers.cs:18`, `DriverHelpers.cs:28`, `DriverPartners.cs:58`, `DriverIntakes.cs:25`, `DriverBrainstorms.cs:24`, `DriverMeetings.cs:29`,
  `DriverOpinions.cs:23`, `OpinionDeliverer.cs:22`, `OpinionGateLook.cs:11`; the gate refuses any of them outside Loop (`StructureCheck.cs:216-217`),
  so they stay in Loop, and only the rules above leave it, as pure functions the executors call.
- **`Domain/` does I/O through the driver's own types**, which the pure rule's tokens cannot see: `Quests/Domain/AskWords.cs:87` reads through
  `ServiceClient`, `Sessions/Domain/DrivenAccess.cs:68`, `:184` through `SessionProcesses` and `ServiceClient`, `Sessions/Domain/ActorRun.cs:169-178`
  holds a `MachineLog`, `Review/Domain/ReviewerChoice.cs:222`, `:369` walks `HarnessRoster`, `Plugins/Domain/PluginCatalog.cs:137` reads `DaorisHome`;
  nine `Domain/` files hold an `async Task`.
- **What the gates hold**: `StructureTests` (`Driver.Tests/StructureTests.cs:4-9`, its map `fixtures/structure.json`, its rules
  `StructureCheck.cs`) keeps every source in its module's folder, only `Domain` as a layer folder, a `Domain` file free of I/O tokens
  (`StructureCheck.cs:71-85`, `:140-149`), each listed test under its module's test folder (`:183-213`), and one direction alone: nothing outside
  Loop names a type declared in Loop (`:216-217`). The map must name exactly the fifteen modules (`StructureTests.cs:185-202`). Most source-scan
  tests find files by name (`SourceFiles.Driver("DriverConfig.cs")`, `Platform/ConfigSeamTests.cs:109`), but fourteen sites read sources by
  folder path, so a move breaks them unless they are made name-based first (O4-7; §14.6's list). The gate's identifier scan skips every name that
  follows a `.` (`StructureCheck.cs:385`), so a qualified, an alias's or a static import's reference names nothing to it today (O4-5); and its
  declaration pattern takes `record struct` and `record class` as a type named `struct` or `class`, which its table drops (`:238`, `:388`), so
  such a type, `Plugins/BoundedLines.cs:9`'s among them, is declared nowhere to it (X4-1.4).
- **The draft against the code.** It had no home for six parts the code holds: the settings (`DriverConfig` named in 165 files of every folder,
  22 of them `Domain/`), the terminal's and pipe's doors (53 `*Command.cs` files in thirteen folders, 18,125 lines, and the instance and lease
  group, 2,235), the local service's client (`ServiceClient`, named in 102 files of thirteen folders) with remote sync and registration
  following, Ask Daoris and its proposals (about 8,600), the plugins (10,854) and install and update with the managed tools (about 6,400). Its
  *Fabric* names what the code's `FABRIC-*` tags (about 1,700, 79 of them in Trees) and the company concept call the workflows, their graphs and
  their pipelines (`docs/2026-10-21-company-concept.md:28`), and holds about 2,000 scattered lines; its *Work* is about 55,000 lines; its Store
  is seven Platform files and 27 per-domain `*Files.cs` and `*Record(s).cs` stores, 118 files calling the file primitives directly.

### 14.2 The map

Twenty-one subdomains, each a folder and, at the last, a namespace `Daoris.Driver.<Name>` (§14.6), listed **bottom to top: a subdomain names only
its own types and those of the subdomains listed before it**, so the graph is acyclic by its order. *Takes* names today's folders and
files (`Platform/` file by file, the rest by folder, a file named where its folder splits); *Owns* its store tables (§13.3), Part B's, which a
subdomain takes only once their rows land; *Seam* the types the rest may name; *Tests* its test folder, which mirrors it (`Driver.Tests/<Name>/`).
Round 4 moves four placements that went against the map's own order (O3-8): the account rule stays with the roster in Agents, which returns
`ResumeChoice` (`Accounts/Harnesses/HarnessRoster.cs:975`, `:1033-1060`), rather than going to Course; `Trees/SessionGroups`, which reads
`StartVerdict`, `GoOnMarks`, `ResumeWords`, `AccountNames` and `SessionTrees`, goes to Work orders rather than Service; the attention policy,
`Quests/Domain/Attention`, which names `CurveMove` (`Attention.cs:338`), `StartVerdict` (`:272`) and `HelpRoom.Repository` (`:80`), goes to
Work orders too, Events keeping the vocabulary, its `HelpRoom` edge cut by the work rule's `daoris:help`, which the participant's work owns
(§2.2); and `DaorisHome`'s edges are none (§14.1).

**Round 4's named changes** (§17) move more, each where the code needs it rather than where a folder sat:
- **The Loop boundary** (O4-1). The ten `LoopContext`-built executors (§14.1) stay in Loop, and only the rules §14.1 lists leave it.
  **The chat runner's boundary**: `ChatContext` (`Chat/ChatContext.cs:14`) and `ChatStart` (`Chat/ChatRunner.cs:8`), the types the course's
  conversation executors share with the runner, are Course's, with the types `ChatContext` holds (`ChatRunner`'s nested `ITurnedChat` and
  `Handing`, and `ChatPendings`). Everything else the nine Course files (`BriefedMove`, `ChatGoOn`, `ChatHandOver`, `CurveMove`, `LimitMove`,
  `LimitWaits`, `StartWith`, `GoOn/UncertainWords`, `HandOver/HandOver`) and `Agents/Acp/Acp.cs` name of the core is one port family, **the chat
  runner's port**, which Course declares and `ChatRunner` in Loop implements: the runner as the executors' constructors take it, `ChatHelp`,
  `ChatTurns`; the static lines they read (`ChatTurns.LostLine`, `ChatTurns.Withdrawn`, `ChatRunner.EndedNote`, `.Recorded`,
  `.EndedByPersonNote`) move down to their owners, each a cut the census names. **The wiring view's sizes**, `WiringSizes` and `WiringBudget`
  (`Platform/WiringSizes.cs:10`, `:66`), go with the space code that names them (`Platform/DownloadClearing`, `SpaceCommand`, `SpaceFree`,
  `SystemSpace`, `ToolHomeLeftovers`, `Trees/Branches/BranchTidying`) to Workplace; the rest of the wiring view stays Loop's.
- **The vocabularies that sat above their readers move down** (O4-6): `ContinueReason`, `ContinueWhy` (`Conversations/Domain/Continuation.cs:9`,
  `:54`) and `SwitchDecision` (`Accounts/Domain/SwitchDecision.cs:7`) into Agents with the account rule, which names them
  (`Accounts/Rotation/ResumeAccount.cs:99-101`; `HarnessRoster.cs:2193-2212`; `Agents/Acp/Acp.cs:3401-3421`); `SessionRecord`
  (`Trees/SessionGroups.cs:14`) and the planner's and intake's record views (`Quests/Domain/Planner.cs:4`, `:246`, `:639`, `:728`, `:752`;
  `Intake.cs:8`, `:106`), the records `ServiceClient` returns, into Service; `PieceLauncher`, `PieceStarts` and `PieceJobs`
  (`Conversations/Partners/`) into Platform with `AgentStarts`, whose containment names them (`Platform/AgentStarts.cs:33`;
  `Agents/Consoles/AgentJobs.cs:77`, `:111-121`, `:139`); `MachineLog` and `HomeWrites` into Platform, since Platform, Service and Install name
  them and they name nothing above Platform; `AttentionKind` and `AttentionEvent` split out of `Quests/Domain/Attention.cs` (`:10`, `:45`) into
  Events, which `Notices` names; and `HarnessConversations`, which stores the binding (`Conversations/GoOn/HarnessConversations.cs:20`), into
  Participants.
- **Every file placed** (O4-5). The files round 4 left out, each with its concept: `Sessions/Domain/SessionEvents`, `SessionEnded` and
  `Observation` (the session's event and end vocabulary) and `Sessions/Events/HeldPermissions` (a held call, beside `RunningCalls`) in Events;
  `Sessions/Domain/AbandonEntry`, `AbandonPlan`, `ArchiveAnswers`, `DeleteOutcome`, `HistoryAnswers` and `HistoryPlan` in Workplace with
  `Sessions/History/`; `Sessions/Domain/AskWork`, `GoAheads`, `PauseOutcome`, `PausedBy`, `StartRun` and `StopAnswer` and the sessions' verbs
  `Sessions/SessionsCommand` in Work orders, the person's orders; `Sessions/Domain/SessionLanguage` and `Platform/DriverChoicesCommand` in
  Settings, the second's edges into ten folders' types (§14.1) cut with the settings' parser's; `Sessions/LookFacts` in Participants with
  `RunningSessions`, which holds it; `Setup/HeldFiles` in Install, which the launcher compiles with `InstallSwap`; `Chat/ChatHelperWords` in
  Course, built from `ChatContext`. mod-0's `structure.json` places every other file the same way, and fails on one it does not place.

**Codex's named changes** (§18; X4-1.19, X4-1.25) complete the chat runner's boundary, each a type moved to its one owner and no new contract,
the stricter of the two reads (round 4's change had left `ChatPending` in the runner's core):
- **The pending family, whole**: `ChatPending` (`Chat/ChatPending.cs:19`) goes with `ChatPendings` (`:207`), which stores and returns it
  (`:209`, `:218`), to Course beside `ChatContext`; both name only Course's types and lower ones (`ChatStart`; `ActorEffective`, `BuildPass`).
- **`ConversationWalk`** (`Chat/ChatRunner.cs:39-40`), the walk's answer `ChatPending.Next` holds (`ChatPending.cs:76`), leaves the runner's
  file for Course, naming only Agents' and Roles' types; `ChatRunner.WalkAsync` (`ChatRunner.cs:946`) answers it from Loop.
- **The configured access's record**, `Help/ConfiguredAccess` whole (the record, its read and its one writer, naming only Platform's and Store's
  types), which `ChatContext` holds (`Chat/ChatContext.cs:80`) and the room's facts read (`Help/HelpCheckouts.cs:146`;
  `Help/Room/HelpRoomMayDo.cs:123`, `:270-318`), is Knowledge's, the lowest subdomain that reads it, so every edge into it points down, Course's
  and Knowledge's own included. Help keeps its judges, which read it (`HelpHousekeeping`, `HelpSettings`, `HelpSwap`), and every grant reads
  exactly as today, nothing migrated or reinterpreted (X4-1.25). `configured_access`, Part B's table (§13.3), stays Participants', written
  through this record.

appointments and the act judge among them), the host (`Daoris.Driver.Host`), the GitDoor, the launcher and the piece start, each its own
project; the service, whose side of this design (the head, the keyed open, the settlement, the journal's triggers, the work's twin) lands in
its own code. A verb file (`*Command.cs`) belongs to the subdomain whose acts it
drives, never to a subdomain of verbs.

### 14.3 The rules the map keeps

- **Downward only.** A file names only its own subdomain's types and those ranked below it. Where a lower subdomain calls a higher one today
  (the landing calling a plugin's `work/land`, `NoteCodes` naming Conversations' types, the nine Course files and `Acp.cs` naming the chat
  runner's core, the settings' parser naming nine folders' rule types), the lower declares a port and the higher implements it, wired by Loop; or
  the type moves down to its one owner (§14.2's round-4 changes; O4-1, O4-6). Settings in particular reads keys by their shape and hands each subdomain its own keys, whose rule types that subdomain owns, so
  `DriverConfig` names none of them; until its 165 readers move, `DriverConfig` stays the composed record, read by each through a census that
  shrinks one subdomain at a time.
- **Loop holds no rules.** Each judgement it holds today moves to its subdomain (§14.1's list, each with its row) as a pure function; Loop composes
  and executes, and its executors built from `LoopContext` stay in it and call those functions (O4-1).
- **`Domain/` stays pure, and is seen to be.** Beside today's tokens, a `Domain/` file names no type of the I/O seams (`ServiceClient`,
  `SessionProcesses`, `HarnessRoster`, `DaorisHome`, `MachineLog`, `WorkingTree`, `DesktopStore`) and holds no `async`; today's six crossings and
  nine `async` files are the census's named exceptions, each cut by its subdomain's row.
- **No global usings** of a subdomain's namespace, once there are namespaces. The census never reads `using` lines as a file's dependencies:
  a fully qualified reference needs none (`Driver/Sessions/SessionsCommand.cs:185` names `Daoris.Driver.HolderKey` so today), so it reads every
  type reference, ordinary, qualified, aliased and static, by Roslyn's semantic model of every file (round 4, X3-4.1; its named changes,
  X4-1.4).

### 14.4 Folders and a census, or assemblies

**Folders, namespaces and a census, chosen; assemblies rejected for now.** Assemblies would hold the graph at compile time, but they cannot exist
while the folders form one cycle: every one of the 59 two-way pairs would have to be cut before the first split builds, which is the big-bang
rewrite the brief §7 forbids. They would also turn the tests' one `InternalsVisibleTo` into twenty-one, re-cut the GitDoor, which compiles fourteen
of the driver's files directly (`Daoris.Desktop.GitDoor.csproj:20-37`), and multiply the projects the merge tool's parallel gates build beside each other, whose
shared outputs already had to be kept apart (KEEPSIZE1b3's FIX-LOG entry). A census in `StructureTests` holds the same graph from the first row,
names every edge against it as *not yet cut* with the row that cuts it, and fails on a new one; once a subdomain's edges all point down it can
become an assembly in a row of its own, measured, Store and Platform the first candidates.

### 14.5 The structure gate, a row of its own (row mod-0)

`StructureTests` reads the map from `fixtures/structure.json`, which names the twenty-one subdomains in their order, each one's folder, its
allowed `Domain/` and, from its namespace row on, its namespace, and the census of today's exceptions; `StructureCheck` gains:

1. **The subdomain rule**: every file of the driver is placed in `structure.json`, a file it does not place failing (O4-5); a source sits in its
   subdomain's folder once its move lands; a file added outside its subdomain fails, so new code is written in place from this row on. Every
   namespace stays `Daoris.Driver` until the subdomain's namespace row (§14.6).
2. **The direction rule**: a file names only its own subdomain's types and lower ones, read by **every type reference** in it, never by
   `using` lines alone (X3-4.1), and **by Roslyn's semantic model** (X4-1.4): one compilation of every driver source, each name that binds to a
   type a driver file declares an edge, whatever its spelling, plain, **namespace-qualified** (`Daoris.Driver.<Name>`,
   `global::Daoris.Driver.<Name>`), **an alias's target**, a nested type's alias among them, or **a `using static` target**, where today's
   identifier scan skips every name that follows a `.` (`StructureCheck.cs:385`; O4-5); **a member's occurrence binds to the member and is no
   edge**, so a member named like a higher type (`DaorisHome`'s `HomeEntry` members, §14.1) counts none while a type of that name named in the
   same file counts, told by the symbol and never by the spelling; every declaration read as the compiler reads it, `record struct` and `record
   class` among them (`StructureCheck.cs:238`, `:388`); comments and strings naming nothing. The tests take `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp` for
   this alone, and nothing that ships takes it (row mod-0). An edge against the order is allowed only where the census names it, **as an exact
   edge, a file and the type it names, under its edge family and the subdomain row after which it is cut** (O4-6; X4-1.4), so no family admits
   an edge it does not list.
3. **The purity rule** widened as §14.3 says, its exceptions named.
4. **Loop's rule** kept and widened: nothing names a type in `Loop/`'s folder, with no exception, so no move takes a class that names
   `LoopContext` out of Loop (O4-1); an edge into a file the map places in Loop that still sits outside its folder (the chat runner's core and
   the wiring view, until mod-xxi) is a census exception named with its cut, and every such cut lands before mod-xxi moves the file in; and Loop
   declares no judgement the census does not name as moving.
5. **The mirror rule** (`StructureCheck.cs:183-213`) with today's test folders listed as not yet moved, each mod row moving its subdomain's
   tests into `Driver.Tests/<Name>/` (O4-5).
6. **No global using** of a subdomain's namespace, from the namespace rows on.

Its proof: the gate run over the tree at this row's commit passes with today's exceptions listed and fails on a planted file that names an
upward type plainly, fully qualified, through `global::`, through a namespace alias, through a nested type's alias and through a static import;
passes a planted member named like a higher type and fails on a file that has such a member and names the type too; reads a planted `record
struct` and `record class` as declared; fails on a planted edge of a listed family its exceptions do not list (X4-1.4); fails on a file
`structure.json` does not place and on a planted edge into `Loop/`; a planted `Domain/` file naming
`ServiceClient`; and a planted rule in Loop; `StructureTests`' own fixtures for each. It amends D166 point 1's fifteen modules (Brainstorms the fifteenth by D176 point 1,
`StructureTests.cs:189-191`) by the twenty-one subdomains, and keeps D166 point 4's order and its rejection of renaming namespaces as files
move (O3-10).

### 14.6 The order of the moves

Each move is a Part A row that **changes no behaviour**: it moves one subdomain's files into its folder, **its namespace unchanged** (D166 point 4:
namespaces last; O3-10), updates the map, moves every **linked consumer** with the files (X3-4.2; the list completed by O4-7):
- the GitDoor's `Compile Include` paths (`Daoris.Desktop.GitDoor.csproj:20-37`);
- the launcher's nine compiled driver files (`Daoris.Desktop.Launcher.csproj:23-40`): `StagedBuild`, `InstallSwap`, `HeldFiles`, `HomeWrites`,
  `HomeLease`, `HomeIdentity`, `DaorisHome`, `DriverException` and `AtomicFile`;
- PieceStart's two (`Daoris.Desktop.PieceStart.csproj:21-22`): `PieceLauncher` and `ProcessJob`;
- the driver project's embedded resources' `Include` paths, their logical names pinned;
- **the fourteen sites that read driver sources by folder path**, made name-based first, as D166 point 4 orders, in row mod-0, before the first move:
  `Driver.Tests/Conversations/DrivenInboxTests.cs:576`, `:579`; `Loop/DriverCommandTests.cs:138-139`, `:166`, `:184`, `:203`, `:284-285`;
  `Platform/InstanceDoorTests.cs:831`; `Trees/GitLaunchTests.cs:200`; `Modules.Tests/Loop/DriverLoopSendsBackTests.cs:38`; and the service's
  `Daoris.Service.Tests/Mcp/McpRoleFilterTests.cs:72` (`Conversations/Domain/PartnerPermissions.cs`) and `McpToolRegistrationTests.cs:61`
  (`Brainstorms/Domain/BrainstormPermissions.cs`).

Each move keeps every gate of the brief §6 green, builds the GitDoor, the launcher and PieceStart and runs the GitDoor's tests where they compile
a moved file, and runs the modules' tests and the service's Mcp tests (O4-7). It lands when no open branch holds the files it moves, since
a move under another branch's edit is its conflict. The moves go **bottom up, one subdomain a row** (X3-6.2), so each moves onto ground already in
place: mod-i Platform; mod-ii Store (today's file primitives; `DesktopStore` is row z-i's); mod-iii Settings; mod-iv Service; mod-v Install;
mod-vi Instance; mod-vii Events; mod-viii Knowledge; mod-ix Agents; mod-x Workplace; mod-xi Roles (the driver's own role files; the act
vocabulary's move is row w-0's, Part B's); mod-xii Participants; mod-xiii Context; mod-xiv Course; mod-xv Work orders; mod-xvi Review; mod-xvii
Plugins; mod-xviii Workflows; mod-xix Setup; mod-xx Help; mod-xxi Loop, the remainder. **The cuts** follow each move, one edge family per row (a
port declared, or a type moved to its owner), each changing no behaviour and shrinking the census by its edges, each named in the census with
its exact edges under their family and the subdomain row after which it is cut (O4-6; X4-1.4), before it lands (the account rule's types and its
vocabulary with the roster, §14.2; the chat runner's port, the types `ChatContext` holds, the pending family whole and `ConversationWalk`, and the
configured access's record down to Knowledge, all before mod-xxi (X4-1.19); `HelpRoom.Repository`'s `daoris:help` moved down to the
participant's work rule; the settings' parser's rule types to their subdomains; the vocabularies §14.2 moves down); Loop's rules leave it as pure
functions in the rows that move their subdomains, the executors built from `LoopContext` staying in Loop and calling them, each a move with its
tests, never a rewrite (O4-1). The order being total makes the map acyclic by construction, not today's placements (X4-1.26): mod-xxi lands only
on a census with no upward exception left and no edge into Loop from outside it, the ten executors built from `LoopContext` still in Loop.
Foundation rows that add code (the account rule, the course, the store, the catalogue) write it in its subdomain's folder from row mod-0 on, so
no new code needs moving.

**The namespace rows come last**, one per subdomain once every edge of it points down, each moving its files into `Daoris.Driver.<Name>` and adding
the `using` lines its readers need. They are in neither part's acceptance and are designed when they come, since a namespace's move changes the
machine log's `type` field, an exception's full name (`Driver/Platform/MachineLog.cs:322`), which each row names as its one known change, and
since a simple name binds to a namespace before a type of the same name, which the design's own types avoid by their names (`LineCourse`,
`Participant.Of`, `ParticipantWork.Of`; §6; O3-9).

### 14.7 Where the three areas, the store and the roles land

| Part of this design | Lands in |
|---|---|
| **Who acts** (§2): the participant read, the work, bindings, lineage, admission | **Participants**, the binding's store `HarnessConversations` among it (O4-6), with the service's twin of the work in the service |
| The capabilities, the role catalogue and the start plan (§2.4, §2.7) | **Roles**; the appointments, the act judge and the act door (§2.5) in the shell's library, outside the map |
| **How work moves** (§3): `LineCourse.Decide`, the move record, the words' rules | **Course**, executed by **Loop**'s two loops, the look's executors built from `LoopContext` and the chat runner behind Course's port (O4-1); `LineAccount` in **Agents** with the roster; a move's brief and carry in **Context**; the say door's head, the keyed open and the words' settlement in the service |
| **What is said** (§4): the vocabulary, the hubs, the journal's readers, the notices, the machine log | **Events**; the outside projection (`work/event`) in **Plugins**; the journal itself in the service's store |
| **Where state is kept** (§13): `DesktopStore`, its migrations and imports | **Store**, each family's tables in the subdomain the §13.3 table names |
| The settings and their export and import | **Settings** |

## 15. Round 3: the reads of round 2 answered

Each finding by number, as `docs/2026-10-25-foundation-reads.md` holds it, checked by the steward against the code at `2a08bde4a`, whose `src/` is
`29a066182`'s, where both reads were made; *taken* says where the body changed, *disputed* says why not. Where the two reads overlap the stricter is
taken, and the pair is named. Codex's verdict names three choices still to make; round 3 makes them: **the role's authorization lifecycle**
(§2.7: the act within the code's table, the appointment's grant and the role's configuration, the grant raised only at the next *Appoint*), **the
move's fencing and rollback protocol** (§3.3, §13.4, §13.5: a store row claimed by its executor role's holder, every external effect fenced by a
mark written before it, no old box beside it and a phase table for an older build), and **the journal's capture schema** (§4.1: an allow-listed
snapshot per row, bounded per row and per table).

### 16.3 What Part B's next round must settle

*Answered in round 5's §19, item by item.*

Part B's round 5 is read by both makers on the protocols round 4 chose, before any of its rows is built: the claim by process run and its
takeover only from a dead run, with what a hung live executor costs and how two installs' shells on one home behave (§3.3, §13.4); the
service's settlement, `said_settled`, its horizon and every door that answers a key, and `Lines.Forward`, the new service step that moves a
superseded destination's words to the head (§3.4 rule 5, §13.4 step 5); `session_opens`, its retention and an older host's lost open; the
transition number's trigger and its use as news's one key (§4.5); the confirmation's *settled* for a headless start and a silent swap (§13.5);
each authority-bearing family's choice between setting aside and writing narrowings through, file by file of z-xi by its older reader (§13.5); the
act vocabulary's move and the grant's lowering ordered across two stores until row z-iv (§2.7; row w-0); and the GitDoor's start with SQLite,
measured in row z-i. Part B's rows wait for that round's acceptance, and the ⚖ rows for both makers' reads of it.

**Six items the Opus reader's read of round 4 adds** (O4-12), each a decision round 5 makes:
1. **Row o's shape.** The shape a word kept for a moving record takes so that an older shell's sweep does not resume it (§3.4 rule 4): a
   protocol decision, open since round 2, not a measurement; round 5 names the shape, or says the row waits for the rollback window and why, in
   place of *where no such shape exists the row waits*.
2. **The version matrix.** One table for each service protocol this design adds or changes (`opens` with `session_opens`, the `Continued`
   refusal, the attempt's answer, `said_settled`, the journal), giving an older desktop against a newer service and a newer desktop against an
   older service, where today each pairing is said in a different section.
3. **The say door's head read.** `Lines.HeadOf` walks the `from` chain inside the exclusive step for every word, with no index on
   `from_session` known (§11, *unverified*), on the hottest path; round 5 fixes row d-ii's landing limit for it, and the index if one is needed.
4. **The settlement's horizon as a decision.** Seven days and 10,000 rows bound D137's and D188's *never sent again* for a producer's retry
   (§3.4 rule 5): a decision note on what the guarantee is past the horizon, not only a bound row p measures.
5. **The `delivered` window.** Between `delivered` and the take (§13.4 step 4), across a downgrade, the word still waits on `said`, marked
   attempted, and an older build settles it as uncertain; §13.5's `delivered` row says so, with what the newer build reads of it on its return.
6. **The chat runner's boundary** in Part B's rows (O4-1, taken in Part A's map, §14.2): rows e, f-i and h write the course's executors and the
   move's take-up into Course, and they take `ChatContext` and the chat runner's port as §14.2 places them, settled before those rows.

**Three items Codex's read of round 4 adds** (X4-3.1 to X4-3.3), each blocking for Part B, each a decision round 5 makes with its proof:
7. **An older connector writing the database directly** (X4-3.1, which widens item 2, the stricter taken). The version matrix gains a third
   column beside the two desktop-and-service pairings: an older MCP host, which composes its own stores (`Mcp/Program.cs:104`) and its own
   helper desk (`Core/Composition/ServiceFactory.cs:410`), writing a migrated service database itself. Its desk checks only the words still
   waiting before it keeps another (`Core/Sessions/HelperDesk.cs:180`, `:189`), so a newer HTTP service's settlement cannot guard that path;
   round 5 says what `said_settled`, `session_opens` and the journal hold against such a writer, or what keeps it from writing.
8. **A grant lowered across a crash and across live processes** (X4-3.2). The order in which a narrowing commits to the store and to its
   backup file (§13.5's *narrowings written through*), and what the come-up finds after a crash between the two; and a proof that every live
   appointment is lowered when two shells run on one home, since the standing confirmation's lock and its list of appointments live in each
   instance's memory (`Modules/Platform/StandingConfirmation.cs:378`, `:390`), so *the one mutation step* alone does not reach the other's.
9. **An occurrence's key beyond a change of state** (X4-3.3). A conversation can answer again and again with no change of its record's state:
   today the watch tells those answers apart by `ChatQueue.LastTurnEnded` (`Driver/Quests/Domain/Attention.cs:333`, `:339`) and each renews its
   notice (`Driver/Platform/Notices.cs:146`). The transition number (§4.5) keys only a change of state, so round 5 names a durable correlation
   for a turn's answer and every other occurrence that changes no state, with proofs of repeated answers and of a reconciliation below the floor
   telling each once.
