## 19. Round 5: Part B's agenda settled

Round 5 is Part B's alone. Part A is accepted (D212's note of 2026-10-25) and building, and its text stands as main has it. This round settles
§16.3's agenda item by item against the code on main at `eced2688e`, whose `src/` is `86f32f40c`'s and `6bac9a0be`'s unchanged, so round 4's lines
hold and every line below is that commit's; `Launcher/` is `src/Daoris.Desktop/Daoris.Desktop.Launcher/` and `GitDoor/` its
`Daoris.Desktop.GitDoor/`. Each item gives what the code does, the protocol chosen with its counterexamples and the row that proves it, what
is measured and what holds until then, and what dropping the item from this design and leaving it to a later one would cost. Where a
smaller protocol meets both readers' concerns it is taken, and four of round 4's additions are withdrawn by it: `Lines.Forward` (19.3),
`session_transitions` with the reconciliation path's news (19.6), the settlement's disposition (19.2) and row o's shape of its own (19.11). Two
larger cuts are proposed for both readers' reads rather than made (19.15): the appointed half of the role catalogue, and the store's families
no Part B protocol reads, `driver.json`'s general settings among them; the actors' staffing stays, as row w-s (19.15, the steward's change). The body (§3, §4, §5.2, §11, §13 and §10's Part B rows) carries what is settled. Nothing of Part B is built.
**Round 6** changes the items below in place where both makers' reads of round 5 found them short, each change marked *(C5-n.m)* or
*(O5-Fn)* and answered by its finding in §20, and **round 7** where both makers' reads of round 6 did, marked *(C6-n.m)* or *(O6-Fn)* and
answered in §21; a line round 7 adds is `abf03ea26`'s. Round 8's changes are marked *(C7-n.m)* or *(O7-Fn)* and answered in §22, and **its
named changes**, which both makers' reads of round 8 asked, are marked *(C8-n.m)* or *(O8-Fn)* and listed in §23, a line they add being
`26ef34d80`'s.

### 19.1 The claim by process run: a hung live executor, two shells on one home

**The code.** The lease tells a live process by its pid and its start time within a second, a start time it may not read counting as alive
(`Driver/Platform/HomeLease.cs:427-449`, `:66-68`). The window keeps one instance per install (`App/Program.cs:368-372`). The pipe is named
by the home and the Windows session, and a second instance on one home in one session is refused it before the standing confirmation loads
(`Modules/Platform/InstancePipe.cs:575-590`; `Driver/Platform/PipeName.cs:19-20`). A shell's come-up and its chat take-up run before and
without the lease (`Modules/Loop/DriverLoop.cs:777`, `:1221-1225`, `:841`), the take-up guarded in its own process only
(`Chat/ChatGoOn.cs:191-262`; `GoingOn`, `:391`). So two shells on one home (two installs', or a workspace build's beside an install's under
one `DAORIS_HOME`) both run conversations today, and both come-ups may take up one ended chat: a race of today's that no move makes.

**Settled.**
1. *Two newer shells.* One standing move per record left, by the partial unique index `moves_standing` over the moves not `delivered`,
   `fell-back` or `closed` (§3.3), so of two executors deciding a move of one line the second's claim finds the standing row under the write lock
   and reads the line as the course's `Never` (§3.1). The index, not the lease, makes them exclusive, and each keeps the moves it claimed while
   it lives; once a move ends, `superseded` or a hold closed `resolved` by §3.3's one rule among its ends (round 7, *O6-F7*), the next move of
   that record claims, and the ended row stays as evidence (round 6, *O5-F1*: round 5's row unique for its record's whole life read `Never` for
   good after any such end).
   *A newer shell's resume beside another's keyed open* is decided by the service (round 6, *O5-F2*): the keyed open's step refuses a record
   left that is active, is not its line's head or does not stand, and the go-on's step, the ledger's move out of an ended or parked state taken
   before anything is spawned (`Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:1660`; `Chat/ChatGoOn.cs:663-667`), refuses a record a keyed open has left while
   the record it opened stands, so whichever commits first is made and the other refused.
2. *A newer shell beside an older one* (restated in round 6, *O5-F2*, *C5-1.1*). A build from before the store is §13.5's older build at every
   moment, not only after a downgrade, and the newer build reads the line's head before it delivers (§13.4 step 5). **Where the service's steps
   decide the order, each waiting word goes once**: an older shell's go-on against this host is refused at its step once a keyed open left the
   record, and a keyed open is refused once the older go-on's step made the record active; a say, a take and a keyed open meet in the service's
   exclusive steps. **Against an older build's own move it is not *once***: that move reads its words before its own open, which names no key and
   which no newer step refuses (`Chat/BriefedMove.cs:99`), and delivers its copy (`:142-143`) before its take (`:158`), so in Codex's schedule
   (the older move reads, pauses, a newer move opens, delivers and ends, the older move opens its successor and delivers) a word goes twice, once
   by each build, each said on its part, as between two of today's shells on one home (above); refusing an unkeyed open from a record that has
   a successor would close it, but would refuse today's unkeyed opens too (§9). A destination that an older build's resume left with no words is
   `held` for the person, naming both parts, and never delivered. A store-aware older build is refused every write once a
   migration has raised the stamp past it (§13.3), so it claims no move; short of that it writes the tables it knows under the same claim, and is
   a newer shell to every move it knows.
3. *Said.* At its come-up a shell reads `store_runs` for another live shell's run on its home, by the lease's test, and says once that another
   Daoris runs on this home, with its pid and since when, on the channel and in the words the lease's wait uses (`DriverLoop.cs:1281-1288`,
   `:1307-1311`). It refuses nothing.
4. *A hung executor* keeps its moves until its process ends. Nothing takes a move from a live run, since a hung run cannot be told from a slow
   one. A move in a phase after `old-ended` whose moment is older than ten minutes, claimed by a live run, is said once on its line, a note
   with its code beside `part.quiet`, worded in both catalogues, and in the machine log as `move.quiet`, with the move, its phase and the run's
   kind and pid (round 6, *O5-F15*: round 5 said it in the log alone, which the person does not read). The person's door is the one they have today for a hung Daoris: quitting
   it, whose end makes its run dead and its moves taken up. `quiescing` has no such bound, since it waits for the old part's own turn, which
   the line already shows.
5. *The home is one machine's*, as the lease assumes: a pid of another machine reads as gone, so neither the lease nor the claim is relied on
   over a home two machines open.

**Proof**, row f-i: two shells on one home deciding a move of one line, one row and one claim; the second shell said once; an executor paused
past ten minutes said `move.quiet` once, on its line and in the log, and never taken over, then killed and its moves taken up at the next look;
round 6's: a move closed `superseded`, and one held and resolved, each followed by a second move of the record, which claims (*O5-F1*); a newer
resume and a newer keyed open of one record in both orders, one made and the other refused (*O5-F2*). Row f-ii, the first whose move delivers
(round 7, *O6-F12*: round 6 put these two in f-i, which adopts no move that delivers): an older shell's resume of the old part beside a newer
shell's open, each word once and the empty destination held; and Codex's delayed older move, asserting the bound in point 2 and nothing a
third time (*C5-1.1*). **Measured** by f-i on the install: how often
`move.quiet` is said; until then nothing more is built for a hung executor, and a person's door for it is a row of its own if it happens.
**To drop:** the claim is what makes a move one executor's, so it stays with the move record; dropping 3 and 4 costs only their telling.

### 19.2 The words' settlement, and its horizon as a decision

**The code.** A word leaves `said` by one of two statements: the take, which rewrites the list (`TakeSaidAsync`, `Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:1597-1612`),
and a state move whose words go with it (`SetStateAsync`'s `clearSaid`, `:866`, `:901`); a word is kept by one more (`KeepSaidAsync`, `:1561-1589`),
and the attempt mark rewrites the list in place with every id kept (`:1623-1637`). The say door answers a key only while
a word with it waits (`Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:955-962`), the helper desk only while a word of that helper waits
(`Core/Sessions/HelperDesk.cs:180-189`, its key `helper:<id>`), the partner desk only while its key waits (`Core/Sessions/PartnerDesk.cs:136-147`).
An older connector composes its own stores and desks (`Mcp/Program.cs:104`; `Core/Composition/ServiceFactory.cs:410`).

**Settled.**
1. *Written by trigger*, as the journal and the revisions are (`Sessions.cs:652-681`). `said_settled` is filled by a trigger on `sessions`,
   `AFTER UPDATE OF said`, guarded `WHEN OLD.said IS NOT NEW.said AND NEW.origin IS NULL`, writing, for each word of OLD's list whose id
   NEW's lacks, one row keyed by **the record it left** (`NEW.id`), **the word's id** and **its producer key** (or `id:<word id>` for a word
   with none), with the moment, `INSERT OR REPLACE` on those three, so a word that leaves two records leaves a row on each and no row of another
   record or another word is replaced (round 7, *C6-1.2*: round 6 keyed the row by the word's id alone, and a word's id is eight hexadecimal
   characters drawn at random, `Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:1383-1384`, while an answer kept before `said` reads as `answer` on every record,
   `Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:1545`, `:1651`; round 6, *C5-1.2*, *O5-F7*, had taken the record over round 5's line). So every writer settles a word as it takes it, the host, each connector and a connector
   built before the table, through the statement it already runs, and no take changes. The attempt mark settles nothing, since it keeps every
   id. A word the keyed open moves leaves the old record and waits on the new one, which the head walk visits from the old, so either reading
   answers its key as kept. **Round 5's line was wrong for a record with no `entry`**: the trigger wrote the record's id where the reader's line is
   the rule's key, never written back (`Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:599-601`; `Core/Sessions/SessionEntries.cs:46-49`), so a successor holding the
   rule's key read another namespace; no line rule SQL can compute reaches that key, which walks quests and publishers
   (`SessionEntries.cs:71-121`), so the settlement keeps the record and the reader brings the line.
2. *No disposition.* The settlement says only that the word left `said`, which is all a retry asks, **and never that a turn took it**: a
   take-back, an uncertain word's settlement, a park's clear, the owner superseding the supervisor's word and the restore leave `said` by the same
   statements (`Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:1312`; `GoOn/UncertainWords.cs:72`) (round 6, *C5-1.4*, *O5-F6*). Whether it was taken, carried or
   uncertain is the attempt's, kept by the move's `move_words` and the record's notes, and a take is told by the session's events (19.4, §3.4
   rule 5). Round 4's disposition column is withdrawn.
3. *Read by every newer keeping door* inside its own exclusive step: `SaidKeys.Settled(records, key)`, by key over `records`, **the line's
   records**: the record the say names and its predecessors back along `from`, one probe by id each, from row p, and from d-ii the records the
   say door's head walk visits forward too, a record already walked ending either walk (round 7, *C6-1.3*, *O6-F1*: round 6 read only the
   records from the one named forward, so the shell's pump, forwarding a word whose say's answer it lost to the conversation its record went on
   in, `Modules/Loop/SessionWords.cs:773-799`, `:843-863`, kept it twice where its first record had taken it; the Opus reader's smallest change
   is taken in its second form, the walk back along `from`, which needs no comparison of heads); with the index `said_settled_key` on the key
   and the record, in the say door, the helper desk (by `helper:<id>`, where today it reads the helper's waiting word) and the partner desk,
   **each asking it first**, before any refusal but the record's absence, so the say door no longer answers `Never` and `running` before the
   key (`Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:941-956`) and a re-say at the head after `Continued` is answered as kept (*O6-F1*); a
   retry whose key the line has settled, or, from d-ii, whose word waits on the line's head, is answered **`Settled`**, its own terminal kind
   with no word, and only a word waiting on the record named is answered `Again` with it, as today (`SessionLedger.cs:956-961`), each to a
   client that sends `settled: true`, an older client answered by today's door (§3.4 rule 5; round 8, *C7-1.1*, *O7-F12*: round 7 answered a
   settled retry `Again` with the settled word's id, which a dispatcher keeps as a new word of the record named and attempts and takes by that
   id alone). **A retry whose word waits on a record the line has left is answered by the door as it stands on the record named** *(O8-F1)*,
   its key read there alone, as today: such a word never goes on where it waits (`Conversations/GoOn/SessionSay.cs:40-45`; `Chat/ChatGoOn.cs:228`;
   19.11), so round 8's `Settled` for it lost the word the shell's pump forwards to the successor after a lost answer
   (`Modules/Loop/SessionWords.cs:789-792`, `:773-779`, `:843-863`), which today the successor keeps and delivers once. The supervisor's door reads no settlement and its release reads its waiting word's id as today (`Loop/Driver.cs:471-473`) (*O7-F12*).
   The walk's records, not `entry`, are the line, so no `entry` index is needed (*O5-F8*), and row p
   lands within the say door's and the `sessions` write's limits (19.13, row k).
4. **The horizon, decided.** D137's and D188's *never sent again by itself* has two halves. **The attempt's half has no horizon**: a word
   attempted is never re-sent by itself, whatever its age, since its mark is on the record and an uncertain word leaves it with the `lost` note
   (`GoOn/UncertainWords.cs:25-94`). **The retry's half**, a producer saying again a word whose answer it lost, **holds for seven days after the
   word left `said`**; past them a retry is a new word, said on its line under its key as any word is. Two producers retry across a restart
   (round 7, *O6-F14*: round 6 named the shell alone): the shell, reading its held words at its come-up (`Modules/Loop/SessionWords.cs:961-985` at
   `abf03ea26`), a word it held being one whose say it could not finish; and the ended limit move's box take-up, which says its composed
   message to the new part again under `limit-move/<session>` at each come-up until the new part shows a `User` event
   (`Chat/LimitMove.cs:394-429`, `:408`, `:419-420`), retired with the box by row f-vi. So the case past the horizon is a say kept with its
   answer lost, the word taken, and the shell then down for more than a week, or a box left that long: that word may be said twice, and is
   said as a new word, never hidden. The trigger trims **by time alone** in its own statement, over an
   index on the moment, and keeps no count cap, so a settlement is answered for seven days after the word last left `said`, however many others
   are written that week (round 6, *C5-1.2*: round 5's cap of 50,000 rows could trim one inside the week, the 50,001st recent settlement
   evicting the oldest); the table's size is a week's takes, a row of a few ids and a moment, which row p measures on the install and lands
   within.
5. *An older door* reads no settlement: its keyed hand retried after a take keeps a second word, as today (19.12). *An older client*, which
   sends no `settled`, meets a newer door that answers it as today's does, so it too keeps a second word after a take, and is never handed an
   id of another record (round 8, *C7-1.1*).

**Proof**, row p: a producer's retry after the take answered `Settled`, with no word, within seven days and kept as a new word past them;
a retry paused after its check while another dispatcher takes the word, one word; a helper's and a partner's hand retried after its take, one
word; equal keys on unrelated records two words; an older connector's own take statement, run in the test over the migrated database, writing
its settlement; a take-back settling its word, the producer's retry then answered as kept; round 6's: a settlement six days and twenty-three
hours old answered while more than 50,000 others are written that week, and one seven days and a minute old trimmed; a record with no stored
`entry` settling and answering its word (*C5-1.2*, *C5-3.2*); round 7's: a word planted on two records by the test's own statements, as an
open would leave it, settling a row on each, its retry to either answered as kept; Codex's case, a say kept with its answer lost, the word
taken on its record, the record continued by a successor the test's own open statement makes, and the retry forwarded to the successor
answered as kept; the same retry to the old record once it runs again answered as kept, never refused `running`; equal word ids on two
unrelated records, `answer` among them, each settling and answering its own (*C6-1.2*, *C6-1.3*, *O6-F1*); round 8's, Codex's reused id
through both real dispatchers: a word K kept on A and taken, a successor B the test's own open statement makes holding another word whose id
the test sets to K's, and K's retry kept through a fresh `ChatTurns` and, in another run, a fresh `DrivenInbox` of B: answered `Settled`,
nothing queued, steered, attempted or taken, B's word still waiting unattempted, K's text never reaching the stand-in agent, and no `User`
event written by the shell's `Show`; the same retry from an older client, sending no `settled`, keeping a second word under a new id as
today, B's word untouched; a helper's and a partner's hand retried after its take, nothing dispatched and no event recorded (*C7-1.1*,
*O7-F12*); the named changes' (*O8-F1*): K said through the pump to A and kept, its answer lost, a briefed move and in another run a live
limit move having read A's words before the keep going on in B, and K forwarded to B kept there and delivered once, never `Settled`, A's
copy never going on; a helper's hand retried after its asker went on, kept once on the newest asker. Row d-ii, once words move: a retry
through two successors one word; a keyless word moved and settled by its id; a word moved from a record with no stored `entry` to a successor
holding one, taken there, its retry to the old record answered as kept (*C5-1.2*); Codex's case with the keyed open moving the word, and a
re-say at the head after `Continued`, each one word (*C6-1.3*, *O6-F1*); an older build's unkeyed successor, K left waiting on the old
record and its retry forwarded to the head kept and delivered there once (*O8-F1*). **To drop:** the settlement is row
p's one service addition; without it a retry after a take keeps a second word, the remainder of COWORK1b's trap 19, while row p's attempts and
dispositions, the client's, stand alone.

### 19.3 `Lines.Forward`, withdrawn

**The question** (§13.4 step 5; X3-3.4): where the line's head has moved past a move's destination, its unattempted words were to follow the head
by a new service step. **Only an older build moves it so**: a newer build's course makes no second move from the move's old part, whose row is
keyed by it (19.1), and none from its destination until the move has ended, `delivered`, `held` or `fell-back`, a precondition the course reads
(§3.1); an older build opens a successor before it delivers and takes (`Chat/BriefedMove.cs:130-158`). Two of today's doors cannot stand in for the step: a
word said again at the head under its producer key is answered as already kept while it still waits on the destination, since the settlement
reads the line (19.2), and taken off first it is lost across a crash.

**Settled, the smaller protocol.** No new step. The move is `held` for the person (reason `superseded`), its note naming the destination, the
head and the destination's waiting words; attempted ones are settled uncertain and said, as before. **What the hold offers** (round 6,
*O5-F14*): the person's own words alone, each to be taken back from the destination (SAIDBACK1a's take-back) and said at the head, two of
today's presses, the hold naming the files said with each, which the person attaches again: a word's files are kept in the folder of the
record it was kept on (`Chat/ChatFiles.cs:90-110`), the destination's once d-ii's copy is made and else the record the open moved it from, and
the hold names that folder (round 7, *O6-F8*: round 6 put them in the destination's folder, which holds none of a moved word's until the copy);
another author's words (a helper's answer, an agent's findings, Daoris's) stay on the destination as claims, never offered as the
person's to say (D188 point 4); and for each word the hold says whether the head's events already show it, since an older briefed move may have
said its copy live to its successor (`Chat/BriefedMove.cs:142-143`): **per word** where an event carries the word's id, as a newer move's
delivery records it (§3.4 rule 3), and **per part** for an older build's copy, one joined event with no id (`Chat/ChatRunner.cs:2588-2604`), as
the take-up's guard reads any `User` event of the new part (`Chat/LimitMove.cs:408`) (round 7, *O6-F2*: round 6 said per word of a copy that
carries no id). The hold closes `resolved` by §3.3's one rule, at the person's press through one of its doors, or at the come-up or look after
nothing it names still waits on the destination (round 7, *O6-F7*). Codex's concern, that nothing is delivered to a part that is not the head, is met by the hold as it was by the step.
**Proof**, rows f-ii to f-vi: an older build opening a successor of the destination and crashing before it moved the destination's words; the
newer build back delivering nothing to the destination and holding the move with the words named, each said once by the person's press; round
6's: the same with the older build live, never crashing, advancing the head while the newer build runs (*C5-1.3*); a word of another author
kept as a claim, a word with files, and one the head's events show, each said so (*O5-F14*); round 7's: a moved word's files named in the
record left's folder where the copy was not yet made, and in the destination's where it was (*O6-F8*); an older build's copy shown per part
(*O6-F2*); the hold closed `resolved` at the next look once the person's take-backs leave nothing it names waiting (*O6-F7*). **Dropping it, as done here, costs** the person's
presses wherever an older build moves a line's head past a newer move's destination, crashing or not, beside a newer build or after a
downgrade (round 6, *C5-1.3*: round 5 said only a downgrade and a crash together made it), and a word's files attached again; keeping it would
be one service step reusing d-ii's word move, with an idempotency key of its own.

### 19.4 The `delivered` window

**The question** (O4-12 item 5): round 4 wrote each word's disposition and `delivered` before the service took the word (§13.4 step 4), so across a
downgrade a word the move called delivered still waited, marked attempted, and an older build's come-up settled it as uncertain.

**Settled, by order.** The take comes first. Once the turn took a word, the dispatcher has the service take it off `said`, whose trigger settles it
(19.2), and only then does one desktop transaction write `delivered`. So no word waits on `said` while its move says
`delivered`, and the window across a downgrade is today's for every word, between its attempt and its take (`Chat/ChatTurns.cs:1224-1233`), which
an older build's come-up settles as uncertain as it does today (`GoOn/UncertainWords.cs:25-94`), but for a moved word's `lost` note, which
that come-up does not write once d-ii records the word's own event before the write (`:84-91`, `:97-98`), the newer build's return holding
the move and offering the person's *left* words for saying again (§13.5's `delivering` row; *O8-F8*). **A crash between is finished from the move's
own `sent`, never from the settlement or a session event** (round 6, *C5-1.4*, *O5-F6*: round 5 read a word gone from `said` and settled as
taken, but the person's take-back and an older come-up's uncertain settlement leave `said` by the same statement,
`Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:1312`, `GoOn/UncertainWords.cs:72`; round 7, *C6-1.4*, *C6-3.2*, *O6-F2*: round 6 read the destination's own
`User` event for the word, but the runner records that event before the write, the go-on's opening at `Chat/ChatRunner.cs:3502-3506` before
its prompt at `:3509`, a turn's at `:3517` before `:3519`, the native door's at `:3758` and `:3765` before `processes.Send` at `:3771`, which
can fail, so a crash between the event and the write, then an older come-up's uncertain take-back, closed delivered a word no agent was sent;
`UncertainWords.ShownTaken`, `:96-98`, makes that reading to decide whether its `lost` note is written, `:84-91`, so the move's take-up
writes its own (§3.4 rule 3; round 8, *O7-F4*); and a move's message records one joined event with no id,
`:2588-2604`, `:2641-2647`, so for a conversation move there was no per-word event at all). **The executor writes each moved word's `sent` in
`move_words` as its door's write returns and before the take** (round 8, *O7-F5*): on the ACP door at the prompt's `OnWire`
(`Driver/Agents/Acp/Acp.cs:2109-2113`), whose `wrote` is the conversation's turn's `sent`, `Delivered` then the take
(`Chat/ChatTurns.cs:1226-1233`), a move's composed message carrying no word id (`Chat/LimitMove.cs:353-354`) and the door's `sent` receipt
being recorded only for one that does (`Acp.cs:2120-2122`); on the native driven door as the new part's start returns its process
(`Loop/Driver.cs:3661-3664`), which records no `sent` today; for a driven new part on the protocol door (`Loop/Driver.cs:3730`, `:3810`), at
its first prompt's write return, outside `ChatTurns`: the run's `prompted` (`Acp.cs:797-800`, `:846-852`) from `SendPromptAsync`'s `OnWire`
(`:2066`), and a transient episode's try's `OnWire` (`:1535`) (*O8-F7*); round 7's `Loop/DriverContinuations.cs:1252-1258` is the native go-on's resumed
run, which writes no move record. So a word with `sent` was written to its agent
and is *taken*, taken off by the move's take-up where it still waits and never written again; a word gone from `said` without `sent` is
*left*, a disposition of `move_words` that says another door took it off and the move did not deliver it, never sent again by the move; a
word still waiting, attempted and without `sent`, is uncertain, settled and said as any attempted word is. The move closes `delivered` when
every word it moved has `sent`, and is `held` for the person, naming its *left* and uncertain words and its begun effect, otherwise; a `sent`
whose transaction failed makes the word read *left*, the conservative side. The word's `User` event, recorded per word by the delivery
(§3.4 rule 3), says on the line where the destination shows it asked, and nothing more. **Proof**, rows f-ii to f-vi, the delivery cut at
each boundary: after the word's `User` event and before the write, the native send failing or the process killed there, then an older
come-up's uncertain take-back: *left* at the return, the move held, never closed delivered (*C6-1.4*); after the write returned and before
`sent`: held, the word uncertain or *left*; after `sent` and before the take: the come-up taking it off as taken and closing the move
delivered, nothing written again, and the same with an older come-up's take-back between; after the take and before `delivered`: closed
delivered by `sent`, an older build's come-up at that moment finding nothing on `said`; round 6's: a crash at `delivering` before the write,
the word then taken back by the person, and in another run settled uncertain by an older come-up, each read *left* at the return, the move
held and nothing delivered again (*C5-1.4*). **To drop:** nothing; it is an order and a reading.

### 19.5 The keyed open: its retention, and an older host's lost open

**The code.** The open's requests name no key (`OpenSessionRequest` and `OpenChatRequest`, `Http/ApiContracts.cs:607-613`), and the service sets
no `JsonUnmappedMemberHandling`, so an older host's binder skips an `opens` member it does not know and opens a new record each time. Successors
are read newest first by `from` (`Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:995-1002`), never unique.

**Settled.**
1. *Retention by trigger, a row kept until its record left goes* (round 6, *C5-1.5*). One trigger `AFTER DELETE ON sessions` deletes the rows whose
   record left is the deleted record, and marks `gone` the rows whose opened record it is, clearing their words, so every writer's delete moves
   them, an older build's included (`Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:1524-1533` deletes by id and knows no desktop move), and the table is bounded by the
   records the home keeps. No time horizon. A repeat open with a `gone` row's key is answered `gone`, a terminal answer on which the desktop
   closes its move `gone`, said on the line, and never opens again. Round 5 let a row go with its opened record, and Codex's schedule broke it: a
   desktop loses the open's answer and stays down while an older build finishes the move and deletes the destination, then asks the key again,
   finds no row and opens a second destination; the move was not over because the service's record was gone.
2. *The keyed open's own check* (round 6, *O5-F2*), **in this order** (round 7, *O6-F5*: round 6 named no order, and a repeat of a committed
   open, whose record left is no longer the head, would have been refused): the key is looked up in `session_opens` first and a repeat answered
   from its row; only then does the step refuse unless the record left stands, is its line's head (`Lines.HeadOf`, row d-i: no record `from`
   it and, for a driven part, no later run of its quest here) and is not active, answering the head, or `gone` for a record left that does not
   stand. The ledger's step refuses every move of a record a keyed open left out of an ended or parked state, the go-on's (`GoOnAsync`,
   `Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:1726`), the move back to the person of an ended record that waited on a helper (`BackToPersonAsync`,
   `:1727-1728`, `:1901`) and a park's move to working (round 7, *O6-F6*: round 6 named the go-on alone, 19.1 point 1), so a resume and a keyed
   open of one record are one or the other.
3. *An older host is told before the open, and by its answer.* The desktop reads the host's protocol marks, kept per host run (19.12; *C5-1.12*).
   Where the host keeps no `opens`, the move is made as today's moves are (the executor reads the words, opens, delivers and takes them), no key is
   written into the move's phase, and a lost answer is held for the person with the candidates named, the records `from` the one left, never
   adopting one. A host whose marks keep `opens` answers the open with its run, the key's echo and its transfer, the words it moved (none at
   `opens` 1, d-i's); a first answer without the echo is read as an older host's, its record new and nothing moved, and the move goes on as
   today's. **A lost answer is asked again only after `GET /api/protocol` is read again**, and **a retried open answered without the echo is
   held for the person**, the records `from` the one left named, never made as today's (round 7, *O6-F4*: round 6 retried under the marks it
   had, so where the host died mid-open after making the destination and moving its words, and an older host replaced it, the retry opened a
   second destination). So *never twice* is a newer host's guarantee alone, as X-3.5 has it.

**Proof**, row d-i: a repeat open with the key answered from `session_opens` after a word planted on its opened record was taken off `said`;
the opened record deleted by an older build's own statement, then the key asked again: answered `gone`, never opened again (*C5-1.5*); the
record left deleted, its rows gone and the key refused; a keyed open of an active record, of one with a successor and of a driven part whose
quest ran again, each refused with the head (*O5-F2*); against a stand-in host with no marks, no key sent, the move made as today, a lost answer
held; and a host replaced under the shell by one with no marks, the next answer naming no run and the marks read again before the next keyed
step (*C5-1.12*); round 7's: a repeat of a committed open whose record left is no longer the head, answered from its row (*O6-F5*); an ended
record that waited on a helper moved back to the person, refused once a keyed open left it (*O6-F6*); an open's answer lost as its host dies
and a stand-in with no marks takes its place, the retry sent only after the marks are read again and, answered without the echo, held (*O6-F4*).
**To drop:** the keyed open is what makes a move's destination one; without it every lost answer is held, as against an older host.

### 19.6 News keys, the occurrences that change no state among them

**The code.** A conversation's turn ends with nothing written to its record: the record goes `working` as it starts and moves again only as it
concludes (`Chat/ChatRunner.cs:2893`, `:3824-3833`). Its answers are told apart today by a moment held in memory, `ChatQueue.LastTurnEnded`
(`Driver/Conversations/Domain/ChatQueue.cs:31-46`, set at `Chat/ChatTurns.cs:1276`), compared by the attention watch
(`Driver/Quests/Domain/Attention.cs:333-345`; `Chat/CurveMove.cs:36-37`), and the notice `answered-<session>` is renewed for each
(`Driver/Platform/Notices.cs:108-113`, `:142-152`). The one durable handle a turn has is its `turn` event's `seq` in the session's event file
(`Driver/Sessions/Domain/SessionEvents.cs:33-34`, `:189-190`; `Driver/Sessions/Events/SessionEventLog.cs:39-64`), by which the usage history
already keys turns (`Driver/Accounts/Usage/UsageHistory.cs:291`).

**Settled: an occurrence's key is the durable id it was minted with, and one path tells it.** A record event is keyed by the store's identity
and its journal `position` (round 6, *O5-F9*: a position alone carries no store). A turn's answer is keyed by its session, the shell's process
run (its `store_runs` id) and the moment its queue recorded the turn's end, `ChatQueue.LastTurnEnded` (set at `Chat/ChatTurns.cs:1276`, read by
the judgement at `ChatRunner.cs:1673`), told by the shell that runs the conversation at the judgement it makes today (`ChatRunner.cs:1655-1675`);
a restart ends the conversation's run as it does today, so no answer is told for a run that ended, as none is for a held call. **Round 5's key
was the `seq` of the `turn` event, which is not durable** (round 6, *C5-1.6*, *O5-F9*): `SessionEvents` caches each session's last `seq` per
instance and locks that instance alone (`Driver/Sessions/Events/SessionEventLog.cs:29-30`, `:45`), so two shells taking turns on one conversation
can number two events alike and an answer is dropped, and `Keep` lets an append fail while the conversation goes on (`:84-94`), leaving no
event to key. The run and the moment need neither the file nor its numbering; a moment repeated within one session's run, two turns ending
within one reading of `DateTimeOffset.UtcNow` (`Chat/ChatTurns.cs:1276`), which promises no unique value, or a clock stepped back, drops the
later answer's telling and never repeats one (round 7, *C6-1.8*: round 6 said only a clock stepped back could repeat it). A Confirm is keyed by its press id (`Modules/Platform/PipeConfirmations.cs:63-69`); a held call by its request
within its process's run; an account's wait by the account and its cool-off's `until`; the look's news by its code and subject, a repeat inside
the week dropped as today (`Driver/Platform/LookSaid.cs:223-236`). **The transition number and `session_transitions` are withdrawn.** They were
there so that a reconciliation below the journal's floor could key an end as the journal did (X3-3.6); no reconciliation tells news now, so one
path gives one key. A teller whose cursor is below the floor reads its records whole for state, tells nothing of the gap, and says its history
is incomplete since its cursor (§4.1); the waits it would have told of stay in *What needs you*, judged from the records. The visible notice
keeps its id, one per conversation, renewed as today; the said-set holds the occurrence.

**Proof**, row m: a conversation answering three times with no change of its record, each answer told once by its session, the shell's run and
its turn's end; the same with two shells running its turns one after the other, and with an append failing between two turns (*C5-1.6*); two
turns ending at one reading of a held clock, the second's telling dropped and none repeated (*C6-1.8*); a crash
between a claim and its balloon telling nothing twice; an end, a reopen and an end told as two positions; a position of another store told as
its own (*O5-F9*); a teller below the floor telling none of the gap and saying so; two tellers reading one row, told once. **Measured** by row k
on the install: how many rows a week journals, so the gap a teller below the floor misses is known (under 7,000 rows a day, 50,000 rows are more
than the notices' week). **To drop:** the reconciliation path is dropped here; what it told is news of the gap below the floor, and the gap is
not always older than the notices' week (round 6, *C5-2.2*): a burst of more than 50,000 journal rows inside a week trims news a teller had not
yet read, recent news among it, which the teller's *incomplete since* says and the waits in *What needs you* keep, and row k's weekly count
says how near the install comes to it.

### 19.7 The confirmation's *settled*: a headless start and a silent swap

**The code.** The swap journal is the install's, `<install>/update/swap.json` (`Driver/Setup/StagedBuild.cs:183`, `:198`, `:222`), its phases
`swapping`, `started`, `confirmed`, `installed`, `rolled-back` and `refused` (`:141-160`). The window confirms a `started` journal with no reason
(`:474-481`) from `InstallUpdater.StartedAsync`, synchronously before its first wait (`Modules/Update/InstallUpdater.cs:129-139`), in the app's
`OnStarting`, before its loop starts (`App/Program.cs:307-335`). The launcher waits a minute on the window it started: confirmed, the build is
installed; the window gone first, it is rolled back; still silent at the minute, it is installed with `confirmed: false`
(`Driver/Setup/InstallSwap.cs:296-330`), and a build that confirmed is never rolled back after (D139). The launcher holds the home's Swap lease
through that wait (`Launcher/Program.cs:90-107`), and a headless loop waits thirty seconds for it and then refuses (`Host/Program.cs:870-886`), as
a verb does (`HomeLease.ForVerb`). A headless start confirms nothing.

**Settled.** A start's confirmation is settled once **its install's swap journal is at no phase the launcher can still roll back from**: no
journal, no install (a workspace build), or `confirmed`, `installed` (confirmed or not), `rolled-back` or `refused`. `swapping` and `started` are
not settled, a `started` with a reason being a roll-back stopped part way, which the launcher finishes as one (`StagedBuild.cs:470-477`). **A
journal that is there and does not read is not settled** (round 6, *C5-1.7*): `StagedBuild.ReadJournal` answers null both for no journal and for
one that does not read (`StagedBuild.cs:357-361`), so the guard reads the file's presence apart and holds every import and stamp on an
unreadable one, saying so, until it reads. The
window's come-up meets it settled, having confirmed before its loop; a headless start and a verb meet it settled or do not run, the swap's lease
keeping them out until the swap ends; the silent swap is settled, being installed. It is read at each come-up and at each look until it is
settled, which in practice is the first read, and the read stays because a confirmation's write can fail. No build identity is compared, and none
is needed: the journal is the install's, and the launcher waits on the one window it started (`InstallSwap.cs:298`, `:309`). **The rollback
window**, which §13.5 and row v name, is therefore at most the minute before a confirmation: D139 keeps the build before only until the new one
confirms, so after a confirmed start an older build meets this home only as a second install's or as one the person published in place, which no
check refuses for being older (`StagedBuild.Check`, `StagedBuild.cs:299-349`); row v's condition is restated by the matrix (19.12).

**Proof**, row z-i: a rollback before the confirmation meeting every file as it was; a start finding `started` importing nothing and raising no
stamp; one finding a journal that does not read importing nothing and saying so (*C5-1.7*); the silent swap's start importing at its first look;
a headless loop beside a swap waiting, then finding it settled. **To drop:** the guard
almost never bites, since the window confirms before its loop; it costs one read, and without it a start whose confirmation failed to be written
could import what a roll-back then meets moved.

### 19.8 The authority families, file by file, and a narrowing's order across a crash and across two shells

**Each file's older reader, and what it reads in a missing file.** A family is *set aside* where its older reader reads a missing file as
narrower; otherwise it is *left in place, its narrowings written through* (§13.5).

| File (row) | Its older reader, the file absent | Class | Written through |
|---|---|---|---|
| `data/standing-confirmation.json` (z-iv) | off, no appointment (`Modules/Platform/StandingConfirmation.cs:543-587`, `:560-563`; its contract `:267-271`) | set aside | — |
| `data/plugin-approvals.json` (z-xi) | nothing approved (`Driver/Plugins/HelpGrants.cs:156-183`, read as none at `:64`) | set aside | — |
| `data/driver.json` (z-ii) | `DriverConfig.Empty`, driving nothing (`Driver/Platform/DriverConfig.cs:940-978`, `:71-75`); set aside, an older build would lose every setting | left in place | `drivable`, a repository removed (absent, nothing driven: `:1610`; `Driver/Quests/Domain/Planner.cs:1946-1949`); `access`, a pause made stricter (absent, each pause's default: `Driver/Sessions/Domain/AccessRules.cs:121-128`, `:187-200`); `standing`, an answer withdrawn, since it is the owner's words handed beneath a quest (`Loop/Driver.cs:4554-4555`). `trees` grants nothing, choosing only a session's own worktree (`DriverConfig.cs:1587-1588`), so round 4's count of it among the authority keys is withdrawn and it is offered back as an import like any key |
| `data/permissions.json` (z-xi) | `PermissionFile.Empty`, the four shipped defaults on and the person's rules lost (`Driver/Agents/Permissions/Permissions.cs:258-297`, `:111-177`), so a deny lost is wider; the GitDoor's own reader reads it as `no-push` on (`Driver/Platform/GitAccessRules.cs:52-66`) | left in place | a deny added, an allow removed, a shipped default turned back on |
| `data/configured-access.json` (z-xi) | none chosen, every checkout on (`Driver/Help/ConfiguredAccess.cs:221-240`, `:106`; `Driver/Help/HelpCheckouts.cs:146`), wider for checkouts | left in place | a checkout turned off; a housekeeping or settings grant withdrawn |
| `data/git-identities.json` (z-xi) | no identity, the act on the person's own credentials (`Driver/Platform/GitIdentities.cs:89-109`; `Driver/Platform/GitDoors.cs:93-102`, `:127-129`) | left in place | an identity removed, so no older door serves a key the person withdrew |
| `data/plugins.json` (z-xi) | nothing disabled, every installed plugin on (`Driver/Plugins/PluginState.cs:15-41`), wider | left in place | a plugin disabled |

**A narrowing's order across a crash** (X4-3.2). The backup first, then the store. The family's writer narrows the backup under the file's own lock
and in its own format, then commits the store's narrowing in one transaction that records the backup's new SHA-256 as its import's baseline. A
crash between leaves the backup narrower than the store, never wider; the come-up's comparison (§13.5) finds the backup changed and, the change
being a narrowing, applies it as an import without a press, which only a widening needs, so the store catches up. The press is answered done only
once the store has committed. A widening is committed to the store alone and never written through. The counterexample to the other order: the
store committed, a crash, and an older build started on the wider backup reads the revoke back.

**Across two shells.** The store is one transaction across processes and the backup's lock is the file's, so a narrowing made in either shell is the
other's at its next read, every reader reading the store rather than a copy in memory, woken by the wake file (§13.4). Appointments are held only
by the instance that serves the home's pipe in its Windows session (`InstancePipe.cs:575-590`), so a second shell in that session holds none, and a
role's narrowing never needs to reach another instance's memory (19.9).

**Proof**, rows z-ii, z-iv and z-xi: for each file left in place, a narrowing in the store followed by an older build's start finding it narrowed; a
crash between the backup's write and the store's commit, the come-up applying the narrowing with no press; a widening in the store, the backup
unchanged and an older build reading it narrower; for each file set aside, an older build's start finding it absent and reading it off; and
(round 6, *C5-1.8*) two different narrowings interleaved between one's backup write and its store commit, from two shells, neither the value nor
its import's baseline losing the other's. **To
drop:** if Part B leaves z-ii, z-iv and z-xi to a later design (19.15), this table is that design's input, and nothing in Part B rehomes an
authority record; both makers' reads of round 5 said yes, so it is that design's, with the interleaved case among its proofs. Row w-s's own
writes keep this order's direction, the file before the store; its newer writers serialize on the staffing's record lock, which lives with
its holder, and never on `driver.json`'s `KeepLock`, which is taken as stale a minute after it was taken, its holder alive or not
(`Driver/Platform/KeepLock.cs:38`, `:120`), so against an older writer two windows are left, decided in 19.15 (round 8, *O7-F13*, *C7-3.1*:
round 7 still said its writes serialize under the file's lock).

### 19.9 Row w-0, and the appointed half of the role catalogue

**The code.** This build offers no appointed role and no grant: `OfferedInThisBuild`, `SupervisorOfferedInThisBuild` and
`StewardOfferedInThisBuild` are false (`Modules/Platform/StandingConfirmation.cs:314`, `:330`, `:336`), gating `SET_APPOINTMENT`, the
supervisor's two acts and `ROLE_ACT` until SUPERVISOR1a-iii and STEWARDROLE1d turn them on. An appointment's effective grant is its instance's
memory (the holder key's hash, its binding and its admitted acts, `:374-412`), and every appointment read at a start is dormant (`:578-581`).

**Settled, should it be kept: the lowering computed, never written.** An act is admitted only within the grant, the role's configuration as it
stands, and **every revision of the role since the appointment's *Appoint***, the appointment keeping the role's revision at *Appoint*, and
`role_revisions`, never rewritten, keeping each revision while an appointment from it stands. Appointed with `answer` and `defer` at revision 1,
narrowed to `answer` at 2 and restored at 3: the revisions' intersection is `answer`, so `defer` waits for the next *Appoint* (X3-2.2's case). No
narrowing writes into a live appointment, so nothing is ordered across a crash and nothing must reach another instance's memory: each instance
reads the store's revisions at admission and at the run. It replaces round 4's write of every narrowing into each live grant in the standing
confirmation's one mutation step, which reaches only its own instance (`:378`, `:390`; X4-3.2).

**Proposed: leave it to a later design** (19.15). Rows w-0, t and y-ii, and z-iv with them, serve appointments no build can make yet. Part B's
catalogue holds working roles alone, its capabilities the work codes; the supervisor and the steward stay the shell's, in code, outside the
catalogue, since the driver cannot name the act vocabulary without w-0 (round 6, *O5-F5*, *C5-2.5*: round 5 kept an appointed seed read per id
and the act vocabulary in row w); and the judge refuses a record of the appointed shape (`appointed-not-configurable`) before it judges anything
else of it. **Cost:** the owner's direction that roles be configured (the brief §8) is met in Part B for working roles,
and for appointed roles in the design read when the first appointment is offered; D208 point 1 and D209 points 1 to 4 keep their text; the two
role doors stay two; nothing a person can do today changes. **Gain:** three ⚖ rows and the grant's lifecycle leave Part B, and this item's
settlement is that design's input, with a case it must prove (round 6, *C5-1.9*): a role id removed and made again while an appointment from
its first life stands, its revision history never resetting the ceiling, so `role_revisions` keeps every revision of an id across its removal.
Both makers' reads of round 5 said yes to the cut.

### 19.10 The GitDoor's start with SQLite

**The code.** The door is framework-dependent and references nothing (`GitDoor/Daoris.Desktop.GitDoor.csproj:3-16`;
`tools/desktop-publish.mjs:460-463`), compiling fourteen of the driver's files (`:20-37`); git starts one per ssh invocation and per remote
helper's run (D198 points 2 and 11; `Driver/Platform/GitDoors.cs:78-103`, `:361-446`). Each act it serves reads, whole, `git-identities.json`,
`driver.json`'s three pointer keys (`Driver/Platform/GitIdentityUse.cs:55-62`, `:80-92`), `run/git/checkouts.json` and `tools.json`
(`GitDoors.cs:125-138`, `:224-239`), and a write without a grant reads `permissions.json` (`:463-472`). D198 measured the door's own start at
about 0.25 s on a loaded machine and a whole `ls-remote` at about 1.7 s (`docs/decisions/D198.md:290-293`); nothing times it today.

**Measured, and held until then.** Row z-i measures a door's start with `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite` opening a store read-only against today's, cold
and warm, and beside a busy writer and a write-ahead log's checkpoint (round 6, *C5-1.10*), at p95 on the install; the limit stays a tenth. **Until a door passes it, no family the door reads moves into the store**, and the door
takes no package; past the limit those families stay files, or their writers keep a file the door reads, a choice for the design that moves them.
With 19.15's proposal no family the door reads moves in Part B (the pointers are `driver.json`'s, z-ii's; the identities and permissions z-xi's;
the tools z-vii's), so **Part B's door opens no store**, z-i adds no package to it, and this design amends no part of D198. **To drop:** nothing of
Part B needs the door in the store.

### 19.11 Row o's shape

**The code.** An older shell's come-up resumes an ended chat with words waiting unless a successor continues it (`Chat/ChatGoOn.cs:225-230`,
through `WordsNever.ContinuedIn`), its limit holds it (`:232-237`) or every waiting word is marked (`:247`; `GoOn/GoOnMarks.cs:131-134`), and its
sweep's take-up does the same (`:136-168`). HANDWORDS1 holds a conversation's words while it is handed over because *the successor's record opens
before the take is done* (`Modules/Loop/SessionWords.cs:455-467`, `TakeWaits` at `:544-545`): a successor and a waiting word on its old part at
once, which an older sweep skips and nothing delivers.

**Settled: the shape is `said` itself, on the old part, where the host moves words at its open.** Against a host whose marks keep `opens` and the
head (19.12), d-ii's open makes the successor and moves the old part's waiting words in one exclusive step, and the say door refuses a word to the
old part after it, naming the head, so the two never coexist. Before the open, an older shell's resume of the old part does what it would have
done had the move never been decided, and the newer build back closes the move `superseded` (§13.5's first row), the words having gone on once,
with the old part. So no shape of its own is needed. Against a host without those marks, the shell keeps today's hold (`TakeWaits`, the held
words and `held-words.json`). Round 4's *where no such shape exists the row waits* is withdrawn. **What `said` cannot do** (round 6, *C5-1.11*,
*C5-2.4*): it cannot take back a copy an older executor already made. An older briefed move reads its words before its own open
(`Chat/BriefedMove.cs:99`), which names no key, and delivers that copy (`:142-143`) before its take (`:158`), so where it pauses before its
open and goes on after a newer move's destination has taken its words and ended, both builds deliver; row o asserts that bound (§3.4 rule 4,
19.1 point 2) and *once* only where the service's steps decide. **Proof**, row o: an older shell's own come-up
and its sweep run over a word kept for a moving record at each phase, before the open and after, resuming nothing in a successor's tree and
delivering each word once; an older executor paused after its read and before its open and resumed after the newer destination ended, each
build's delivery once and nothing a third time (*C5-1.11*); the same against a stand-in host without the marks, the hold kept. **To drop:** `held-words.json` stays a second home
of the words a winding-up or moving record waits for, with row b's identity, and nothing is lost; row o is small now and stays.

### 19.12 The version matrix

**How the newer side tells** (round 6, *C5-1.12*, *O5-F12*). The service answers `GET /api/protocol`, added by row p, with **its run**, an id
the host draws at its start, and the marks it keeps, each a version: `settled` 1 (row p: retries answered from the settlement, a key the
line has settled, or from d-ii one waiting at its head, answered `Settled` to a say or hand that sends `settled: true`, round 8, *C7-1.1*,
*O8-F1*), `opens` 1 (row
d-i: a keyed open answered from `session_opens`, its transfer empty) and 2 (row d-ii: the open moves the old part's words), `head` 1 (row d-ii:
the say door's `Continued`), `journal` 1 (row k). Every answer of a step a mark changes (the open, the say, the take, the attempt, the change
stream) carries the host's run, a header an older host never writes; the desktop keeps the marks by that run and reads them again whenever an
answer names another run or none, before its next step that depends on one, and a host that answers no `GET /api/protocol` keeps none. A step
already sent to a replaced host is read by its answer: a first keyed open answered without the key's echo is an older host's open, its record
new and nothing moved (19.5); and a step whose answer was lost, which no answer names a run for, is asked again only after
`GET /api/protocol` is read again, a retried open answered without the echo held for the person (round 7, *O6-F4*: a host that dies mid-open
loses the answer that would have named its successor's run). Round 5 kept the marks by the running entry's pid and start, which are the desktop's own process, written by the desktop
(`Driver/Platform/RunningAddress.cs:8`, `:65-67`), so a host replaced under the same shell kept its predecessor's marks. A connector reads no
marks: it writes the database itself, and what binds it is the tables' triggers.

**When an older build meets a newer one.** After a confirmed update none of this install's older processes runs: the launcher refuses a swap while
anything runs from `app/`, its host and connectors among them (`Launcher/Program.cs:72`, `:82-87`), and keeps no build past the confirmation
(19.7). So an older build writes beside a newer one only as a second install on the home, or as an older build the person published in place.

| Protocol (row) | A newer desktop, an older host | An older desktop, a newer host | An older writer over the migrated database (a connector or host from before the table) |
|---|---|---|---|
| The keyed open, `session_opens` (d-i) | no `opens` mark: no key sent, the move made as today, a lost answer held (19.5); `opens` 1 alone: the key sent for its replay, the words read, delivered and taken by the executor as today, the open's transfer empty | no key sent: opened as today, no row; its go-on refused at the ledger's step once a keyed open left its record (19.1) | never opens with a key; its delete moves the rows, by trigger (19.5) |
| The head and `Continued` (d-ii) | no `head` mark: the driver's `WordsNever.ContinuedIn`, chats alone, as today (`Conversations/GoOn/SessionSay.cs:54-58`) | `Continued` read as a refusal it does not know, the service's sentence naming the head said (`SessionSay.cs:205-210`) | its say door judges no head, so a word it keeps on a superseded part waits there, as today |
| The open moving words (d-ii), and row o's keeps | `opens` below 2: the move reads and delivers the words itself and the shell holds as today (19.11) | its opens move nothing | moves nothing |
| The attempt's answer (p) | the client's change alone: every host with the door answers `unmarked` (`Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:1218-1246`); a host without the door, a word unmarked, not sent and said (`Driver/Service/ServiceClient.cs:564-567`) | unchanged | unchanged |
| `said_settled` (p) | no `settled` mark: a retry after a take keeps a second word, as today, the request's `settled` field read by nothing (no member of `SayRequest`, `Http/ApiContracts.cs:485`, and an unknown member ignored) | its takes settled by trigger; its retries, sent without `settled`, answered by today's door, its refusals first and its key read on the named record's waiting words alone, so a retry after a take keeps a second word, as today, and no answer hands it an id of another record (round 8, *C7-1.1*: round 7 answered them from the settlement with an `Again` such a client keeps as a new word) | its takes settled by trigger; its own keyed doors read none, so its retry after a take keeps a second word, as today (`HelperDesk.cs:180-189`) |
| The journal (k) | no `journal` mark: each reader keeps its old surface as its adapter, the attention watch's snapshot and `onEnded` (§4.3) | never hears the `journal` family, which the stream names only to a reader that asks for it: an older client's stream reader would wake its `ledger` topic for any family named (`Driver/Service/LedgerChanges.cs:66-70`, `:107-115`), only its family-filtering readers dropping one (`Modules/Loop/QuestsStateSlice.cs:132-138`), so round 5's *passes it over* was wrong (round 6, *O5-F10*); row k runs an older client's own reader to prove it | its statements journal through the triggers, whose inserts trim it |
| The head's indexes (d-ii, 19.13) | — | invisible to it | maintained by its statements |

Nothing keeps an older writer from writing, and nothing need: what it writes is what it wrote before, and every record it writes is journaled and
settled by the triggers it fires. **Row v's condition**, *once no build in the update's rollback window reads them*, becomes *once no pairing of
this table has a build that writes or needs them*, since D139 keeps no build past a confirmation, shown for each pairing in row v, never read
from one install's confirmation (*C5-3.10*). **Proof**, in each row named: the older build's
own statements run in the test over the migrated database (`compatible-builds.md`), and the newer desktop against a stand-in host that answers no
marks; round 6's: a host replaced under one shell by a stand-in with no marks, the next answer naming no run and the marks read again before
the next keyed step, and the reverse; a d-i-only host (`opens` 1, no `head`), the key sent and the words moved by the executor (*C5-1.12*). **To drop:** the marks are one read; without them rows d-ii and o could not tell an older host before they act.

### 19.13 The say door's head read, and its landing limit

**The code.** The sessions table has no index on `from_session` or on `quest` (its indexes, `Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:504`, `:632`, `:762`); the
successor's read and the quest's last run here scan it (`:995-1002`, `:1041-1051`); and the say door runs inside a `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` step
(`:785-786`), so a scan there holds every writer of the database.

**Settled.** Row d-i adds `sessions_from` on `(from_session, created)` and `sessions_quest` on `(quest, created)` in the service's migration,
with `Lines.HeadOf`, which its keyed open's check reads (round 6, *O5-F2*), invisible to an older build's statements, which maintain them.
`Lines.HeadOf` asks one indexed probe on `sessions_from` of a record with no successor, and for a driven part with none **a second, on
`sessions_quest`, for its quest's last run here** (`LastOwnForQuestAsync`, `Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:1041-1051`; round 6, *C5-1.13*: round 5
said one probe for every record with no successor), and one per hop otherwise, a record already walked ending the walk as unplaced. **The
landing limits:** d-i lands with the `sessions` write's p95 within a tenth of before, and d-ii, which reads the walk in the say door, with the
say door's latency at p95 on the install within a tenth of before, measured before and after on the same home, both branches of the walk among
the says measured; past either a row does not land. Row p lands within the same two limits, its trigger on `sessions` and its read in the say
door's step coming before d-ii's indexes and k's limits (*O5-F8*). **Proof**, row d-ii: the head across kinds; a chain of three parts; a cycle
planted by hand ending the walk; a driven part with no successor whose quest ran again here, and one whose quest did not (*C5-1.13*); the
latency before and after. **To drop:** the head is what closes
LIMITWORD1's and HANDWORDS1-i's race; without it the driver's `ContinuedIn`, chats alone, stays.

### 19.14 The chat runner's boundary in Part B's rows

Part A's map places `ChatContext`, `ChatStart`, the pending family and `ConversationWalk` in Course and the chat runner behind Course's port (§14.2).
Rows e, f-i and h write in Course: the course's pure decision in its `Domain/`; the move record and its take-up in Course's family, the take-up asked
first in `ChatGoOn.TakeUpAsync` where `LimitMove.TakeUpBoxesAsync` is asked today (`Chat/ChatGoOn.cs:195`); and each conversation path calling
`LineCourse.Decide` from its own Course file. Each names only Course's types and lower ones, the runner through the port, so they land after
mod-xiv and its port's cut, as §10's rule already orders for any Part B row whose files a move of Part A holds, and each keeps `StructureTests`
whole in its proof, so no runner type crosses the port unseen (round 6, *C5-1.14*). **To drop:** nothing; it is an
order.

### 19.15 What round 5 proposes to drop, and Part B's rows now

**Withdrawn in this round**, each by a smaller protocol that meets both readers' concerns: `Lines.Forward` (19.3), `session_transitions` and the
reconciliation path's news (19.6), the settlement's disposition (19.2), and row o's shape of its own (19.11).

**Proposed for later designs, for both readers' reads**, each with its cost said where it is settled:
1. **The appointed half of the catalogue**: rows w-0, t, y-ii and z-iv (19.9), until the first appointment is offered.
2. **The store's families no Part B protocol reads**: z-ii's general settings (the configuration's keys but `actors`, with D50's amendment for
   them and the configuration's full export and import) and z-vi to z-xiv, each family a row of a design of the store's families once z-i has
   landed and been measured; z-ii, z-ix and z-xi are ⚖. Part B keeps the families its own protocols read: z-i, z-iii (the bindings a-ii and x
   read), the course's moves (f-i), the roles' staffing and the catalogue's working roles (w-s, w) and z-v (the notices m reads). **Cost:** the
   owner's direction that configuration and state move into SQLite (the brief §9) is met in Part B for the Fabric's records, the actors and the
   roles, the moves and the news, and for the general settings and the rest in that later design, whose protocols 19.8's table and §13.5
   already give; the shell's and a `daoris-driver`'s race on `windows.json`, `reads.json` and `usage.json` (§13.1) waits with z-vi;
   `driver.json` stays the home of every setting but `actors`, and D50 stands for them. **Gain:** no row of Part B rehomes a grant or a rule,
   the GitDoor takes no package (19.10), and Part B's compatibility surface is the service's new tables, the store's own families and w-s's
   one key.

**The actors' staffing stays in Part B** (the steward's change to round 5, before both reads, on the owner's direction that the Fabric, the
actors and the roles are better read, set and managed in the store, the brief §9). Every role's staffing, seeded or added, has one home,
`role_staffing`, filled by a row of its own cut out of z-ii, **w-s ⚖**, landing before w. **Round 8 makes it smaller** *(C7-1.2, C7-3.1,
O7-F1 to O7-F3, O7-F6 to O7-F9)*, the steward's view: the reads found something new in w-s in four rounds running, so a smaller protocol that
still meets the brief's §9, every role's staffing in the store and the file a projection an older build can read, replaces another patch.
**The store holds what `driver.json`'s staffing holds today, the newer build reads only the store, and the file is the store's projection,
the file today's build would write, kept by one lock that lives with its holder and one check that reads the file back.** What it drops is
said at the end of this list.
- **The code.** Each actor's stored state is absent (*as before*, read from today's keys at account default), `[]` (*off*, for Intake and Ask

  1. *The staffing lock.* It takes the home's record lock for the staffing, `RecordLock` at `locks/records/role-staffing.lock`
     (`Platform/RecordLock.cs:37-65`; at `26ef34d80` `Store/RecordLock.cs`, the same lines), and holds it to its end. The handle is the lock: a
