     of its live run, which its own failure after its intent left in place for its process's life, refusing every other writer, *O7-F6*). **A
     writer that finds it held waits `RecordLock.Wait`, ten seconds, and is then refused with nothing written and said** (`:23`, `:42-65`), as
     every record lock's writer is today *(O8-F3)*: round 8's case of a writer waiting past another's minute, both kept, could not pass.
  2. *The change.* It reads the store's staffing and `written`, and applies the change by today's `ActorChoices.With`, its judge, its
     write-through and its `Keep` unchanged, but for *Take it*, whose change is the found row's paths as they stand (below, *C8-1.3*,
     *O8-F4*); a refusal ends it with nothing written.
  3. *The file, against its expected baseline*, under `driver.json`'s `KeepLock` *(C8-1.1)*: the file is read again; where its staffing paths
     show the **expected baseline**, `written` for a press and the four paths the check observed under both locks for the check's own repair,
     the projection is written onto those paths alone, as `JsonKeeping` edits, beside then renamed, the lock read again just before the rename
     and the rename made only while it still holds this writer's text; where they show anything else, or the lock is no longer its own,
     nothing is written: a press is answered not done and said, and the check runs; the check's repair, refused so, runs no check again and
     leaves the comparison to the next check. A file that does not read is never written, as today (`DriverConfig.cs:1030-1052`). Round 8 gave
     the step one baseline, `written`, which the check's repair, made on a file that differs from `written` by its nature, could never meet
     (Codex's 1.1).
  4. *The commit*: one store transaction writes the staffing and sets `written` to the projection; only then is the press answered done.
  5. *The check*, below, once, under the staffing lock it still holds, `KeepLock` taken again (*O7-F7*: round 7 left the file to whatever an
     older writer renamed after it).
  So the file changes before the store, and a narrowing is never in the store and not in the file. A crash before the rename changes nothing,
  the system letting the lock go; a crash after the rename and before the commit leaves the file at a projection the store does not hold, which
  the next check reads as a change found: a turn-off in it applied, so a narrowing press completes, the rest kept for the person, so a widening
  press is never answered done and is pressed again. No store transaction spans the file (§13.4).
- **The check** *(O7-F1, O7-F7, O7-F8)*, round 7's *return*. At each start of the shell and of the headless loop, at each look of the loop that
  holds the home's lease (the look reads `driver.json` again, `Loop/DriverWatch.cs:719`), and at the end of each write, the newer build compares
  the file's four staffing paths with `written` and the store's projection, on the text it already read, and only where they differ takes the
  staffing lock and `KeepLock` and reads them again. **The look's check takes both with no wait** *(O8-F3)*, `RecordLock.Take` and
  `KeepLock.Take` given a wait of zero (`Store/RecordLock.cs:42`, `Store/KeepLock.cs:63` at `26ef34d80`, each taking its wait as an argument),
  and finding either held leaves the comparison to the next look, its tick never failing for it, where round 8 let a hung newer writer hold
  each look up to ten seconds for each lock and throw inside it; a start's check and a write's end's check wait as a writer does and, refused,
  leave the comparison to the next check, a committed press answered done all the same. Then:
  - at `written`, the projection unchanged: nothing;
  - at `written`, the projection moved without a write (a record that stopped reading, row w's): the projection written over them as in step 3,
    its baseline the paths observed, and `written` set *(O7-F8)*;
  - at the store's projection as it stands (a write's own, after its rename and before its commit's `written`): `written` set, nothing found;
  - otherwise another writer changed them (an older build, a hand edit, a program, a writer paused past the lock's minute, a write cut off after
    its rename): one store transaction records **what was found**, a row of `role_staffing_found` holding the four paths as the file showed
    them and `written` as it stood, and applies **every turn-off the file shows**, Intake or Ask Daoris read by today's reader as starting
    nothing (its entry `[]`, no entry and no key, or an entry that does not read) where `written` starts it, made `[]` with no press; then the
    projection of the staffing that leaves is written over the file's staffing paths as in step 3, **its expected baseline the four paths the
    check observed** *(C8-1.1)*, and then `written` set to that projection in a store transaction of its own. A found row equal to the newest
    one not yet taken or dismissed is not recorded twice.
  **The repair's order across a crash** *(C8-1.1)*: a crash after the found row's commit and before the file's replacement leaves the file as
  found, which the next check records no second time, its turn-offs already applied, and repairs; one after the replacement and before
  `written`'s update leaves the file at the store's projection, which the next check reads as the third case above, `written` set and nothing
  found; and a repair whose baseline no longer stands, an older writer having renamed after taking the aged lock, writes nothing and runs no
  second check, the next check finding that writer's file.
  So nothing the file showed is lost, kept whole in its row whatever else the same change did (*O7-F1*: round 7 wrote a turn-off found at the
  return over the change found beside it, so an older build's Build change offered with it then failed its own `observed` check); a narrowing
  found is applied at once and a widening never by itself; and the file is back at the store's projection at the check. A file that does not
  read is never written: the check finds nothing, and the look says it as today.
- **What was found, offered** *(O7-F1, O7-F9)*. The AI screen's *Actors* lists each found row not yet taken or dismissed, saying in words what
  it changed against its `written`, with two presses: **Take it**, a write that makes the store's staffing what the found file showed, path
  by path where it differs from the row's `written`: **each role's entry set to what the file shows, absent included, and each first-agent key
  set to what the file shows, as the store's key alone, whatever its role's entry** *(C8-1.3, O8-F4)*, with no `ActorChoices.With`
  write-through and no `Keep`, since the found text already holds what its writer's `With` and `Keep` did; each entry judged by
  `ActorChoices.Judge` and each key read as today's parser reads it (`Platform/DriverConfig.cs:1613`, `:1633`, `:1635`), a turn-on among them
  by this press; and **Dismiss**. **One rule for both reads.** Round 8 replayed a changed key through its verb only where its role's entry had
  not changed. So Codex's case (1.3), an explicit Build A that an older build moved to B and then cleared, its `With(..., null)` removing the
  entry and keeping `adapter` at B (`ActorChoices.cs:165-178`), took the removal and skipped `adapter`, leaving Build *as before* on A; and the
  Opus reader's (F4), a key-only change replayed through `With`, made Build one explicit choice, replacing an explicit order, and its `Keep`
  pinned Chat and Brainstorm *as before* at the old agent (`:159-205`, `:199`), where the file itself moves every *as before* role and leaves
  an explicit order alone (`:131-144`). Taking each changed path as it stands meets both: Codex's case leaves Build *as before* on B, and
  Intake's and Ask Daoris's the same on their keys; a key-only change sets the store's key alone, moving every role *as before* on it and
  leaving an explicit order as it stands, so a key-only change is taken as the store's key alone, the reader's first way. `daoris driver actor found` lists them, and `found take <n>` and
  `found dismiss <n>` press them. Taking reads the row, never the file, so it needs no baseline to land on, and a found change is taken
  whatever the file shows since (round 7's `observed`, *C6-1.6*, withdrawn with the offers). An older build's `SET_ACTOR` writes `actors`
  whole, Chat and Brainstorm pinned at its old `adapter` by its `Keep` (`:191-205`): taken, the pins come with it, as the older build meant;
  left, the store is unchanged and its Chat and Brainstorm *as before* follow the store's own `adapter` (*O7-F9*: round 7's *as before* roles
  read the file's key live, so they followed the older build's Build while its pins waited as an offer).
- **Its bound against an older writer, both orders, decided** *(C7-1.2, C7-3.1, O7-F7)*. An older writer shares only `KeepLock`, which it
  takes as stale once a minute has passed since its holder took it, however short the pause after the holder's last read (`KeepLock.cs:78`,
  `:120`), and its save renames with no second look at the lock (`DriverConfig.cs:1018`, `:1027`). So:
  - *An older writer renaming late.* Paused after its re-read (`:1018`), its lock taken as stale, it renames (`:1027`) after a newer write has
    committed, a narrowing among them, and the file shows the older staffing, an enabled Intake perhaps. The next check finds it: a turn-on, so
    found and offered and never applied, and the projection written back. **The file is wider than the store from that rename to the next
    check**: the next look of a newer loop holding the lease, else the next newer start or write. Only an older build's start in between acts
    on it; the newer build never runs it. Where the newer write was a turn-on, the stale file shows the work off, and the check applies that
    as a turn-off: the newer build narrower than the person's press, never wider, its found row saying so on the screen.
  - *A newer write whose lock aged*, a staffing write or any newer save of the home's `driver.json`, an unrelated setting's included, since
    each takes the fence *(C8-1.2)*. Its `KeepLock` a minute old before its rename, an older writer takes it as stale and saves. Before the
    newer write's last look at its lock, that look finds the lock not its own: the newer write writes nothing, a staffing write running the
    check and any other save refused, the next check then finding the older save, a turn-off in it applied and the rest offered. **Between
    that last look and its rename**, an older save completed inside that instant is renamed over and lost, as any `driver.json` write is lost
    today under CONFIGLOCK1's bound; were it a turn-off, the work stays on in both builds until the person turns it off again. Round 8 fenced
    the staffing's own writes alone, so an unrelated save paused past its lock's minute lost an older turn-off whole, its rename showing
    `written` again (Codex's 1.2).
  - *A lock already aged* when either comes: either build takes it as stale, and the two cases above are all it leads to.
  **Decided**: these two windows are the downgrade's bound, stated in §11 and named in the proof, never claimed closed; closing them needs a
  fence the older writer reads, and it reads none (`compatible-builds.md`). The newer build's own writes never leave the file wider than the
  store past one check, and nothing an older writer wrote is lost but in the second window.
- **The price, for an older build** (round 6, *O5-F3*; round 8, the projection whole, where rounds 5 to 7 gave an older build the first-agent
  keys alone). A build that reads `actors` (D205's, MODELROLE1c on) reads every seeded role's staffing whole, its order, models and efforts, as
  today's build wrote it; a build from before it reads the three keys: **Build**, **Chat** and **Brainstorm** on `adapter` at account default,
  **Intake** and **Ask Daoris** on their keys, **Helper** on its callers' rules' candidates (`HelperChoice.Order` with no `helper` actor,
  `Conversations/Helpers/HelperChoice.cs:43-49`), **Second opinion** and **Partner** on their rules' own choice (`ActorChoices.cs:93-99`;
  `ReviewerChoice`, `PartnerChoice`). Either reads **no added role and no scope**: Intake and Ask Daoris are off in it wherever the store holds
  them off in any scope, and any entry that does not read holds its starts, as in the newer build. So an older build is never wider in
  **whether** a work the person turned off starts but for the two windows above. **Narrowed in round 7** (*C6-1.7*: round 6 claimed it for every work): an older build reads no added role, skipping an id it
  does not know (`ActorChoices.cs:216-217`), so where an added record does not read, which holds every start in its scope in the newer build
  (§2.7), an older build starts a work that may not be off there (Build, Chat, Brainstorm, Helper, Second opinion, Partner) on the seeded
  staffing, as it would had the role never been added; no person can turn those works off, so no turn-off is undone, and the works that may be
  off are off in it while the record does not read, the projection counting such a record as taking both. A legacy-readable hold for those
  works, an unreadable entry under each seeded id, was weighed and not taken: the newer build reads `actors`' unreadable entries too, so it
  would hold the same works machine-wide in both builds.
- **Its class: projected** (round 8; rounds 5 to 7 set `actors` aside under a receipt and kept the keys written through). `driver.json`'s
  staffing stays in the file as the store's projection, every staffing write written through to it, a widening by the person's own press: it is
  §13.5's one family written through beyond a narrowing, and §13.7's rejection of a file written through beside the store holds for it as the
  row states there, the newer build reading only the store and the check keeping whatever it overrules.
- **D50, amended for the staffing alone** (round 6, *O5-F4*; round 8). A change of the staffing's four paths in the home's own `driver.json`,
  by hand or by any program, takes effect through the person's *Take it* on what the check found, a turn-off in it at once; for a role *as
  before* too, which round 7 read live from the file. Taken, it does what the file did, each changed entry and key as it stands (*C8-1.3*,
  *O8-F4*). A file `DAORIS_DRIVER_CONFIG` names is read and written as today. For every other key D50
  stands.
- **Its own verbs, and the tools** (round 6, *C5-3.7*, *O5-F11*, *O5-F12*). `daoris driver actor export` writes the seeded eight's staffing as
  readable JSON in D205's shape, `actors`, and `daoris driver actor import` reads one back as a write, a turn-on by that press; `found` as
  above; the AI screen's *Actors* shows what was found and its presses, its words in both catalogues under the UI and translation contracts.
  Added roles join the export with row y-i, and nothing here names a verb of row y-i's or a judge of row w's. No tool seeds `actors` (`git grep
  actors -- tools` finds none), and each that seeds a first-agent key names its file by `DAORIS_DRIVER_CONFIG` (the family rehearsal,
  `tools/family-rehearsal.mjs:102-111`, `:981-988`, `:1177-1181`; the INT4f proof through the proof kit, `tools/int4f-proof.mjs:174`,
  `tools/proof-kit.mjs:288-296`), so each reads and writes its file as today, with no store; the census fails a tool that writes a staffing
  path into a home's own `driver.json` with no `DAORIS_DRIVER_CONFIG`.
- **Proof**, each case of w-s's suite, the older build's own `DriverConfig.Save` run in the test where an older writer is named
  (`compatible-builds.md`): every actor's three states round-tripped through the store, the export and the import (`ActorChoicesTests` whole);
  the import killed before its commit and run again, the file untouched; a write killed before its rename (nothing changed), after its rename
  and before its commit (a turn-off completed by the next check, a turn-on found and offered, neither answered done) and after its commit;
  **the check's repair cut between the found row's commit and the file's replacement (the next check recording no second row and repairing),
  between the replacement and `written`'s update (the next check setting `written`, nothing found) and after it; and a repair whose observed
  baseline an older writer's late rename changed, refused with nothing written and no second check, the next check finding that file**
  (*C8-1.1*); two newer writers in two processes at once, the second waiting while the first holds the staffing lock and kept after it, and,
  the first paused holding it past `RecordLock.Wait`, the second refused with nothing written and said (*O8-F3*, where round 8 claimed both
  kept past `KeepLock`'s minute, which `RecordLock`'s ten seconds refuse); **a look whose check finds the staffing lock, and in another run
  `KeepLock`, held by a hung newer writer, its tick completing and the comparison made at the next look** (*O8-F3*); a writer killed holding
  the staffing lock, the next taking it at once; **Codex's schedule: an older save paused after its re-read with Intake
  on, its lock aged, renamed after a newer turn-off committed; an older build's start between that rename and the next check starting an
  intake, named as the bound; the check finding it, the turn-on offered and the file narrowed again, no start after it** (*C7-1.2*); **the
  reverse: a newer write paused with its `KeepLock` aged while an older save turns Intake off, before the newer write's last look at its lock
  (the newer write dropped, the turn-off applied by the check) and between that look and its rename (the older save lost, named as the bound,
  never proven closed)** (*C7-1.2*, *O7-F7*); **the same reverse for a newer save of an unrelated setting (a cap, say): paused after its
  re-read with its `KeepLock` aged while an older save turns Intake off, before its last look at its lock (refused with nothing written, the
  turn-off kept in the file and applied by the next check) and between that look and its rename (the older save lost, named as the bound)**
  (*C8-1.2*); **a lock planted older than a minute under a live pid, each build taking it as stale, and each case above run over it**
  (*C7-1.2*; the unrelated save's among them, *C8-1.2*); **two older `SET_ACTOR`s found at one check, one turning Intake off and one changing
  Build: Intake made off at once, both kept in one found row, Build's change taken by *Take it* with Chat and Brainstorm pinned as the older
  build pinned them, and in another run dismissed, the store's Build unchanged and its Chat and Brainstorm *as before* on the store's
  `adapter`** (*O7-F1*, *O7-F9*); **the downgrade and return for Build, Intake and Ask Daoris each: an explicit entry on agent A, an older
  build's own `SET_ACTOR` moving it to B and then clearing it, its `With(..., null)` leaving the key at B; found, and taken: the role *as before*
  on B, never on A** (*C8-1.3*); **`adapter` hand-edited with Build, Chat and Brainstorm *as before*, found and taken: all three on the new
  agent, none made explicit and none pinned; and, Build given an explicit order of two, the same edit taken: Build's order kept, Chat and
  Brainstorm on the new agent** (*O8-F4*); **a hand edit of `adapter` with Build set: the AI screen, the starts' wiring and `daoris-driver chat` on the store's agent until
  the person takes it** (*O7-F2*); **a set Intake during an update's drain starting no intake; a set Helper's order kept; a save of another
  key leaving the staffing paths as found; the census of those lines and of every writer** (*O7-F2*); **each composition root's load reading
  the store, the three keys through the port and `Actors` and `ActorErrors` from Roles, a file `DAORIS_DRIVER_CONFIG` names read and written as
  today with no store, and `StructureTests` whole, no census entry added, on a tree where mod-iii's, mod-v's and mod-xi's cuts have landed**
  (*O7-F3*, *O8-F2*); **a write whose `KeepLock` wait expires, and one whose commit is refused, each followed by another process's write,
  which proceeds** (*O7-F6*); **the look's time and a load of the home's configuration timed on the install before and after on the same
  home, the row landing only with each p95 within a tenth of before** (*O8-F5*); **with
  the machine's Intake *as before*, a check after a projection that writes `intakeAdapter` absent, the machine's intake still starting on the
  store's key** (*O7-F8*; the scoped case row w's); a hand edit of a key of a role *as before* found and offered, the role reading the store's
  key meanwhile; an unreadable entry holding its starts in both builds, then repaired; Build given a second choice and a model, a build
  that reads `actors` walking them and one from before walking `adapter` alone; `HelperLookTests`, `InstallUpdateTests` and
  `DriverConfigKeepsTests` whole; the screen in both languages. Round 7's case of an added record that does not read is row w's, where added
  roles arrive (*O7-F10*). ⚖, since its import and its *Take it* change who may start (Intake and Ask Daoris on or off).
- **What round 8 drops** (the steward's view: a smaller protocol over another patch). The store's pending, its generation and its process run
  for the staffing, and the come-up's finishing of a pending (a record lock the system lets go with its holder instead); `actors` set aside,
  with its receipt `data/driver.actors.imported` and the come-up's order for it (the file keeps the whole staffing as the projection); an
  offer's `observed` baseline, which had to land on the file, and `role_staffing_offers` (a found change is kept in its row and taken from it);
  a role *as before* reading its key live from the file, with round 7's materialization before a scope's off and its extra `Keep` (the store
  keeps the keys, and today's `With` and `Keep` run on them); round 7's seven readers moved onto `FirstAgent` (none moves). **What the person
  loses**: a hand edit, or a program's write, of the staffing in the home's own `driver.json` no longer takes effect by itself for a role *as
  before* either, where round 7 read it live; a tool that names its file by `DAORIS_DRIVER_CONFIG` is untouched. **What an older build
  gains**: the whole staffing of the eight, where rounds 5 to 7 gave it the first agents alone.
- **To drop the row:** without it the seeded roles' staffing would stay in `actors` and an added role's in the store, two homes for one kind
  of record, which the steward's change refuses.

## 20. Round 6: both reads of round 5 answered

Both makers read round 5 at `9f48860ba` (`docs/2026-10-25-foundation-reads.md`, *Codex, round 5* and *Opus (foundation), round 5*). Round 6 is
written by Claude Opus 5.5 from main at `6736d2db9`, whose `src/` is `86f32f40c`'s unchanged (`git diff 86f32f40c 6736d2db9 -- src` is empty), so
every line both reads cite is HEAD's, and the steward checked each against it (§20.3). Each finding has one row: *taken*, with where it went;
*answered*, with the reason that holds; or *open*, with why. Where the two reads found one thing, the stricter is taken, and where Codex asks a
protocol choice and the Opus reader names a smallest change, that change is taken where it meets Codex's counterexample, each such row saying so.
No finding is left open.

### 21.3 The code, checked

Every line both reads cite was read at `abf03ea26`, where it stands as the reads cite it. The claims hold as the reads state them: a word's id
is the first eight hexadecimal characters of a new GUID (`Core/Sessions/SessionLedger.cs:1383-1384`), and an answer kept before `said` reads as
the word `answer` (`Core/Sessions/Sessions.cs:1545`, `:1566-1572`, `:1651`); the say door answers `Never` and `running` before it reads a key
(`SessionLedger.cs:941-956`); the shell's pump says a held word to its record with its key, breaks on a lost answer and on `running`, and
forwards the word with its key to the conversation the record went on in (`Modules/Loop/SessionWords.cs:773-799`, `:843-863`); a move's
message is recorded as one joined `Asked` event with no id (`Driver/Conversations/Chat/ChatRunner.cs:2588-2604`, `:2641-2647`), the go-on's
opening records each word under its id before its prompt (`:3502-3509`; `Conversations/Domain/ResumeAsk.cs:112-134`), a turn's `Recorded`

lines are HEAD's, and the steward checked each against it (§22.4). Each finding has one row: *taken*, with where it went; *answered*, with the
reason that holds; or *open*, with why. **Where the two reads meet, the stricter is taken**, and the row says so: Codex's 1.1 with the Opus
reader's F12 (a settled answer that is its own terminal kind, never a word, and what an older client does with it), and Codex's 1.2 with its
F7 (both orders, the aged lock, the re-read after a rename, and the bound that remains, decided). **The steward's view on w-s is taken**: the
reads found something new in it in four rounds running while the settlement, `sent` and the keyed open held, so round 8 replaces round 7's
w-s by a smaller protocol that still meets the brief's §9 rather than patching it again, and says what it drops (§22.3, 19.15). No finding is
left open.

## 23. Round 8's named changes, and where each went

Both makers read round 8 at `41a596671` (`docs/2026-10-25-foundation-reads.md`, *Codex, round 8* and *Opus (foundation), round 8*), and
**both verdicts are accept after named changes the steward can check without another read**: Codex, its §0 finding its round-7 findings
taken or answered and none open, its §1 to §3 making 7 findings, 3 blocking for Part B (1.1 to 1.3, all in row w-s ⚖), and no claim about the
code wrong; the independent Opus reader, its §0 finding no finding of either round-7 read wrong, 9 findings, 2 blocking (F1 in row p, F2 in
row w-s ⚖), and five of round 8's claims wrong (its §3, each a finding's). The named changes are taken by Claude Opus 5.5 from main at
`26ef34d80`, which carries Part A's mod-ii beside round 8, and each finding's lines were checked there (§23.3). **Every named change is
taken as its read names it**; where a read offers two ways, the row says which was chosen and why; **Codex's 1.3 and the Opus reader's F4
touch the same *Take it*, and one rule meets both** (C8-1.3's row); and every non-blocking finding is taken too, each being small. Each
changed passage is marked *(C8-n.m)* or *(O8-Fn)* where it stands, and §22's dispositions that change say so in their rows.

### 23.3 The code, checked

Every line both reads cite was read at `26ef34d80`. Between `41a596671`, where both reads were made, and it, main merged Part A's row mod-ii
(`git diff --name-status -M 41a596671 26ef34d80 -- src`): Store's seven files moved from `Driver/Platform/` to `Driver/Store/` (`KeepLock.cs`,
`RecordLock.cs`, `JsonKeeping.cs`, `JsonFile.cs` and `RecordList.cs` unchanged; `AtomicFile.cs` and `Json.cs` changed by its cut), its
cut's `Platform/BesideReads.cs` and `Platform/JsonText.cs` added and `DaorisHome.cs`, `HomeIdentity.cs`, `HomeLease.cs` and `Moments.cs`
reading through them, the census (`Driver.Tests/StructureTests.cs`, `fixtures/structure.json`) and the test files moved with their
subjects. So **the reads' `Driver/Platform/RecordLock.cs` lines** (`:13-15`, `:23`, `:37-65`, `:42-65`) **are `Driver/Store/RecordLock.cs`'s
same lines, and `KeepLock.cs`'s** (`:35`, `:38`, `:78`, `:106-121`, `:120`) **`Driver/Store/KeepLock.cs`'s**; the census's lines the Opus
reader cites at `41a596671`, `structure.json:828-834` and `:1025-1029`, are `:820-826` and `:1017-1021` at `26ef34d80`, listing the same
edges; every other file either read cites is unchanged, so its lines stand as cited. The claims hold as the reads state them, but one line:
- Codex's 1.1 is the design's own text (round 8's 19.15, its *A write*'s step 3 and its check's last branch), as the read states.
- Codex's 1.2: `DriverConfig.Save` takes `KeepLock` (`Driver/Platform/DriverConfig.cs:1013`), computes its whole text over the file read again
  (`:1018`) and renames it (`:1027`) with no second look at the lock, newer and older builds alike.
- Codex's 1.3: `ActorChoices.With` removes the entry for no choices (`Driver/Platform/ActorChoices.cs:165`) and writes a first agent through
  only for choices (`:168-178`), so `Adapter` stays where an older build cleared Build's entry.
- The Opus reader's F1: the pump breaks on an unanswered say with its word held (`Modules/Loop/SessionWords.cs:789-792`) and forwards it with
  its key to the conversation its record went on in (`:773-779`, `PassAsync` at `:843-863`); the briefed move reads the old record's waiting
  words before it opens (`Driver/Conversations/Chat/BriefedMove.cs:99`) and takes only those ids (`:157-158`); the go-on judges a continued
  record `Continued` or `Superseded` (`Conversations/GoOn/SessionSay.cs:40-45`) and the come-up skips it (`Chat/ChatGoOn.cs:228`); the helper
  desk walks to the newest asker (`Core/Sessions/HelperDesk.cs:140-155`) and reads its key there alone (`:180`).
- The Opus reader's F2: the census lists `DriverConfig.cs`'s and `DriverChoicesCommand.cs`'s `ActorChoice` and `ActorChoices` under *the
  settings' parser's and verbs' names of other subdomains' rule types*, after Settings (`structure.json:820-826` at `26ef34d80`), and
  `Setup/InstallUpdate.cs`'s `ActorChoice` under *Install names Roles*, after Install (`:1017-1021`); the drain sets `IntakeAdapter` null and
  Intake's entry `[]` on the record in memory (`Setup/InstallUpdate.cs:50-55`).
- The Opus reader's F3: `RecordLock.Wait` is ten seconds (`Driver/Store/RecordLock.cs:23`), past which `Take` throws (`:42-65`), and
  `KeepLock.Wait` is the same (`Driver/Store/KeepLock.cs:35`); each `Take` takes its wait as an argument (`RecordLock.cs:42`, `KeepLock.cs:63`).
- The Opus reader's F4: a stored entry is read before any key, and a role *as before* reads `adapter`, `intakeAdapter` or `helperAdapter`
  (`ActorChoices.cs:131-144`); `With` writes through and `Keep` pins Chat and Brainstorm *as before* at the old `adapter` (`:159-205`, `:199`).
  Today's parser reads the three keys at `DriverConfig.cs:1613`, `:1633` and `:1635`.
- The Opus reader's F5: each look loads `driver.json` (`Loop/DriverWatch.cs:719`), and each conversation's `MachineConfig` loads it
  (`Chat/ChatContext.cs:126`).
- The Opus reader's F7, **its line corrected**: a driven part runs on the protocol door (`Loop/Driver.cs:3730`, `:3810`); its run's first
  prompt goes through `PromptingAsync` and `SendPromptAsync` (`Driver/Agents/Acp/Acp.cs:857-862`, `:2040-2081`), whose write return,
  `OnWire` (`:2066`, `:2109-2113`), tells the run's `sent` and with it `prompted`, *told once the first prompt is on the wire*
